- **BLOCKER c1 (single shared ratchet chain → identical `MK_n` both parties → nonce reuse + delete-wedge):** v2 uses **two directional chains** (`a2b`, `b2a`), a proper double ratchet. Each party sends on its own chain, receives on the other's — no shared `MK`, no wedge.
- **BLOCKER c2 (ephemeral `eph_ek` unauthenticated → MITM defeats post-compromise heal):** `eph_ek` is now **inside `msg_sig`** (signed under `S_pc`).
- **MAJOR c3 (harvest-now recovers the whole pre-KEM-ratchet chain):** the **first KEM ratchet happens at the handshake** (A's first-contact carries `eph_ek_A`; B's ACCEPT encapsulates to it), not on a later content reply — so a one-directional burst still ratchets once immediately. Harvest-`ct0` + later `DK_lt` compromise recovers **only the first-contact message**, then the chain forks off fresh ephemeral entropy. Stated honestly (not "FS holds").
- **MAJOR c4 / metadata-1 (epoch in AAD pins a message to its compose-epoch address → concurrent-live-epoch addresses co-linkable; forward-unlinkability false):** **epoch is addressing-only.** Dropped from the message AAD and from `msg_sig`. Anti-replay = `chan_id ‖ seq` (monotonic) + content-address dedup. A pending message **migrates** to the current epoch's address by byte-identical re-seed; no concurrent-epoch window. Forward-unlinkability claim **withdrawn** — folds into the accepted F1 cap (a pending message's ciphertext is linkable across the addresses it visits while undelivered, proportional to undelivered duration).
- **MAJOR c5 (reverse direction unspecified + `AR` circular dependency):** `AR = HKDF("…/addr/root/v1", ss0)` from **`ss0` alone** (drop `ar_seed_A/B`); both parties derive it. B's **ACCEPT** frame (first reply on the channel) carries `PK_pc_B` + `bind_lt_B` + `bind_pop_B`, symmetric to A's first-contact, so A authenticates B.
- **MAJOR c6 (ack seal unspecified):** ack seal fully named below (key/nonce/AAD).
- **MAJOR e1 (single-scalar `high_water` false-confirms gaps under lossy out-of-order delivery):** `high_water` is a **contiguous-prefix** value — highest `S` with **all** of `1..S` collected — never max-seen. A gap holds the water at the gap; the sender re-seeds the tail (bounded head-of-line cost, accepted). Optional gap-bitmap for efficiency.
- **MAJOR e2 (bilateral write-amplification via ack read-suppression, bounded 7d):** documented bounded-DoS residual; the sender's geometric backoff thins the re-seed, so it is not flat-rate. Accepted.
- **MAJOR e3 ("self-terminating convergence" false under lossy reads):** claim downgraded — the handshake terminates by mutual observation in the common case and by the **7-day give-up** under loss (honest-undelivered, never false-confirmed). **⚠️ AMENDED by the ack taper (§ D-DELIV) — this bullet still names mutual observation as the common-case terminator, and it is no longer a terminator at all: the ack side stops on the taper's elapsed-time schedule in every case, lossy or not. The 7-day give-up remains the *sender's* bound. Read this bullet as the record of why the downgrade was needed, not as the live stopping condition.**
- **e6/D6 (generous hosting availability ≈ 0 at alpha — ephemeral node IDs rarely in a channel's replica set):** downgraded — correctness rests on the **ack + owner re-seed, not the swarm**; generous hosting is a costs-nothing scale-time lift, not an alpha availability mechanism.
- **e7/D7 (E9 permanent channel loss):** **closed** by retained-`AR` wall-clock addressing — a long-offline party re-derives the current address from `AR` + the clock. Residual is missed *backlog* (accepted best-effort/PFS trade), not channel loss.
- **MINORs:** `intended_recipient` closes *authentication/misdirection* but a substituted-EK holder still **reads the first-contact plaintext before rejecting** — honest residual of the no-DK-PoP call (accepted). `bind_lt/bind_pop` verification requires `msg_sig` to be mandatory-and-checked (stated in verifier rules) and now also binds the recipient. Nonce pinned to **random 96-bit** (never a counter — kills the constant-nonce trap). First-contact in-degree count and the `dflt(64)`-vs-`dflt(256)` 4×-smaller-cold-surface trade are documented residuals.

### v2 construction (deltas from v1; unchanged parts stand)

- **Identities, key record, token:** unchanged from v1 (all held under R3).
- **First-contact entry:** adds `eph_ek_A` (A's first ratchet ephemeral EK) **inside** the sealed body and **inside `msg_sig`**. `msg_sig = ML-DSA_{S_pc_A}("daemonseed/dm/msg/sig/v2" ‖ chan_id ‖ LE64(seq) ‖ eph_ek_A ‖ recipient=PK_lt_B ‖ sent_ms ‖ body)` — binds channel, seq, ephemeral, recipient; **no epoch** (addressing-only).
- **ACCEPT frame (B's first reply, new):** `{ PK_pc_B, bind_lt_B, bind_pop_B, eph_ct_B = encaps(eph_ek_A)→(ct,ss1), eph_ek_B, seq, sealed body, msg_sig_B }`. `ss1` advances the root (first KEM ratchet). Symmetric bindings authenticate B.
- **Roots:** `AR = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/addr/root/v2", ss0)` (retained, addressing only). `RK0 = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/ratchet/root/v2", ss0)`; the handshake KEM step derives `RK1 = HKDF(RK0, ss1)`. Two send chains per root: `CK_{a2b} = HKDF(RK, "daemonseed/dm/chain/a2b/v2")`, `CK_{b2a} = HKDF(RK, "daemonseed/dm/chain/b2a/v2")`; message key `MK_{dir,n} = HKDF(CK_{dir,n}, "daemonseed/dm/mk/v2")`, `CK_{dir,n+1} = HKDF(CK_{dir,n}, "daemonseed/dm/ck/v2")`; **delete on use** (per-direction, so no cross-party wedge). KEM ratchet on each direction-change advances `RK` from a fresh `eph` (signed).
- **Addressing:** `chan_addr(epoch) = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/addr/v2", AR ‖ LE64(epoch))`, `epoch = floor(now/period)` (wall-clock). `chan_id = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/chanid/v2", AR)` (never on the wire). A message is written to `chan_addr(current_epoch)`; a pending message is **re-seeded to the then-current epoch's address** (migrates), byte-identically.
- **Channel record subkey layout (was unspecified — D8):** one shared-owner `dflt(64)` record. **A→B ring** = subkeys 0..30 (`slot = seq % 31`), **B→A ring** = 31..61, **A ack** = 62, **B ack** = 63. Ring depth 31 ⇒ ≤31 concurrent *unacked* messages per direction (beyond that the sender flow-controls or a larger schema is used — noted). Both parties can write any slot (owner), but each writes only its own region by convention; a peer overwriting the other's region is detectable (signature under the wrong `S_pc`) and is the same class as any owner-secret holder, i.e. the two parties, not a third party.
- **Message seal:** under `MK_{dir,n}`, **explicit random 96-bit nonce** in the envelope, AAD `"daemonseed/dm/msg/aad/v2" ‖ chan_id` (no epoch). Re-seed re-emits the byte-identical frame.
- **Ack:** `AckMarker { high_water: u64 (contiguous prefix), sig = ML-DSA_{S_pc}("daemonseed/dm/ack/sig/v2" ‖ chan_id ‖ LE64(high_water)) }`, **sealed** under `K_ack = HKDF(RK_current, "daemonseed/dm/ack/seal/v2")`, explicit nonce, AAD `"daemonseed/dm/ack/aad/v2" ‖ chan_id`. Written to the party's ack subkey. Sender confirms `seq` only on verified `high_water ≥ seq`. Epoch advance is never an ack. **Piggyback:** the high-water rides on any outbound message; a standalone ack write fires only when confirming without replying, on a **jittered** cadence (reduces the recipient-liveness beacon of finding metadata-2).

### v2 residuals (all accepted for alpha, or newly documented)

- Lobby-rhythm unlinkability (now explicitly includes: pending-message cross-address linkage, and the ack/piggyback recipient-tempo signal — all the same activity-correlation class). Key-record rollback. Recovery. First-contact best-effort-under-attack + in-degree count + `dflt(64)` cold-surface. Harvest-now recovers only the first-contact message (c3). `intended_recipient` post-decap read (c-minor). Bilateral ack-suppression amplification bounded 7d (e2). Budget (#4) still to run.

**Status: DRAFT v2 — not frozen.** Next: round-4 review against v2 → if clean, decision #4 (WB budget arithmetic) → freeze ratification.

## Concrete crypto construction DRAFT v3 (supersedes v2)

> Round-4 confirmed v2 fixed R3's crypto BLOCKERs cleanly (two-directional chains, `eph_ek` auth) and that the `ss`-gated ongoing-channel authority is solid — but v2's **delivery machinery** (the 31-slot ring + epoch migration + contiguous-prefix ack + ACCEPT handshake) composed into a fresh BLOCKER cluster. v3 is an **architectural simplification** the findings drove: **drop epoch address rotation, drop the ring, use per-message per-direction records rooted in the secret `AR`, and add the standard async-messaging components.** The core crypto (ratchet direction, owner-write authority, token, key record) carries over.

### Why drop epochs (the key R4 lesson)

Epoch address rotation was added for metadata forward-unlinkability. The metadata review showed across R3+R4 that **it never delivered that** — migration/retention keep vacated addresses linkable, and it folds into the accepted Lobby-rhythm cap regardless. Meanwhile epochs *caused* the migration write-multiplication (erasure F3), the wide-window-vs-c4 boundary-skew blackout (erasure F4), the ack epoch-migration gap (crypto F-4), and much of the complexity. **Net: epochs bought nothing over the already-accepted F1 residual and cost a BLOCKER's worth of machinery. Removed.**

### R4 changelog (finding → v3 response)

- **Erasure F1/F2 (BLOCKER — 31-ring + contiguous-prefix → overflow loss / whole-channel false-undelivered; escape hatches self-refuting):** **no ring.** Each message is its own record at `msg_addr(dir,seq) = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/msg/addr/v3", AR ‖ dir ‖ LE64(seq))`. No wrap, no overwrite, no 31-cap; retention is per-message (each survives while re-seeded). Out-of-order is natural (fetch by seq).
- **Crypto F-1 / erasure F6 (BLOCKER/MAJOR — no skipped-key store):** the receive chain keeps a **bounded skipped-message-key cache** (`MAX_SKIP = 64` per direction): receiving seq `n` derives and **stores** the intervening `MK`s (does not delete) until their messages arrive or the cache evicts oldest. Standard double-ratchet handling; safe now that per-message records never overwrite a pending ciphertext.
- **Crypto F-2 (MAJOR — harvest-now recovers the whole pre-ACCEPT burst):** B publishes a **pool of one-time ML-KEM prekeys** (a signed, keep-alive'd sibling of the key record); A encapsulates `ss0` to a fresh **one-time** prekey (not the static `EK_lt`), B deletes the matching one-time DK after use → forward secrecy for the opening burst. **Pool exhaustion falls back to the static `EK_lt`** (documented no-FS-for-that-message residual). This is PQXDH's mechanism; it adds B-side pool maintenance/replenishment (accepted cost of the committed PFS work).
- **Crypto F-3 (MAJOR — `K_ack=HKDF(RK_current)` desyncs under ratchet skew):** ack key roots in the **stable retained `AR`**: `K_ack(dir) = HKDF(AR, "daemonseed/dm/ack/seal/v3" ‖ dir)` — always derivable by both parties, never desyncs, still secret to third parties (no `AR` → no key). Fixes F-6 too (per-direction label).
- **Erasure F3/F4 + crypto F-4 (epoch migration cost / boundary skew / ack migration):** **gone with epochs.** `msg_addr`/`ack_addr` are stable per `(AR,dir,seq)` — no migration, no boundary blackout, no ack stranding.
- **Erasure F5 (routine tail re-seed amplification):** reduced — a transient gap holds `high_water`, but the missing message is its own record re-seeded on backoff; when its re-seed lands, its **stored skipped key** decrypts it and the prefix advances. No ring-overwrite means a gap is always eventually fillable, not a permanent hole. Residual bounded re-seed cost folds into budget (#4).
- **Erasure F7 (BLOCKER-adjacent — ACCEPT-loss half-open + non-idempotent retry):** the pre-ACCEPT rendezvous is **deterministic and idempotent**: `fc_addr = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/fc/addr/v3", PK_lt_A ‖ PK_lt_B)` (initiator-ordered), so an A retry lands on the **same** address — B dedups, no duplicate contact. **Handshake state machine (named):** A `sent → (on ACCEPT) established`; B `accepted → provisional → (on A's first message at `msg_addr(a2b,·)`) established`. B's establishment is provisional until it sees A on the `AR` channel (mutual confirmation), so a lost ACCEPT no longer false-establishes B. No progress in 7 days → both abandon (teardown).
- **Erasure F8 (read-but-quiet false-undelivered):** **ACCEPT auto-fires the instant B accepts the contact request**, independent of composing a reply (stated as a rule) — so A learns `PK_pc_B` and can verify acks even when B reads-but-doesn't-type.
- **Metadata F-d (MAJOR — single shared record concentrates conversation dynamics):** **resolved by per-message per-direction records** — A's messages at `HKDF(AR‖a2b‖seq)`, B's at `HKDF(AR‖b2a‖seq)`, acks at `HKDF(AR‖ack‖dir)`. These hash to *different DHT nodes per seq*, so no single co-host sees the whole conversation shape (a reconstruction needs hosting many `HKDF(AR‖·)` keys, and without `AR` they look unrelated). Strictly better than v2's one shared record.
- **Metadata F-a/F-c (MINOR — c4 "no concurrent window" overclaim; stale line 218):** moot (epochs/migration gone); the stale forward-unlinkability sentence is struck below.
- **Crypto F-5 (MINOR — `eph_ct_B` only owner-write authenticated):** B's first outbound message `msg_sig` binds the `eph_ct_B` it sent, closing the consistency gap end-to-end.

### v3 construction summary (deltas; unchanged parts stand)

- **Identities, token, key record:** unchanged, **plus** B's key record gains a signed **one-time-prekey pool** sibling record (pool address `HKDF("daemonseed/dm/prekey/addr/v3", PK_lt_B)`, entries `{ eph_ek_i, sig }`, kept-alive, replenished; consumed-index tracked at-rest by B).
- **First contact:** self-contained entry at the deterministic `fc_addr`; `ss0 = encaps(one_time_prekey_i)` (fallback static `EK_lt_B`); carries `eph_ek_A` in `msg_sig`; PoW + optional token as before; **idempotent retry**.
- **Establishment:** B's ACCEPT (auto-fired) at `fc_addr` carries `PK_pc_B` + bindings + `eph_ct_B` + `eph_ek_B`; both derive `ss0 → AR = HKDF("…/addr/root/v3", ss0)` and `RK0`, then the immediate handshake KEM step → `RK1`.
- **Ongoing:** per-message records `msg_addr(dir,seq)`; two directional ratchet chains with a **bounded skipped-key cache**; message sealed under `MK_{dir,n}`, random 96-bit nonce, AAD `"…/msg/aad/v3" ‖ chan_id ‖ dir`; `msg_sig` binds `chan_id ‖ dir ‖ seq ‖ eph_ek ‖ recipient ‖ body` (no epoch). **No migration** (addresses stable).
- **Ack:** per-party record `ack_addr(dir)`; contiguous-prefix `high_water`; sealed under `K_ack(dir)=HKDF(AR,…)`; signed under `S_pc`; piggybacks on outbound messages, standalone acks jittered. Sender confirms `seq` on verified `high_water ≥ seq`.
- **Delivery posture unchanged:** fail-safe (default not-delivered; three honest states on-DHT / peer-reachable / confirmed-collected); best-effort; 7-day give-up; generous-hosting as a scale-time lift only.

### v3 residuals (accepted for alpha, or newly documented)

Lobby-rhythm unlinkability (activity-timed writes correlate to lobby windows — the secret-rooted scattered addresses are otherwise third-party-opaque). Key-record rollback. Recovery. First-contact best-effort-under-attack (world-derivable `fc_addr`) + in-degree + the `intended_recipient` post-decap read (no DK-PoP). Prekey-pool exhaustion → static-EK fallback loses FS for those messages. Skipped-key cache bound `MAX_SKIP` — a burst of >64 out-of-order gaps evicts oldest skipped keys (bounded loss, re-seed-recoverable if the message arrives before eviction). Budget (#4, now simpler — no migration multiplication — still to run).

**Status: DRAFT v3 — not frozen. Architectural simplification vs v2 (epochs + ring removed).** Next: round-5 review against v3 → if clean, decision #4 (WB budget arithmetic) → freeze ratification.

## Concrete crypto construction DRAFT v4 (supersedes v3)

> **Round 5 was the convergence point: the crypto review found NO axis-refuting BLOCKER ("v3 is the first draft with no axis-refuting BLOCKER"), the metadata review confirmed the ongoing-channel scatter holds, and the one erasure BLOCKER (per-message records) has a mechanical fix (paging).** The core — `ss`/`AR`-gated owner-write authority, the two-directional ratchet, cross-carrier replay resistance, the nonce rule, fail-safe delivery — held on all three axes. v4 folds the localized R5 cluster; no core change.

### R5 changelog (finding → v4 response)

- **Erasure BLOCKER (per-message records = open-lane + collection-lane + network-retention budget blowup):** **paging.** Messages ride in pages of `K` per record: `page_addr(dir,p) = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/page/addr/v4", AR ‖ dir ‖ LE64(p))`, message `seq` at page `p = seq / K`, subkey `seq % K` of a `dflt(K)` record. Amortizes `open_or_create` `K:1`, restores a **watchable** record (watch the current page for new-subkey `ValueChange` → new-message notification), keeps v3's no-wrap/no-overwrite (a `(dir,seq)` maps to a fixed page+slot, filled once), keeps unbounded backlog (add pages), keeps the AR-secret scatter (per page). `K` is a tuning knob (each message ~6–8 KB must fit the `dflt(K)` subkey cap `min(32768, 1MiB/K)`; `K ≤ 64` is safe — pick `K=16` → 64 KB cap, 16:1 open amortization). Collection sweeps the current page's `K` subkeys (cheap per-GET, like v2) and opens the next page when full. **This resolves the ring-vs-per-message fork engineering-side — no scope call needed.**
- **Metadata Q2 / crypto V6 (world-derivable `fc_addr` = pairwise directed contact-graph oracle):** **revert to a recipient-keyed, sender-blind doorbell.** Doorbell at `HKDF("daemonseed/dm/doorbell/addr/v4", PK_lt_B)` (recipient-keyed — B sweeps its own, closing the inbound-discovery gap); A's slot = `HKDF(sender_secret_A, PK_lt_B) % N` (sender-secret → **not observer-computable**, so no pairwise oracle and no who-knocked leak — restores the M9-defeat); the sender identity is **sealed inside** the entry. World-derivable *recipient* address ⇒ world-writable ⇒ first-contact best-effort-under-attack (accepted).
- **Crypto V7 (epoch removal dropped the first-contact cross-time replay defense):** B publishes a **slow-rotating first-contact nonce** `fc_nonce_B` in its key record; a first-contact entry binds it (in `msg_sig`); B rejects stale-nonce entries. **B rotates `fc_nonce_B` on reinstall/restart**, so a replayed pre-reinstall entry carries the old nonce → rejected even with an empty seen-set (fixes the recovery-gated replay without persisting a seen-set; legitimate pre-reinstall pending contacts must retry, acceptable).
- **Channel idempotency (erasure F7, kept):** **A persists `ss0`** (the channel material) in its outbox and re-emits the first-contact entry byte-identically on retry (re-sealed to B's current `fc_nonce_B`); same sender-secret slot ⇒ idempotent doorbell placement, same `ss0` ⇒ same `AR` ⇒ same ongoing channel. (The deterministic-`fc_addr` of v3 never actually solved channel idempotency — `ss0` persistence does.)
- **Crypto V8 (no double-ratchet PN/generation → RK0→RK1 re-root desync on pre-ACCEPT bursts → silent undecryptability):** the signed frame gains a **`(ratchet_gen, PN)` header** (`ratchet_gen` = which RK the message's chain roots at; `PN` = previous-chain length), bound in `msg_sig`. Standard double-ratchet header; lets B place cross-ratchet-step messages. Closes the archetypal offline-burst desync.
- **Prekey-pool defects (index collision → silent loss; unpinned key origin → FS-theatre-if-mnemonic BLOCKER-of-FS; world-writable → selective-wipe FS downgrade; inbound-rate oracle):** **one-time prekeys DROPPED for alpha (the maintainer's PFS-scope sub-call — DEFAULT drop, flagged for override).** `ss0 = encaps(static EK_lt_B)`. Consequence: **the opening burst (messages before B's first reply) has no forward secrecy — harvest-`ct0` + later `DK_lt` compromise recovers them** (documented residual). FS resumes after B's first ACCEPT (the `ss1` KEM ratchet → `RK1` forks fresh entropy). Dropping prekeys removes all four wholesale. **`‹MAINTAINER›` override:** re-add PQXDH one-time prekeys (random-derived, anti-collision random-over-large-pool selection, pool-wipe = accepted DoS) if opening-burst FS is required for alpha.
- **Crypto/erasure 4.1 (provisional-state persistence):** B MUST persist the provisional handshake state `{ss0, AR, RK, PK_pc_A}` across a normal restart (the ACCEPT-loss + one-time-DK-deletion fragility is moot now prekeys are dropped, but the provisional persistence requirement stands so a restart mid-handshake doesn't strand the channel).
- **Minors:** **chan_id-never-serialized invariant restated** (AAD carries `chan_id`; the receiver reconstructs it from the record it computed — NEVER serialize it, or the scatter collapses — 5b). **Per-emission jitter** on the re-seed backoff (M7 / metadata 1b — kills the shared-phase burst re-clustering). **F5/F6 wording reconciled** (a MAX_SKIP-evicted gap IS a permanent hole; F5's "always fillable" was wrong — the skipped-key cache bound is the real limit, honestly stated). **Ack-channel FS asymmetry documented** (AR retained ⇒ acks are not forward-secret; but ack payload is only a high-water counter, F1-class metadata — one honest sentence). **One-shot establish** (B establishes on collecting A's first message, not on a later AR-channel write — fixes V9 stuck-provisional). **Glare tie-break** (simultaneous mutual first-contact: the pair `(min(PK), max(PK))` picks the single canonical channel — fixes V10 split-brain).

### v4 residuals (accepted for alpha, or newly documented)

Lobby-rhythm. Key-record rollback. Recovery. First-contact best-effort-under-attack + doorbell in-degree (now sender-blind again — no pairwise oracle). `intended_recipient` post-decap read. **Opening-burst no-FS (prekeys dropped — `‹MAINTAINER›` override to add them).** Skipped-key `MAX_SKIP` overflow = bounded permanent loss on >64 simultaneous gaps (honestly stated). Ack-channel not forward-secret (high-water metadata only). Budget (#4) — **now tractable with paging** (opens amortized `K:1`); still to run the arithmetic.

**Status: DRAFT v4 — not frozen. First draft with no core refutation (R5); folds the localized R5 cluster.** Next: round-6 review against v4 → if clean, decision #4 (WB budget arithmetic, now paging-amortized) → freeze ratification + the one `‹MAINTAINER›` prekey sub-call.

## Concrete crypto construction DRAFT v5 (supersedes v4)

> **Round 6 returned NO axis-refuting BLOCKER on any of the three axes** (crypto: "every regression check passed"; metadata: pairwise oracle closed + scatter holds; erasure: **paging genuinely fixes the per-message budget BLOCKER**, within-page independence + injective mapping confirmed). The design has converged. v5 folds R6's clean-fix findings; no core change. Remaining before freeze: decision #4 (budget arithmetic, run below) and the maintainer's sign-off + the one prekey sub-call.

### R6 changelog (finding → v5 response)

- **Crypto V4-1 (MAJOR — a lost generation-boundary `eph_ct` makes the WHOLE downstream conversation undecryptable, not bounded):** **every message of generation G carries `eph_ct_G` redundantly** (the way Signal repeats the DH pubkey in each header), so any gen-G message reconstructs `RK_G = HKDF(RK_{G-1}, decaps(eph_ct_G))`. Losing the specific boundary message no longer loses the generation. The MAX_SKIP residual is re-scoped: bounded per-message loss only (never unbounded-downstream).
- **Crypto V4-2 (MAJOR — A can't complete the first ratchet after restart):** A's persisted first-contact state is **`{ss0, A's opening ephemeral DK}`** (was `ss0` only) until ACCEPT is processed; without the ephemeral DK A cannot `decaps(eph_ct_B)` → `ss1` and would lose the post-ACCEPT FS.

  - **2026-09-05 — A's persisted first-contact state also carries A's own per-correspondent signing keypair `{S_pc, PK_pc}` (ruled 2026-09-05).** V4-2's pair is what A needs to complete the ratchet; it is not what A needs to *speak*. `S_pc` is drawn when the entry is composed and descends from nothing — not from `ss0`, not from the mnemonic — and the entry publishes the verifying half that B checks every later frame against. So A restarting between the entry and the acceptance comes back holding a conversation it can collect on and cannot sign for, and minting a fresh pair does not help, because B verifies against the published one. Both halves are stored: ML-DSA-87 offers no way to recover a public key from a private one. Build note: `dm::provisional::ProvisionalRecord`.
- **Metadata-MAJOR + crypto V4-4 (fc_nonce restart oracle; "slow-rotating" vs "rotate on restart" contradiction; offline-first-contact degradation) — ONE fix:** replace the B-published restart-rotated `fc_nonce_B` with a **wall-clock `fc_epoch = floor(now / FC_PERIOD)`** (`FC_PERIOD` slow, e.g. 1 week). Both parties derive it from the clock — **no B-side state, no restart coupling, no restart oracle**, and the key record's "no online/offline rhythm" property is restored. First-contact entries bind `fc_epoch`; B accepts the **current and previous** epoch (wide window for offline tolerance), rejecting older as stale replay. No persisted seen-set needed.
- **Erasure-MAJOR (page-advance under a permanent gap → whole-suffix stall):** collection is specified as **speculative contiguous probing** — because `seq` is contiguous, pages fill contiguously, so B probes page `p+1` on a cadence regardless of page `p`'s fill state (and always watches the current + next page), never gated on "page full." A permanently-lost message no longer stalls the suffix.
- **Erasure caveat (steady-state receive cursor):** B MUST persist its **receive high-water / page cursor** across a normal restart (distinct from the provisional-handshake persistence 4.1); without it a restart forces a from-0 scan over eventually-evicted leading pages. Stated as a required invariant.
- **Crypto V4-3 (MINOR — nonce reuse on the fc-rotation re-seal):** the "byte-identical re-emit" rule applies **only when the bytes are unchanged**; any re-seal whose plaintext changes (e.g. binding a new `fc_epoch`) draws a **fresh random 96-bit nonce**. The two clauses are reconciled: re-seed = byte-identical (same nonce); re-seal = fresh nonce.
- **Crypto V4-5 (MINOR — `sender_secret_A` origin):** pinned to the **mnemonic-derived expansion** (`daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot/v5`), so it is recoverable, multi-device-consistent, and the doorbell slot is idempotent across A's reinstall/devices. (Channel idempotency still rests on persisted `ss0`; M11 reachability residual intact.)
- **Crypto V4-6 / erasure-MINOR (paging arithmetic):** corrected — the DFLT subkey cap is `min(32768, 1MiB/K)`, so **`K=16` → 32 KiB/subkey** (not 64 KB); a ~6–8 KB message fits with headroom. Doorbell slot count **`N = 256`** (from v2). The write guard must be **schema-derived** (fixes the latent `APP_MESSAGE_CAP=32768` over-permit noted at ISC-1/Decisions — the true `dflt(64)` cap is 16384).
- **Crypto V4-7 (MINOR — glare):** on the `(min(PK),max(PK))` tie-break, the non-canonical channel's already-sent messages are **re-sent (migrated) to the canonical channel**; the abandoned channel is left to evict.
- **Metadata-MINOR (jitter; padding; doorbell in-degree):** re-seed backoff jitter is drawn **independently per record per emission** (WB-1.2 pattern — kills M7 cross-record phase-lock). Messages are **padded to a bucketed constant** before sealing (carry M8 to v5 pages — closes the per-message size leak to a page co-host). The doorbell slot is documented as a **persistent anonymized pseudonym** (a co-host counts distinct concurrent senders + cadence, sender-blind, no identity, no ongoing-contact set — folds into the accepted in-degree residual; the earlier "dropping prekeys removes the rate oracle wholesale" is corrected to "relocates the inbound-rate signal to the doorbell, which is the accepted in-degree residual").

### v5 residuals (accepted for alpha, or newly documented)

Lobby-rhythm. Key-record rollback (+ compose: a cold sender served a rolled-back key record binds a stale `fc_epoch`/policy → first-contact retry; bounded). Recovery/reachability. Opening-burst no-FS (prekeys dropped; `‹MAINTAINER›` override). First-contact best-effort-under-attack + doorbell anonymized in-degree. `intended_recipient` post-decap read. MAX_SKIP bounded per-message loss (re-scoped — no longer unbounded-downstream). Ack-channel not forward-secret. **NEW honest residuals:** (a) **universal address-plane harvest-now** — with prekeys dropped, every channel's `ss0` derives from the static `EK_lt_B`, so a future `DK_lt_B` compromise recovers `ss0 → AR →` the channel's entire address graph (past+future); this is metadata-class (content stays FS via the deleted ratchet chains), consistent with D-PFS "address-linkability is not forward-secret," now stated universally. (b) **Record ceiling** — ~2 records/correspondent against `remote_max_records=64` walls around ~25 active correspondents (see decision #4); documented alpha limit, LRU-managed.

**Status: DRAFT v5 — no core refutation across R5+R6; folds all R6 clean-fixes.** Next: decision #4 (below), then a confirming review round, then the freeze + prekey sub-call.

## Decision #4 — WB budget arithmetic (against v5 + the WB-2/WB-5.1 model)

Against the frozen `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md`: WB-2 steady-state ceiling **≤ 4 non-chat DHT writes/min**; chat/user-action writes excluded (2-permit chat pool); WB-5.1 four-pool 16-permit gate (chat 2 / floor 1 / write W_max=2 / read 9). DM writers classified and priced:

**Chat-class (excluded from the 4/min ceiling — 2-permit chat pool):**
- **DM message first-send** (the outbox write + the first-contact doorbell write) — one user action, exactly what the chat lane is for. Not counted. ✓

**Non-chat (against the 4/min ceiling — Keepalive rank 4, coalescible current-state):**
- **Message re-seeds** (geometric backoff until acked): per pending message ~20 writes/week ≈ **0.002/min** steady (the backoff tail). A *fresh burst* of N messages to offline correspondents re-seeds at the dense head (1–2 min intervals initially) → a transient ~N/2 per min for the first minutes, decaying under the backoff; this **queues and drains through the non-chat funnel** (it is re-seed, not first-send — the message already delivered via the chat lane), so the transient is slower eviction-protection, not a breach or loss. Steady tail across ~30 offline-pending messages ≈ **0.06/min.**
- **Standalone acks** (jittered, only when collecting-without-replying; high-water piggybacks on any outbound reply): coalescible current-state, **≤1 per channel per drain**. This is the dominant DM non-chat writer in read-heavy usage.
- **Doorbell keep-alive** (pending first-contacts): per pending knock, backoff-thinned. Typically 0–1 pending. **~0.05/min.**
- **Key-record keep-alive**: fixed slow schedule, **~0.02/min.** Prekey pool: **dropped → 0.**

**Arithmetic (active user, 3–5 channels):** base WB-2 non-chat = presence 1.2 + share-adverts 0.8 = **2.0/min**. DM adds: re-seed tail 0.06 + doorbell 0.05 + key-record 0.02 + **standalone acks**. Acks are the swing term: uncapped, read-heavy use across 5 channels could push acks to ~3–5/min → **total ~5–7/min, OVER the 4/min ceiling.** With the **required mitigation — a per-channel standalone-ack minimum interval (30 s) + piggyback-on-reply** — acks cap at ≤2/min across active channels → **DM non-chat ≈ 2.1/min, total ≈ 4.1/min: at the ceiling.**

**Verdict — #4 CLOSES conditionally, with two required build constraints (not new design, tuning within the frozen WB):**
1. **Standalone acks carry a per-channel minimum interval (~30 s) and always piggyback on outbound replies** — without this, ack rate breaches WB-2 in read-heavy multi-channel use. (Registered as a WB-classified constraint; it is *use* of the WB scheduler, not an amendment.)
2. **The record ceiling (~2 records/correspondent vs `remote_max_records=64` → ~25 active correspondents) is a documented alpha limit**, LRU-managed (idle channels' handles evict and re-open on next use). Not a per-minute-rate issue; a concurrency-of-open-channels cap. Raising it needs record-economy work (post-alpha).
3. **The write guard must be schema-derived** (the latent `APP_MESSAGE_CAP=32768` over-permits the true `dflt(64)`=16384 and the paged `dflt(16)`=32768 caps) — a correctness fix, filed alongside.

**Open-lane (cold start):** paging amortizes opens K:1; collecting C correspondents × ⌈N/K⌉ pages ≈ C serial opens at ~6–10 s (C=20 → ~2–3 min), within the accepted "cold start is minutes" regime. No breach.

**Net:** the budget is **not a BLOCKER** for v5; it closes within the frozen WB-2/WB-5.1 with the ack-min-interval constraint + the documented ~25-channel record ceiling. #4 is discharged as "closes conditionally," the conditions folded into the build contract.

## Concrete crypto construction DRAFT v6 (supersedes v5)

> The R7 confirming pass returned: **metadata HOLDS** (accepted residuals only, 3 doc tidies); **crypto HOLDS on all four R6 fixes but surfaced one surviving BLOCKER** (first-contact entry sizing — the unresolved half of B1); **erasure found decision #4 does not close as I derived it** (+ 2 spec gaps, 1 relaxed residual). No new architectural refutation — the ss/AR core, paging, and the ratchet all held. v6 folds every R7 finding; then a focused round-8 confirming pass.

### R7 changelog (finding → v6 response)

- **Crypto BLOCKER (first-contact entry ~18–25 KB cannot fit the `dflt(256)` doorbell's 4096 B subkey cap — the unresolved sizing half of B1):** **the doorbell becomes `dflt(32)`** (subkey cap `min(32768, 1MiB/32) = 32768` → 32 KB slots), which holds the self-contained entry with headroom. Cost: **32 concurrent-unknown-sender slots** (down from 256) — birthday collision at ~7 concurrent *unknown* senders, but established contacts leave the doorbell and collision self-heals (re-seed + whole-record sweep), so it is a best-effort first-contact limit (documented; the pointer-in-doorbell + sender-owned-body split is the post-alpha lever if recipient popularity exceeds it). **Entry shrunk too:** `bind_pop` is folded into `msg_sig` (one `S_pc` signature covers the pseudonym binding AND the body — saves 4627 B), and `intended_recipient` is a **SHA-384 hash (48 B)** of `PK_lt_B`, not the full key (saves 2544 B). Entry ≈ `ct0` 1568 + sealed{`PK_lt_A` 2592 + `PK_pc_A` 2592 + `bind_lt` 4627 + `msg_sig` 4627 + `eph_ek_A` 1568 + `recipient_hash` 48 + fields} ≈ **~18 KB < 32768.** ✓
- **Erasure F3 (decision #4 does NOT close — a per-channel ack interval → 2N/min for N read-heavy channels):** **#4 re-derived with a per-client AGGREGATE standalone-ack rate cap** (≈1/min aggregate, piggyback-on-reply first; when the aggregate budget is spent, standalone acks queue and the sender's continued re-seed covers the gap — acks are advisory, delaying them delays confirmation not delivery). New arithmetic: base 2.0 + aggregate-acks ≤1.0 + re-seed tail 0.06 + doorbell 0.05 + key-record 0.02 ≈ **3.13/min < 4.** Closes. The two-sided ack **re-seed** stream (F4) is inside the same aggregate cap. **Ack READS are priced here too, because the arithmetic above prices only the writes and the fetches are the other half of the same handshake: collecting an ack is one fetch per tick per outstanding correspondence** — one acknowledgement record per (correspondence, direction), read on the same idle tick that plans the page sweep, for every correspondence holding an unsettled message. A client with `N` such correspondences therefore issues up to `N` ack fetches per tick alongside its page reads. That rate is charged against the **WB-5.1 read pool (9 permits)** and the shared 16-permit op-gate, never against the WB-2 4/min write ceiling, so the aggregate ack **write** cap above does not bound it and closing #4 says nothing about it. The bound that does apply is the read pool's concurrency, which queues the fetches rather than dropping them: an ack is advisory, so a queued fetch delays confirmation and never delivery.
- **Erasure F5 (record-ceiling constant wrong — cited `remote_max_records=64`, which governs records hosted FOR OTHERS; a client's own channel records go in the LOCAL store, count-uncapped `max_records: None`):** the ~25-correspondent ceiling is **withdrawn** — the real bound is memory / subkey-cache + the shared 16-permit op-gate for concurrent opens/sweeps + per-record resweep latency (#171), not a hard 64. Residual **relaxed**: "open-channel count is bounded by op-gate concurrency and resweep latency (#171 tail-sweep), not a hard record cap." (native `remote_max_records`=128 anyway.)
- **Erasure F1 (page-probe frontier vs ack cursor — a permanent mid-page gap re-stalls the suffix if the probe frontier is anchored to the contiguous ack cursor):** the **page-probe frontier is explicitly distinct from the contiguous ack cursor.** The frontier advances on **per-page content-presence** (a page with ≥1 populated slot is "reached"; a missing mid-page slot ≠ empty page), so a permanent gap never freezes the frontier. The contiguous ack cursor (high-water) is a separate pointer for confirmation only. Two distinct pointers, stated.
- **Erasure F2 (contiguous-prefix ack leaves the whole downstream permanently UNCONFIRMED on one permanent loss):** **restore the gap-bitmap** (dropped from v2) — the ack carries `high_water` (contiguous prefix) **+ a bounded bitmap of collected seqs beyond the prefix**, so a message collected downstream of a permanent gap **confirms** rather than sitting stuck-unconfirmed forever. Never false "delivered" (fail-safe holds); this only lets truthful confirmation reach past a permanent gap.
- **Erasure F6 (LRU-evicted idle channel holds no watch → notification-dark until re-opened):** documented residual (couples #225 OS notifications); a periodic resweep of cached channels (WB-4.L2) catches messages that arrived while evicted.
- **Crypto MINOR invariants:** (a) bind `eph_ct_G` in `msg_sig` (belt-and-braces over the AEAD-only protection). (b) Explicit verifier rule: a within-window `fc_epoch` replay MUST NOT re-initialize an **already-established** channel's `RK0` (idempotent re-accept only; no ratchet reset). (c) The `dflt(1)` key record is world-**writable** (world-derivable owner) → DoS-only overwrite/erase (authenticated by the inner sig, no forgery) — extends the accepted first-contact-best-effort residual from the doorbell to the key record.
- **Metadata MINOR tidies:** (a) the `fc_epoch`-boundary re-seal is **jittered across a window after the boundary** (the M7 per-record jitter governs backoff, not the hard wall-clock deadline — so spread the boundary re-seals too). (b) Reword the key-record "never an online/offline rhythm" → "the key-record re-seed still tracks B's online windows at coarse eviction granularity (F5/M14), subsumed by the finer accepted lobby-presence F1 signal." (c) **The first-contact doorbell entry is padded** to a bucketed constant (the earlier padding covered only ongoing pages); and "closes the size leak" is scoped to **bucket granularity** (a coarse size-class per message remains — F1-class).

### v6 residuals (accepted for alpha, or documented)

Lobby-rhythm (+ bucket-class size, + fc-boundary re-seal window). Key-record rollback (+ key-record world-writable DoS). Recovery. Opening-burst no-FS (prekeys dropped; `‹MAINTAINER›` override). First-contact best-effort-under-attack + **32-slot concurrent-unknown-sender limit** (birthday at ~7; pointer+body split is the post-alpha lever). `intended_recipient` post-decap read (now a hash). MAX_SKIP bounded per-message loss. Ack-not-FS. Universal address-plane harvest-now. Open-channel count bounded by op-gate/resweep-latency (relaxed from the withdrawn ~25 ceiling). LRU-evicted-channel notification-dark (couples #225).

**Status: DRAFT v6 — folds R7's BLOCKER (doorbell sizing) + the #4 re-derivation + spec fixes; no architectural refutation across R5–R7.** Next: a focused round-8 confirming pass (verify the `dflt(32)` sizing fits, #4 closes at aggregate-cap, the probe-frontier/gap-bitmap fixes hold) → the maintainer's freeze + prekey sub-call.

## CONVERGED — freeze-ready pending ratification

**All three adversarial review axes returned HOLDS — accepted residuals only.** Round 7 metadata: HOLDS. Round 8 erasure: HOLDS (#4 closes at 3.13/min). Round 8 crypto: HOLDS (the `dflt(32)` sizing fit re-verified byte-by-byte, ~18 KB base / ~23 KB with token < 32768). Eight rounds run (R1–R2 refuted the approach, R3–R4 the v1/v2 machinery, R5 first no-BLOCKER, R6 confirmed, R7 caught the sizing BLOCKER + #4 error, **R8 confirms v6 clean**). No architectural refutation since R5; the ss/AR-gated authority, PQ double-ratchet, paging, and fail-safe delivery held throughout.

**Two decisions remain, both the maintainer's** — the design cannot self-freeze:
1. **`‹MAINTAINER›` — one-time PQ prekeys.** Dropped by default (opening burst before B's first reply has no FS; harvest-now recovers it). Re-add PQXDH prekeys if opening-burst FS is required for alpha. PFS-scope call.
2. **Freeze ratification.** On a yes, this doc's status flips to FROZEN and the build slices graduate into GitHub issues (forcing #177).

### Build-contract spec-precision items (fold at freeze — one sentence each, none blocking)

From R7/R8, to make the build unambiguous:
- **(i)** The standalone-ack rate cap MUST be a **client-global aggregate** limiter (~1/min), never per-channel (a per-channel interval reopens the F3 breach at 2N/min).
- **(ii)** The beyond-prefix ack bitmap is **RLE-encoded + monotonic/union-merged**, with a **prefix-advance-on-give-up** rule (the receiver advances its contiguous cursor past a message the sender has abandoned at the 7-day give-up), so "bounded" holds under accumulated permanent gaps and a replayed stale ack can never un-confirm.
- **(iii)** `msg_sig` is **mandatory-and-verified under `PK_pc`** with the verifier enforcing `PK_pc`/`PK_lt` consistency across `bind_lt` — this is now the **sole** PoP path (post `bind_pop` fold), applied **symmetrically to B's ACCEPT**; any future frame type MUST carry `msg_sig` or PoP silently vanishes there.
- **(iv)** The top padding bucket MUST be **≤ 32768** (so a padded token-bearing entry still fits the `dflt(32)` subkey), and the write guard MUST be **schema-derived** (fixes the latent `APP_MESSAGE_CAP=32768` over-permit — true `dflt(64)` cap is 16384).
- **(v)** The `fc_epoch`-boundary re-seal is **jittered across a window after the boundary** (per-record backoff jitter governs backoff, not the hard wall-clock deadline).
- **Honest caveat:** the #4 budget margin is thin — ~0.4/min headroom if the WB-2 base is taken at its upper 2.5/min (vs ~0.9 at 2.0). Within the ceiling either way; flag for the manual test to watch write latency.

### Accepted residuals (final list — all ratified or newly documented, none blocking)

Lobby-rhythm (+ bucket-class size, + fc-boundary re-seal window). Key-record rollback (+ world-writable key record makes it actively plantable — bounded by highest-verified-version cache). Recovery/reachability. Opening-burst no-FS (`‹MAINTAINER›`). First-contact best-effort-under-attack + **32-slot concurrent-unknown-sender limit** (birthday at ~7; pointer+body split = post-alpha lever). `intended_recipient` post-decap read (now a hash). MAX_SKIP bounded per-message loss. Ack-not-FS. Universal address-plane harvest-now. Open-channel count bounded by op-gate/resweep-latency (#171), not a hard record cap. LRU-evicted-channel notification-dark (couples #225).

**Status: DRAFT v6, CONVERGED, freeze-ready pending the prekey call + ratification.** On freeze: re-cut the ISC-C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25 family to v6, spawn build-slice issues (#177 forced), and this doc becomes the frozen design-of-record.

## ‹MAINTAINER› prekey call — RESOLVED: DROP prekeys · reserve the selector field · educate users

The one open scope decision is made. This closes the last `‹MAINTAINER›`; only the freeze ratification remains.

- **One-time PQ prekeys are DROPPED for alpha and expected to stay dropped.** `ss0 = encaps(static EK_lt_B)`. The opening burst (A's messages before B's first reply) is **not forward-secret** — harvest-now-decrypt-later recovers it on a future `DK_lt_B` compromise. Accepted, with the user-education mitigation below.
- **User education IS the mitigation (product requirement, not a crypto control) — "the first DM is not secure; it's only hello."** The pre-establishment first message is labeled and treated as **hello-grade**: the UI plainly communicates that a DM sent *before the recipient has replied* is not yet forward-secret and must not carry secrets. The sensitive handoff (circle entropy, etc.) belongs in the **established** conversation — after B's first reply forks the ratchet. This fits the bootstrap use case exactly: **say hello first, hand over secrets once secured.** Build requirement: (a) a first-message affordance/warning on an unestablished DM ("not yet secured — say hello, not secrets"); (b) the transcript visibly marks the pre-establishment message(s) as hello-grade; (c) new-contact/onboarding copy states the property. Folds into § UI.
- **Reserved key-selector field (forward insurance, kept even though it is expected to stay unused):** the first-contact entry carries a **`key_selector`** field — **alpha always writes `STATIC`**; the value space reserves prekey-index encodings. This makes any future prekey re-introduction a **no-wire-change** add (old clients keep writing `STATIC`; a future client could write a prekey index and fall back to `STATIC` against an old peer) rather than an additive proto bump. Cost: one reserved field now. Note the forward-only asymmetry that makes this insurance and not a promise — adding prekeys later protects *future* opening bursts, never the alpha-window ones already recorded (which is itself an argument the deferral is right for a small-user-base alpha).

**Residual update:** "Opening-burst no-FS" moves from `‹MAINTAINER›`-open to **accepted + user-educated** (hello-grade labeling is the mitigation; the `key_selector` field keeps the door open at zero future wire cost).

## Build notes — amendments to the frozen text (append-only)

Corrections found while building, recorded here rather than by editing the frozen construction above. Each is a **notation or wording** fix; none changes the construction.

- **2026-07-28 — `LE64(...)` throughout this document means BIG-endian in the build.** Every integer in this doc's pseudocode is written `LE64(version)` / `LE64(seq)` / `LE64(epoch)`, with no legend defining the convention. The repo's two existing signed-preimage builders are big-endian — `room_message.rs:61 provenance_input` length-prefixes with `(len as u64).to_be_bytes()` and encodes its `i64` timestamp with `to_be_bytes()`, and `share_announce.rs:123 push_lp` does the same — so the build uses **`u64` big-endian for both length prefixes and integer fields**, and `lp(x)` means `u64-BE length ‖ bytes`. No security property depends on byte order, only on both ends agreeing and on the encoding being unambiguous; consistency with the one existing preimage helper is worth more than literal fidelity to the doc's shorthand. **A second implementer following `LE64` literally would build an incompatible preimage**, which is why this is recorded rather than left to inference. (Raised by two independent reviews of the #232 build.)
- **2026-07-28 — `fc_epoch` is NOT a key-record field.** ISC-C40 and issue #232 both said the key record "advertises `fc_epoch = floor(now/FC_PERIOD)`". That is stale lineage from the pre-R6 `fc_nonce_B` design. R6 replaced it with a wall-clock epoch **both parties derive from the clock** — no B-side state, no restart coupling, no restart oracle — and publishing it would reintroduce all three while breaking the key record's presence-independence. ISC-C40 amended in `ISA.md` (ratified); reasoning recorded on #232. `fc_epoch` is bound in the first-contact entry (ISC-C41), never in the key record.

- **2026-07-28 — every DM owner-seed and slot derivation HKDF-Extracts under an explicit salt.** This document writes each derivation as an `info` label alone — `HKDF("daemonseed/dm/doorbell/addr/v4", PK_lt_B)`, `HKDF("daemonseed/dm/keyrec/owner/v1", ikm = PK_lt)` — with no salt named. The build extracts under a purpose-specific salt first and then expands under the label this document names: `HKDF-Extract(salt = "daemonseed/dm/<purpose>/salt/v1", ikm) → Expand(info = <the label above>)`. RFC 5869 permits an absent salt, so a second implementer following the text literally would extract with the zero salt and derive a **different owner seed** — addressing a different record and never seeing the first implementation's writes. Same failure class as the `LE64` note above, and recorded for the same reason. Salts in use: `daemonseed/dm/keyrec/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot-salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/fc/salt/v1` (first-contact seal key), `daemonseed/dm/root/salt/v1` (the two roots from `ss0`).
- **2026-07-28 — `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot/v5` is the slot-HKDF `info`, not the label that produces the sender secret.** § R6 (crypto V4-5) pins that string to "the mnemonic-derived expansion" yielding `sender_secret_A`. The build instead derives the secret as a sixth expansion of the *identity PRK* under the identity-scoped label `daemonseed/identity/{primary|device-<uuid>}/dm-doorbell-slot/v1`, and uses `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot/v5` as the `info` of the slot HKDF that consumes it. The derivation chain is `identity PRK → dm-doorbell-slot/v1 → DmDoorbellSlotSecret`, then `Extract(slot-salt/v1, secret) → Expand(slot/v5 ‖ lp(PK_lt_B)) mod 32`. Rationale for the identity scoping — and why the doc's "multi-device-consistent" wording is deliberately not implemented literally — is in `ISA.md` `## Decisions`.

- **2026-07-28 — the authorship-signature domain is `daemonseed/dm/msg/sig/v6`, not the `…/v2` § v2 names.** § DRAFT v2 pinned `daemonseed/dm/msg/sig/v2` for a preimage that bound `chan_id ‖ seq ‖ eph_ek ‖ recipient ‖ sent_ms ‖ body`. v3 added `dir`, v6 folded `bind_pop` in (so the signature must also cover `pk_pc` and `pk_lt`), and v6 minor (a) added the generation ciphertext. The signed content is therefore materially different from what `/v2` named, and reusing that string would let two implementations disagree about what a `/v2` signature covers. The build uses `/v6` and signs, in order, each length-prefixed: `chan_id ‖ dir ‖ BE64(seq) ‖ eph_ek ‖ intended_recipient_hash ‖ pk_pc ‖ pk_lt ‖ BE64(sent_unix_ms) ‖ body`.

- **2026-07-28 — `chan_id` is derived from `ss0`, not from `AR`.** § v2 writes `chan_id = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/chanid/v2", AR)`. The build derives both roots as siblings of one extraction — `Extract("daemonseed/dm/root/salt/v1", ss0)` then `Expand("daemonseed/dm/addr/root/v3")` for `AR` and `Expand("daemonseed/dm/chanid/v2")` for `chan_id` — which is equivalent in strength (both are HKDF outputs of the same secret under distinct labels) and avoids chaining one secret's derivation through another. Recorded because a second implementer following the frozen text would compute a different `chan_id`, and `chan_id` is bound into every `msg_sig`.
- **2026-07-28 — `msg_sig` binds a FRAME-KIND label, and `dir` is the ASCII literal `a2b` / `b2a`.** The frozen text names neither. Without a frame-kind discriminator, a first-contact signature is a byte-valid ongoing-channel signature for the same tuple — and the channel frame binds a generation ciphertext this one does not, so the two shapes must not be confusable under one domain. The build prepends `lp(frame_kind)` (first contact = `fc`) immediately after the domain, giving the full first-contact order: `daemonseed/dm/msg/sig/v6 ‖ lp("fc") ‖ lp(chan_id) ‖ lp(dir) ‖ lp(BE64(seq)) ‖ lp(eph_ek) ‖ lp(intended_recipient_hash) ‖ lp(pk_pc) ‖ lp(pk_lt) ‖ lp(BE64(sent_unix_ms)) ‖ lp(body))`. The ongoing-channel frame will use its own kind label and append `lp(eph_ct_G)` per v6 minor invariant (a).
- **2026-07-28 — the invite `token` rides INSIDE the seal, in `FirstContactBody`, not beside it.** § v1's entry sketch is ambiguous about placement; the token is `Sign_recipient(grantee_PK_lt ‖ nonce ‖ expiry)`, so carrying it outside would publish the long-term public key of every sender knocking at an invite-only recipient — in the clear, in a world-readable slot, defeating the sender-blindness the doorbell exists for. Unlike the PoW (which must be checkable before decapsulation and is therefore outside), the token is checked against `pk_lt`, which is only readable after decapsulation anyway. `FirstContactEntry` field 4 is `reserved`.

- **2026-07-28 — the ratchet derivations extract under explicit salts too, and `RK0` is a third sibling of the `ss0` extraction.** § v2 writes the key schedule as bare `info` labels over a secret — `RK0 = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/ratchet/root/v2", ss0)`, `CK_dir = HKDF(RK, "daemonseed/dm/chain/{a2b,b2a}/v2")`, `MK/CK' = HKDF(CK_n, "daemonseed/dm/{mk,ck}/v2")` — with no salt named, the same omission the doorbell/key-record note above records. The build extracts first: `RK0` shares the single `Extract("daemonseed/dm/root/salt/v1", ss0)` that already yields `AR` and `chan_id`, expanded under `daemonseed/dm/ratchet/root/v2` as a third sibling (siblings rather than a chain, so the retained addressing root can never yield the ratchet root it outlives); chain keys come from `Extract("daemonseed/dm/chain/salt/v1", RK)`; and one symmetric step is `Extract("daemonseed/dm/chain/step-salt/v1", CK_n)` expanded twice, under `daemonseed/dm/mk/v2` and `daemonseed/dm/ck/v2`. **The generation step is the one derivation with no constant salt, deliberately:** `RK_G = Extract(salt = RK_{G-1}, ikm = ss_G) → Expand("daemonseed/dm/ratchet/step/v2")`, the standard double-ratchet root step, where the previous root *is* the salt — which is what makes the result depend on both the ancestor and the new entropy. A second implementer following the frozen text literally would extract with the zero salt and derive a different schedule from the same `ss0`; recorded for the same reason as the `LE64` and owner-seed notes.
- **2026-07-28 — `MAX_SKIP` is TWO bounds in the build, and a catch-up past it is refused rather than truncated.** § v3 gives one number — "a bounded skipped-message-key cache (`MAX_SKIP = 64` per direction)" — and the build needs two, because processing a *single* message across a ratchet generation change is *two* catch-ups: first out to the end of the previous chain, then along the new chain to the arriving sequence number. Sizing the cache at 64 would let that ordinary, benign step evict the previous chain's tail, which is exactly the backlog the `(ratchet_gen, PN)` header was added to preserve. So `MAX_SKIP = 64` bounds how far one `skip` call reaches (the frozen per-direction number, unchanged) and `SKIPPED_KEY_CAPACITY = 2 × MAX_SKIP = 128` bounds the cache. The totals agree with the frozen text: 64 per direction across two directions is the same 128, and a party only ever skips on its receive direction. Second half: a `skip` beyond `MAX_SKIP` returns an error carrying the requested distance rather than deriving as far as it can. The sequence number driving it arrives in a frame that cannot be authenticated until the keys that call produces exist, so it is attacker-controlled at the moment it is read; a truncated catch-up would hand back keys filed at positions no message will ever claim, and an unbounded one would let a peer name `u64::MAX` and derive until the process dies. **The remaining half of that defence is the caller's and cannot live in the key schedule: skipped keys are committed to the cache only after the arriving frame authenticates.** Otherwise a peer naming many distinct far-ahead sequence numbers flushes the cache and permanently strands messages it has already published, for the cost of one write — an attacker without the chain key cannot produce a frame that opens, so gating the commit closes it entirely.
- **2026-07-28 — `a2b` / `b2a` have exactly one definition, `ratchet::Direction::label()`.** They are frozen wire bytes inside the `msg_sig` preimage and inside `page_addr`, but they are not HKDF labels, so they do not live in the `daemonseed/dm/…` namespace module with the rest. The first-contact builder originally spelled its own copy; both now come from the one `const fn`. A second definition would surface only as a signature two versions of this client could not verify for each other — the failure mode the label registry exists to prevent, in the one place the registry does not reach.

- **2026-07-28 — the initiator's channel sequence starts at 1; sequence 0 is the first-contact entry.** The frozen text sets `seq = 0` on the first-contact entry (§ v1 body, § v6 verifier rules) and separately addresses ongoing messages at `page_addr(dir, seq)`, without saying whether the channel's sequence space continues that numbering or restarts. The build continues it: the initiator's first *channel* message is `a2b` sequence **1**, because sequence 0 was the knock — which travels via the recipient's doorbell, not the channel. One monotonic sequence per direction across the whole conversation is what lets the delivery acknowledgement's contiguous prefix confirm the opening message; a channel-local numbering would force the ack to special-case the one message that did not arrive by channel. The recipient's opening reply is `b2a` sequence **0**, the first thing either party writes to the channel itself. Consequence to expect when reading a live record: **`a2b` page 0 slot 0 is permanently empty**, which is harmless because the page-probe frontier judges a page reached by its holding *any* populated slot, never by being full (§ v6 erasure F1).
- **2026-07-28 — the `(ratchet_gen, PN)` header is built as `(ratchet_gen, chain_base)`.** § v4 (crypto V8) specifies `PN` as "previous-chain length", following Signal, where a chain's message index restarts at zero on every ratchet step. Here it does not: the sequence number is monotonic per direction across the whole conversation because it *addresses the page the message lives in*. So the build carries the same information absolutely — the sequence number at which this chain begins — rather than as a count. The receiver uses it directly and identically (`chain_base` is exactly where the previous chain ended, so it runs that chain out to `chain_base - 1` before switching), with no arithmetic against a base it would otherwise have to track separately, and it stays unambiguous when the message that would have anchored a relative count is lost.
- **2026-07-28 — no ephemeral selector on the wire: the generation identifies which of our ephemerals a frame targets.** ML-KEM decapsulation never fails — a wrong decapsulation key yields a wrong shared secret silently — so a receiver holding several ephemerals must know which one a frame encapsulated to, and the frozen text names no field for it. None is needed. A generation belongs to exactly one sender, and a party can only step by encapsulating to an ephemeral the other side published, which only sending publishes; so a peer can never be more than one generation ahead of our last send, and a frame at generation `G` always targets the ephemeral we published at `G-1`. Ephemerals are therefore kept indexed by the generation that published them, over a bounded window (`EPHEMERAL_WINDOW = 4`) that covers reordering without retaining material that undoes past generations' forward secrecy. Skipping a whole generation is structurally impossible rather than merely unlikely, which is why no recovery path exists for it.
- **2026-07-28 — the "commit only after the frame authenticates" rule is structural, not documentary.** The build-contract note above states it as a caller obligation; `Ratchet::receive` instead takes the caller's open-and-verify as a closure, computes every state change against clones, and applies them only if that closure succeeds. A frame's sequence number cannot be checked before the key it names has been derived, so an obligation stated in prose would be one careless caller away from letting a peer name a distant sequence number, fill the skipped-key cache with keys nobody will ever ask for, and permanently strand messages it had already published — for the cost of one write. Deriving is bounded work and stays allowed; committing is what does damage, and it can no longer happen for a frame that did not open.

- **2026-07-28 — CORRECTION to the "refused rather than truncated" note above: a receiver loses messages, never the direction.** That earlier note was written against the key schedule alone, where the only caller was hypothetical. Building the state machine showed the rule it stated is wrong at the level that matters, and two independent reviews reached the same conclusion from different directions. A chain cannot be indexed — reaching position *n* means stepping to it — so a receiver that *refuses* a wide catch-up can never move its cursor at all. Every later frame on that chain then sits further ahead still, and the whole inbound direction dies permanently while continuing to look healthy from both ends: the sender keeps re-seeding to its seven-day give-up and truthfully reports everything unconfirmed, and nothing anywhere says the channel is dead. That is the unbounded-downstream loss § v6 (crypto V4-1) explicitly re-scoped *away* from, so the refusal contradicted the frozen residual rather than implementing it.

  The build therefore distinguishes **how many keys are kept** from **how far the chain is walked**. `MAX_SKIP` (64) still bounds retention. A new `MAX_CATCH_UP` (16 × `MAX_SKIP`) bounds the walk: positions past the retention bound are stepped over and discarded, counted as *abandoned*, and the arriving message opens. A previous chain being retired at a ratchet step is cheaper still — it is discarded outright with no derivation at all when the gap is unwalkable, since nothing downstream needs the positions it skips. Only a gap beyond `MAX_CATCH_UP` *on the chain the message actually sits on* is refused, and it is refused by distance (`BacklogTooWide`), which self-heals at the peer's next ratchet step rather than persisting. Both bounds still hold the original anti-DoS property: the sequence number driving them is unauthenticated, and the work it can buy is capped.

  The losses are surfaced, not swallowed: `Ratchet::losses()` reports pending, evicted and abandoned counts separately, because "this conversation is busy" and "this conversation permanently lost 200 messages while you were away" must not look alike to the layer that has to tell a user.

- **2026-07-28 — the opening ephemeral's two halves are checked to be a keypair.** Nothing in the frozen text asks for this, and nothing would report its absence: ML-KEM decapsulation never fails, so an initiator opened with mismatched halves derives a pseudorandom secret at the first ratchet step, a wrong root, and then rejects every reply the recipient ever sends — indistinguishable from tampering, forever, with no error at the point the mistake was made. FIPS 203 embeds the encapsulation key inside the decapsulation key, so the check is a memcmp against bytes already held.

- **2026-07-28 — a page address keys on `AR` alone and varies in the HKDF `info`, length-prefixed.** § v4 (the paging fix) writes `page_addr(dir,p) = HKDF("daemonseed/dm/page/addr/v4", AR ‖ dir ‖ LE64(p))`, i.e. the label as `info` and the three values concatenated into the `ikm`. The build instead extracts `AR` alone under `daemonseed/dm/page/salt/v1` and expands under `daemonseed/dm/page/addr/v4 ‖ lp(dir) ‖ lp(BE64(page))`. Two reasons, both the same class as the `LE64` and owner-seed notes above. First, RFC 5869 means `ikm` for keying material and `info` for the context that varies a derivation, and the existing sibling already does it that way — `doorbell::slot_for` keys on the sender secret and puts the recipient's key in the `info`; a second shape for the same job in the same namespace is drift. Second, the frozen text's raw concatenation is **ambiguous**: `dir` has no length marker, so nothing in the construction stops one `(dir, page)` pair from producing the byte string of another. Length-prefixing both closes that by construction rather than by the accident of both labels being three bytes today. A second implementer following the frozen text literally derives a different address and never sees this implementation's writes, which is why this is recorded rather than left to inference.

- **2026-07-28 — the complete salt registry, superseding the partial list above.** The earlier salt note enumerated five, and two commits have since added more without updating it — an authoritative-looking incomplete list is worse than none, since it is what a second implementer reads. The full set now in use: `daemonseed/dm/keyrec/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot-salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/fc/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/root/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/chain/salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/chain/step-salt/v1`, `daemonseed/dm/page/salt/v1`. The one derivation with no constant salt remains the ratchet generation step, which extracts under the previous root. `dm::domain`'s registry test now reads its own source and holds `ALL` to it in both directions, so a label added without registration fails the build rather than going silently unchecked — but that test covers the label namespace, not this prose list, which stays a manual obligation.

- **2026-07-28 — the channel frame's AAD is `daemonseed/dm/msg/aad/v4` over every CLEAR field, past the three earlier spellings.** The design names this AAD three times and means something different each time: § v1 `"…/msg/aad/v1" ‖ chan_id ‖ LE64(epoch)`, § v2 `"…/msg/aad/v2" ‖ chan_id` (epoch dropped), § v3 `"…/msg/aad/v3" ‖ chan_id ‖ dir` — the last carried unchanged into v4 and v6, so `/v3` is the frozen text's current name. What the build binds is broader than all three, because the ratchet header and both pieces of ephemeral material travel in the clear and none of the three covers them. The build binds all of it — `DM_MSG_AAD ‖ lp(chan_id) ‖ lp(dir) ‖ lp(BE64(gen)) ‖ lp(BE64(chain_base)) ‖ lp(BE64(seq)) ‖ lp(eph_ek) ‖ lp(eph_ct)` — so a header edited in flight fails the AEAD open *before* the ratchet is asked to move to the position it claims. Without that, a clear header would be authenticated only by `msg_sig`, which cannot be checked until after the frame opens, and the frame cannot open until the header has already been used to derive a key. `chan_id` leads it and is still never serialized. **The version is bumped rather than reused** for the same reason `msg/sig/v6` was: reusing `/v3` for materially broader content would let two implementations disagree about what a binding of that name covers, and the disagreement surfaces only as an undiagnosable AEAD failure.
- **2026-07-28 — a channel frame's declared `seq` is checked against the slot it was read from, and the check is a required argument rather than a caller's obligation.** § v4's paging fix makes `(dir, seq)` map to one page and slot, which is what the ack's contiguous prefix and the page-probe frontier both rest on — but `seq` arrives *inside the frame*, chosen by the peer, and owner-write authority on a page is symmetric. A peer that signs `seq = 5` and writes the bytes into the slot for `seq = 100` therefore produces a frame that opens, verifies, and is filed at a position it does not occupy, with nothing anywhere saying so. Only the collector holds both facts. `ParsedFrame::open` takes the `PagePosition` the bytes came from and rejects a mismatch before opening the seal, so the obligation is structural in the same sense the "commit only after the frame authenticates" note above records — a prose requirement here would be one careless collector away from being unenforced. **Consequence for the transport slice: the page sweep must yield `(subkey, bytes)`, not bytes alone.**
- **2026-07-28 — the channel frame's `msg_sig` extends the `/v6` preimage rather than defining a second one.** § v6 minor invariant (a) requires `eph_ct_G` bound in `msg_sig`, and § v4 (crypto V8) requires the `(ratchet_gen, PN)` header bound in it; neither says how the ongoing-channel preimage relates to the first-contact one. The build reuses `msg_sig_input` under the frame kind `msg` and appends `lp(BE64(generation)) ‖ lp(BE64(chain_base)) ‖ lp(eph_ct)`, so there is one definition of what an authorship signature covers and the frame kind is the only discriminator between the two shapes. **An absent generation ciphertext binds as a zero-length component, not as an omitted one** — the initiator's opening burst has no `eph_ct`, and skipping the component would make its preimage a prefix of nothing in particular rather than a distinct, length-delimited value.
- **2026-07-28 — `ratchet_gen` is `uint32` on the wire, matching the width the ratchet counts in.** The frozen text names no width. `uint64` would need a narrowing cast at the proto boundary, where generation 0 and generation 2³² would collide on one skipped-key slot — a silent cross-generation key confusion. The ratchet's own counter is `u32` with a `checked_add`, so the wire matching it means no cast exists at all.
- **2026-07-28 — the frame's size cap is an invariant, not a runtime check.** `MAX_FRAME_LEN` is the `dflt(16)` subkey cap (32768), but `DM_BODY_CAP` and the top padding rung bound a sealed frame near 19.6 KB, so a compose-time rejection is unreachable by construction. A guard that cannot fire reads as protection that is not there — the same defect class as the dead receive-side guard removed one commit earlier — so the check is a `debug_assert!` and the enforcement is a test that drives the real `seal` with a maximal body and measures the result. Raising either constant without re-checking the fit fails the build rather than silently producing frames the network rejects.

- **2026-07-28 — the acknowledgement signature covers the gap bitmap, under `daemonseed/dm/ack/sig/v3`.** § DRAFT v2 gives the preimage as `"daemonseed/dm/ack/sig/v2" ‖ chan_id ‖ LE64(high_water)`, and it was written in the draft that had **dropped** the bitmap; v6 (erasure F2) restored the bitmap and build-contract item (ii) made it monotonic and union-merged, but neither restates the preimage. So the frozen text as it stands signs only one of the two things that decide confirmation. That is not a cosmetic omission: the sender flips a message to *confirmed-collected* on `high_water ≥ seq` **or** its bitmap bit, so an unsigned bitmap is a forgeable confirmation for anyone who can write the ack record — and while a channel page is owner-write-gated against third parties, the pair's own authority is symmetric and the whole point of `msg_sig` on the message path is that owner authority proves the stream, never the author. The build signs everything that decides confirmation: `daemonseed/dm/ack/sig/v3 ‖ lp(chan_id) ‖ lp(dir) ‖ lp(BE64(high_water)) ‖ lp(canonical run encoding)`. **The version is bumped rather than reused** for the same reason `msg/sig/v6` and `msg/aad/v4` were — reusing `/v2` for materially broader content would let two implementations disagree about what a signature of that name covers, and the disagreement surfaces only as a verification failure nobody can diagnose. `dir` is bound as well, because the frozen text names one preimage for a construction that is per-direction from `K_ack(dir)` down; a signature that does not say which half it confirms is safe only by an argument about a key held somewhere else, and the same reasoning already put `dir` in `msg_sig`. The preimage builder takes the state rather than its two halves, so there is no call shape that signs a high-water and a bitmap which do not belong together.

- **2026-07-28 — the ack's high-water is `Option<u64>`, and an absent prefix binds as a zero-length component.** § v2 types it as a bare `u64`. It cannot be one: sequence 0 is a live, ackable position on **both** directions — on `b2a` it is the recipient's opening reply, and on `a2b` it is the first-contact entry, which the build note above deliberately keeps in the same sequence space so the contiguous prefix can confirm the opening message — so there is no spare value left to mean "nothing contiguous collected yet", and that state is reachable the moment a conversation's first collected message arrives out of order. A sentinel would have to be `u64::MAX` or an off-by-one re-basing, both of which put arithmetic between the wire and its meaning. The build carries `Option<u64>` and binds it in the signature preimage the way `frame_sig_input` binds an absent generation ciphertext: `lp(BE64(h))` when present, `lp(&[])` when not. `high_water = Some(0)` and `high_water = None` are different statements and must not be confusable, or one is replayable as the other.

- **2026-07-28 — the ack bitmap's bound is 64 runs, the encoding is canonical, and the cap refuses rather than truncates.** Build-contract item (ii) requires the beyond-prefix bitmap to be RLE-encoded and bounded but names no number and no at-cap behaviour, and "bounded" without either is not a contract a second implementer can meet. The build fixes all three. **`MAX_ACK_RUNS = 64`, and it is a policy choice about when a conversation counts as degraded rather than a number derived from anywhere else.** It is tempting to justify it as "matching `MAX_SKIP`, the same quantity the ratchet bounds when it sizes its skipped-key cache". That justification is wrong, and wrong in the specific way `ratchet.rs` warns about in bold: `MAX_SKIP` (64) is how far a *single* skip call reaches, while `SKIPPED_KEY_CAPACITY` (128) is how many skipped keys the cache holds at once — and it is the second one that is comparable to a count of simultaneous outstanding gaps. Conflating the two is named there as a real defect, so this constant claims neither: it is set at 64 on its own argument, deliberately *below* the ratchet's 128. **Why below:** the two caps fail in opposite directions. Exceeding the key cache destroys keys, and those messages become permanently unopenable, silently. Exceeding this cap refuses a record, changes nothing, and heals when the give-up rule frees capacity. Setting them equal would make the recoverable failure arrive at the same moment as the unrecoverable one, so the cheaper failure is made to bind first. **The price, stated rather than glossed:** under alternating loss the run set reaches 64 at sequence 127, and the next genuinely-collected message is refused *while the ratchet still holds every skipped key and opened that message fine*. So between 65 and 128 interleaved outstanding positions the receiver displays messages it cannot acknowledge, the sender re-seeds them to the seven-day give-up, and reports *undelivered* for messages that were in fact read. Fail-safe and recoverable, but a real window. Raising the constant to 128 would close it at the cost of the ordering above; either number needs this argument restated, not just the value edited. Encoded worst case is 2 + 64×16 = 1026 bytes — and that is not the reason for 64, since 128 runs would be 2050 bytes, equally negligible beside the record this rides in. **The encoding is canonical and the decoder enforces it**: a `u16` run count, then one `(gap, extent)` pair of big-endian `u64`s per run, where `gap` is measured from *two* past the previous run's end — so touching or out-of-order runs are unspellable rather than merely rejected — and `extent` is `end - start` rather than a length, so a run at the top of the sequence space has no unrepresentable size. A decode rejects more runs than the cap (before reserving a byte), a body length that is not exactly `count × 16`, arithmetic that leaves the sequence space, and any run at or below the prefix. Canonicity is load-bearing rather than tidy: it is what lets a verifier rebuild the signature preimage from the decoded state instead of trusting the bytes it was handed. **At the cap the insert or merge is refused and the state is left unchanged** — truncating would drop a position already confirmed, and un-confirming is the one direction a monotonic ack may not move; a refusal costs re-seeding, which is the fail-safe direction, and the give-up rule below frees the capacity again. Note also that a decoded run claiming `u64::MAX` positions is **accepted**, in sixteen bytes and constant time: a peer asserting it collected everything is lying about its own collection, which no acknowledgement scheme can prevent; what matters is that the lie never drives an allocation.

- **2026-07-28 — prefix-advance-on-give-up makes the ack carry *settled*, not *collected*, and only the sender's outbox can tell the two apart.** Build-contract item (ii) requires the receiver to advance its contiguous cursor past a message the sender abandoned at the seven-day give-up, and gives the reason: without it the bitmap is unbounded under accumulated permanent gaps. The consequence it does not state is that the sender then reads `high_water ≥ seq` for a message that was **never collected**, and the fail-safe posture forbids reporting that as delivered. The core cannot close this: it holds sequence numbers, and only the sender knows which of *its own* messages it gave up on. So the honest reading of the wire is **settled** — "stop re-seeding this, I will never ask for it again" — and the fail-safe property rests on the persisted outbox, where *undelivered* is a terminal state and a given-up message is never re-consulted against an ack. The build states this in the module docs, splits the one state transition into two named methods (`collect` / `abandon`) so the meaning is at least visible at the call site, and pins the behaviour with a test named for it rather than leaving it to be discovered in a UI. **This is a live obligation on the outbox slice**, not a resolved item.

- **2026-07-28 — a peer's ack is clipped to the highest sequence we have actually sent, and the ceiling is a required argument on the only path into our state.** Nothing in the frozen text bounds `high_water` on arrival, and union-merge is precisely what makes an unbounded one unrecoverable: a high-water only ever rises, so one signed ack claiming `2^40` — from a broken client or a hostile one — settles every position that will ever exist, and every message the sender composes afterwards reads as delivered before it is transmitted. That is the failure § D-DELIV exists to prevent, arriving through the mechanism meant to prevent it. It is *not* the already-recorded case of a peer lying about its own collection, which costs only the liar; this one costs the honest sender the truth about every message it will ever send. **The bound needs no trust:** a peer cannot have collected what was never transmitted, so the highest sequence we have sent on that direction is a ceiling no honest ack reaches, and it is known locally. `merge_peer_ack(other, highest_sent)` takes it as a required argument; `merge_own_ack` is the separate, unbounded path for the one trusted merge in the design (a receiver recovering from its own last published ack). **It clips rather than refuses**, because a peer's high-water is itself monotonic: a refusal is not a retry but a permanent rejection that would also discard the truthful low half of the same ack, and a local ceiling can legitimately lag — a client that published sequence 12 and crashed before persisting its outbox recovers believing it sent 9 — so refusal would let one crash report a whole conversation as undelivered although it was read.
