Clipping keeps the honest prefix, discards only what no honest peer could claim, is strictly fail-safe (a subset of the claim, so the error is only ever toward *undelivered*), and heals as the ceiling advances. The clip covers the runs as well as the prefix, since a run above the ceiling settles positions just as effectively, and it applies to the peer's copy alone — our own retained state is never clipped, because the ceiling can regress across a restart while our settled positions may not. **The clip happens on merge and not in the decoder**, because a verifier rebuilds the signature preimage from the decoded state: a decoder that altered the state would rebuild a preimage the peer's signature cannot match. The order is decode → verify → merge, and the decoder is named `decode_unvalidated` to say so at the call site. `merge_peer_ack` returns `PeerAckOutcome`, so an over-claim is visible to the transport slice rather than silently absorbed; nothing legitimate produces one.

- **2026-07-28 — the state's query is `is_settled`, not `is_confirmed`.** Item (ii)'s prefix-advance-on-give-up means an abandoned position answers `true`, so *confirmed* — the word a UI maps directly onto *delivered* — was the one identifier in the module contradicting its own contract. The rename costs nothing (there are no consumers yet) and puts the honest word at the call site, where the caveat is actually read. The settled-is-not-collected caveat now sits on the query's own docstring as well as on `abandon` and in the module docs.

- **2026-07-28 — `daemonseed/dm/ack/salt/v1` joins the salt registry, and `daemonseed/dm/ack/seal/v3` is the HKDF `info` with the direction length-prefixed.** § v3 (crypto F-3) writes the ack key as `K_ack(dir) = HKDF(AR, "daemonseed/dm/ack/seal/v3" ‖ dir)` — the same shape the page-address note above records: a label as `info`, values concatenated without length markers, and no salt named. The build extracts `AR` under `daemonseed/dm/ack/salt/v1` and expands under `daemonseed/dm/ack/seal/v3 ‖ lp(dir)`, for both reasons already recorded there — RFC 5869's zero salt would derive a different key from the same root, and unprefixed concatenation is ambiguous by construction rather than by the accident of both direction labels being three bytes today. The salt registry note above is now short by one; the complete set is `daemonseed/dm/{keyrec,doorbell,fc,root,chain,page,ack}/salt/v1` plus `daemonseed/dm/doorbell/slot-salt/v1` and `daemonseed/dm/chain/step-salt/v1`, with the ratchet generation step still the one derivation extracting under no constant salt.

- **[SUPERSEDED by the 2026-07-30 note below — the steady-state ratchet is not persisted at all; the surplus this note calls non-optional was removed, and its subject no longer exists. The three-list reading below is still accurate and is why no freeze amendment was needed.]** **2026-07-28 — the persisted ratchet state is a superset of the frozen text's three lists, and the surplus is not optional.** The design names what must survive a restart in three places: § v4 (crypto/erasure 4.1) `{ss0, AR, RK, PK_pc_A}` for the provisional handshake, § v5 (crypto V4-2) `{ss0, A's opening ephemeral DK}` until ACCEPT is processed, and § v5 (erasure caveat) the receive high-water / page cursor. All three are about a conversation that has not started yet or about *addressing* one that has; **none of them describes a running ratchet**, and none is sufficient for one. `RK` alone is generation zero's root: a party restored from it comes back at generation zero while its peer carries on, every subsequent frame takes the advancing path, and the ephemeral that path needs no longer exists — `UnknownEphemeral`, in both directions, permanently, with nothing on either end reporting it (#243).

  The build therefore persists `{role, generation, root, both chains with key/base/cursor, our retained ephemerals, the peer ephemeral, the last-consumed peer generation, next_send_seq, the skipped-key cache in eviction order, the evicted and abandoned counts}`. Four of those are worth naming because the frozen text implies none of them and each one's absence is silent rather than loud. **The last-consumed peer generation** is what stops a restored party re-stepping against an ephemeral the peer has already moved past — the repeats § v5 (V4-1) mandates are byte-identical, so only the generation tag distinguishes them, and a re-step is a frame the peer cannot derive at all. **The generation ciphertext** is exactly the redundancy V4-1 exists for; a restart that dropped it would send frames claiming a generation with nothing to root it in, re-opening the failure V4-1 closed, for any peer that missed that generation's opening message. **The skipped-key cache** is the frozen residual made real: those keys are the only copies of messages already published and already stepped past, so losing them at a restart converts a *bounded* per-message loss into an unbounded one, silently. **The two loss counters**, because § v6's whole argument for accepting bounded loss is that it is reported; a restart that zeroed them would make a conversation that ate two hundred messages indistinguishable from a healthy one.

  Two things are deliberately absent. **`ss0` is not persisted here** — the root already descends from it, and the first-contact state that § v5 (V4-2) *does* require keeps it for its own reasons. **`chan_id` and `AR` are not in the encoding at all**, and a test asserts their bytes do not appear in it: § v4's minor invariant forbids serializing `chan_id` anywhere, and writing `AR` down beside the keys would put the conversation's whole address graph in one record. A store keying one snapshot per conversation therefore needs a local identifier of its own.

  The at-rest form is `daemonseed/dm/ratchet-state/v1\0` followed by a fixed-width big-endian body — the version inside the magic, so a decoder has one thing to compare and cannot read a v1 body under a v2 header. It is **plaintext**: a complete set of live keys, unsealed and unauthenticated, to be sealed by whatever store holds it exactly as `ss0` is. Where that store should be is an open decision, not settled by this slice.

- **[SUPERSEDED by the 2026-07-30 note below — there are no snapshots to erase. `Ratchet::snapshot` and `RatchetSnapshot` no longer exist, so nothing here is an obligation on the store any more. The forward-secrecy reasoning is why the steady-state ratchet is not persisted at all, and is retained for that.]** **2026-07-28 — the store must erase each superseded snapshot in place, and a cadence that merely writes a new one degrades forward secrecy rather than protecting it.** `Ratchet::snapshot` is infallible and non-destructive precisely so a client may snapshot on a cadence, and the cost was first written down as "a duplicate of every key the conversation holds". That understates it on this trust surface. A **superseded** snapshot is not a stale copy of secrets that are still current — it is a live decryption capability for a generation the conversation has already left: it carries that generation's root *and* the decapsulation keys of the ephemerals published in it, while the ciphertexts those frames were sealed under are already on the wire and already recorded by anyone watching. One recovered superseded snapshot therefore yields the plaintext of every message from its generation forward, up to our next self-generated step — which is exactly the forward secrecy § v5's ratchet exists to provide. So **erasure-in-place is a requirement on the profile store, not a tidiness preference**, and it is the property a naive cadence gets wrong: appending, journaling, or copy-on-write leaves every past snapshot recoverable, and a higher cadence then makes the exposure strictly worse. `ratchet.rs` cannot enforce it; the obligation is recorded here and in `RatchetSnapshot`'s rustdoc because whoever builds the store is the only one who can meet it.

  Related, and in scope for the same store: the integrity checks in `Ratchet::restore` catch corruption and *partial* rollback — one field older or otherwise inconsistent with the rest — but **none of them detects a rollback to a wholly consistent earlier snapshot**, because such a record is indistinguishable from the state it once was. That is the rollback that matters, and closing it needs a monotonic counter outside this module. Erasure-in-place is what keeps such a record from existing in the first place; the two obligations are one design problem, not two.

- **2026-07-29 — speculative contiguous probing is specified as a cadence with no interval, no lookahead bound, and no rule for re-reading a hole; the build fixes all three, and the third is a gap rather than a detail.** § v5 (erasure-MAJOR) says B "probes page `p+1` on a cadence regardless of page `p`'s fill state (and always watches the current + next page)", and § v6 (erasure F1) adds that the frontier advances on per-page content-presence. Neither names how often, how far ahead, or what happens to a position already known missing. **Interval:** `PROBE_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000`, a policy choice — the probe is a *read*, so it is outside the WB-2 write ceiling decision #4 closes against, and it is a backstop behind the watch on the current and next page rather than the path a message normally arrives by. **Lookahead:** exactly one page past the frontier, which is the frozen text read literally; deeper speculation is what #234's deferred create-on-probe side effect would charge for, since `open_or_create` means probing an unwritten page creates it. The residual that leaves is stated rather than hidden, and the first statement of it was wrong in a way worth recording: a page whose sixteen slots are *all* permanently unavailable stalls the frontier, because nothing ever probes past it — and the give-up rule, which is this design's own bound on permanent loss, **could not lift that stall**. `abandon` is `settle`; it advances the contiguous cursor over the dead page and touches no page number, the outstanding set is then empty, and the probe plan stays `[p, p + 1]` for the life of the conversation. One wholly-lost page ended the direction permanently. The build closes it by having the cursor **floor** the frontier — settling a position lifts the frontier to that position's page if the cursor has run past it, never pulls it back. A floor is not the anchor F1 forbids: F1's defect is the frontier being a *function of* the cursor, which pins it at a permanent gap, and a lower bound leaves a frontier that has already run ahead exactly where it is (pinned by `the_cursor_floors_the_frontier_and_never_pulls_it_back`, and by F1's own headline test, which the anchor mutation also fails). **The residual that genuinely remains** is therefore bounded rather than permanent: a wholly-unavailable page stalls the direction until the give-up settles every one of its sixteen positions, so the stall lasts the give-up window; and a give-up applied to only some of them leaves the cursor inside the page and the frontier with it. **Re-reading a hole is the gap.** The frozen text has the frontier run ahead and the beyond-prefix set (§ v6 erasure F2) confirm past a gap, but nothing re-reads the page a gap sits on — and the sender re-seeds an unacknowledged message until its seven-day give-up, so the bytes may well be there on a later read. Without a re-read the contiguous cursor never advances over a recoverable hole and the sender re-seeds to give-up reporting *undelivered* for a message that was recoverable all along, which is the failure F2 exists to bound rather than to cause. The build therefore makes each probe plan carry the watched pair **plus up to `MAX_BACKFILL_PAGES` (2) pages holding a hole, oldest first** — oldest because the oldest hole is the one holding the cursor, bounded because an unbounded backfill would make a gappy conversation's every probe proportional to its gap count against a shared 16-permit op-gate and the per-record resweep latency of #171. **A hole qualifies whether or not a settled position witnesses it**, and the first build got that wrong too: seeding the backfill from the outstanding set alone reaches only holes *below the highest settled position*, so a page that is populated but opens nothing — `UnknownEphemeral`, the expected answer when the frame introducing the ephemeral is among the missing ones — settles nothing, witnesses nothing, advances the frontier past itself, and drops out of every plan there will ever be, which is the exact failure the backfill was added to prevent surviving in the case where the frontier moved first. The pages from the cursor up to the frontier are therefore candidates on their own: a direction's sequence numbers are contiguous, so content observed at the frontier is proof that every position below it was written, and a hole under it is genuinely missing rather than merely not-here-yet.

- **2026-07-29 — the collector holds the probe frontier and *borrows* the contiguous cursor from the acknowledgement; it does not keep a second one.** § v6 erasure F1 requires the two pointers to be distinct, and the obvious build of that is two fields. It is the wrong one: `AckState` already carries a contiguous prefix plus a bounded set of settled positions beyond it, with the monotonicity, the run cap and the prefix-advance-on-give-up rule, and a second cursor beside it would be a second answer to the same question — free to disagree with the one that goes on the wire, and with no oracle anywhere to say which was right. `dm::collect::Collection` therefore owns an `AckState` and exposes it read-only; every mutation goes through `collected` / `abandoned`. The distinctness F1 demands is preserved exactly where the finding puts it — the **frontier is a page number advanced by observing content**, never a function of any sequence number, so `position_of` is used only on the cursor side. Two build-added rules the frozen text does not state: the frontier **never regresses** (sweeps complete out of order, and a late sweep of an older page pulling it back would oscillate the probe plan), and a fold that meets a slot outside the record **rejects the whole page and records nothing** (such a page was opened under a shape that disagrees with `PAGE_SLOTS`, so every position it reports is about some other page). The `settled`-not-*collected* caveat of `dm::ack` travels with the borrowed cursor unchanged, and is restated at the collector's own surface rather than left one indirection away.

- **2026-07-30 — the steady-state ratchet is not persisted at all; a restart tears an established channel down loudly. This supersedes the two 2026-07-28 notes above about the persisted ratchet state and the store's erase-in-place obligation, whose subject no longer exists.** The earlier note read the three restart-persistence requirements as implying a running ratchet and concluded the build must persist a superset of them. Re-reading them, none does: **§ v4 (crypto/erasure 4.1)** scopes `{ss0, AR, RK, PK_pc_A}` explicitly to *"so a restart mid-handshake doesn't strand the channel"*; **§ v5 (crypto V4-2)** scopes `{ss0, A's opening ephemeral DK}` to *"until ACCEPT is processed"*; and **§ v5 (erasure caveat)** is a page cursor justified on avoiding a from-0 rescan, not on correctness. Two are the pre-establishment window and the third is a page number.

  Persisting the running ratchet was also in tension with this document's own § v5, where the chains **delete on use**: a chain written down at rest is a chain that was not deleted, which is the forward secrecy the ratchet exists to provide. And the surrounding design already accepts the loss — hard give-up at 7 days, *"No progress in 7 days → both abandon (teardown)"*, *"Residual is missed backlog (accepted best-effort/PFS trade), not channel loss"*.

  So the defect this closes was never that a restart loses the conversation. It was that the conversation died **silently**: both ends returned at generation zero, every frame failed `UnknownEphemeral` permanently, the ratchet reported no losses, and neither the peer nor the local user was told. **What the build ships instead:** a **provisional handshake record** — `{ss0, eph_ek, eph_dk}` plus a binding tag over `ss0` and the ephemeral, `AR`/`chan_id`/`RK` recomputed from `ss0` rather than stored, sealed under `daemonseed/dm/provisional/seal/v1` from the profile's at-rest material and bound as AAD to the channel it belongs to (`daemonseed/dm/provisional/aad/v1 ‖ lp(recipient_keyrec_addr) ‖ lp(fc_epoch)`, the same scoping `daemonseed/dm/fc/aad/v1` uses), one fixed size, overwritten in place on establishment; the **receive page cursor**, bounded forwards by what the receiver has actually read; and a **loud teardown** through the existing ISC-C28 trust-event taxonomy (`dm-channel-torn-down-on-restart`, `dm-provisional-handshake-lost`, `dm-provisional-record-unreadable`, all persistent-non-blocking and all audit-logged, which ISC-A-C12 forbids suppressing).

  **No amendment to the frozen construction.** Steady-state ratchet persistence was never in it, so removing it changes nothing above; the three requirements that *are* in it are met — §4.1 and §V4-2 by the record (its recipient half, `PK_pc_A`, being contact-cache state per ISC-C44 rather than record state), §517 by the cursor. **Honest scope on the erasure the record asks for:** overwriting in place is a filesystem-API promise only — an SSD's FTL remaps the write and a copy-on-write filesystem allocates a new extent, so an adversary holding the raw flash is outside what any store here can deliver. That is a narrower and truthful version of the obligation the superseded note stated as absolute.

- **2026-07-30 — the re-seed schedule is a rung ladder whose last rung repeats, and the give-up clock starts at compose. Neither is stated outright above; both are forced by the frozen text's own arithmetic.** § Task 2 gives the schedule as `1 min → 2 → 4 → 8 → 16 → 32 → 64 → hourly → daily` and prices it at *"~20 writes over a week vs ~5,000 at the operator's flat 120 s"*, without saying whether "hourly" and "daily" are rungs or phases. They are rungs. The flat figure checks out exactly (seven days at 120 s is 5,040), and under the rung reading a pending message spends 127 minutes on its seven geometric rungs, an hour on the eighth, then a day per emission to the give-up: 7 + 1 + 6 ≈ 14 re-seeds plus the first send, which is the neighbourhood of "~20". Reading them as phases gives ~35, on the wrong side of it. **The build keeps the ladder literal rather than fitting a curve to it, because it is not a curve:** rung 6 is 64 minutes and rung 7, "hourly", is 60 — a four-minute *decrease* that any capped doubling would smooth away. **The give-up origin is settled by M14, not by taste.** § Task 2 says only "hard give-up at 7 days", but M14 reads *"`awaiting-key` gives up at 7 days"*, and an `awaiting-key` message has had no emission by definition — its recipient's key record is unfetchable, so nothing is sealed and nothing is published. A clock anchored at first emission would never start for exactly the messages M14 is about, and those entries would sit pending forever. Compose is the only origin under which the frozen text is consistent with itself.

- **2026-07-30 — the outbox's third state is *confirmed-collected*, not *presumed-delivered*, and *peer-reachable* is not a state at all.** § Task 2's four states were written before the adversarial review and define the third as "stopped on presence / establishment" — which **B5** refuted as a BLOCKER and **M16** showed replayable, and which § D-DELIV replaced with "default not-delivered; confirm only on a genuine ack". ISC-C39's re-cut carries the replacement; § Task 2's sentence was never revised to match, so the two read as a contradiction they are not. Once the transition is ack-gated there is no presumption left in it, so the build uses the ack's own word, and it takes **no presence input at all** — B5 made structural rather than remembered. #235's third UI state maps onto it directly; #235's *peer-reachable* is an annotation a UI draws from the presence layer over an on-DHT message, which is exactly where B5 leaves presence: informative, never load-bearing. Recorded in `ISA.md` `## Decisions`.

- **2026-07-30 — prefix-advance-on-give-up needs a clock as well as a terminal state, because a give-up is swept.** The 2026-07-28 note above hands the outbox slice the rule that *undelivered* is terminal and a given-up message is never re-consulted against an ack. That alone is not sufficient. A give-up is a swept transition, so between the seventh day and the next sweep an entry is past its window and still awaiting collection — precisely the window in which the receiver may legitimately advance its prefix past that position — and whether the message reads as collected then depends on which of two unrelated calls a caller happened to make first. So confirmation is refused for any entry past the give-up window, swept or not. The residual left is a peer that abandons a position early: `collect` and `abandon` are identical bytes on the wire, so the ack cannot distinguish them, and this is the already-recorded residual that a peer "lying about its own collection" is unpreventable — bounded by `merge_peer_ack`'s ceiling to sequences actually sent, and costing only the liar, who is the intended recipient.

- **2026-07-30 — the give-up clock is the one number in the outbox an unsealed file must not be believed about, and the fix is to refuse the record rather than repair it.** The give-up is what makes *"marked undelivered in the UI, never silently abandoned"* true, and it is computed from a single stored timestamp. A `composed_at_ms` in the future switches it off permanently and invisibly — `is_given_up` answers false at every clock the user will ever see, so the entry never gives up, never sweeps, never surfaces, and re-seeds until something else stops it. The reflex fix, clamping to the caller's clock, is the more dangerous of the two: it rewrites persisted state from one unverified reading, so a boot with a dead RTC ten days slow rewrites every live entry, the next write persists it, and the moment the clock corrects the whole file is past its window and given up — every pending message reported failed, from a fault that had already passed. **Refusal is the only non-destructive option**, and the "but refusing discards the other pending messages" objection does not apply to it: nothing is written, the caller keeps the bytes, and a later decode with a good clock returns the record intact. The asymmetry is worth naming for anything else that persists a clock here: **a value we cannot verify may bound what we do, but must never overwrite what we were told.** `next_due_ms` needs no such guard, because the give-up does not read it — a corrupt one costs emissions, not the guarantee.

- **2026-09-04 — `chan_id_{n+1}` IS consumed; the "derived but not yet consumed" paragraph of the `DM_REESTAB_CHAN_ID` note (§ *Decision — scrub every record before unlinking it*, at `:1491`) describes a state the build has left.** That paragraph records that nothing reads the value. It is no longer true, and the line is left exactly as written because these notes are append-only. It sits BELOW this one in the file — the build-notes section is append-only in its own right and predates the decision sections, so a correction to a later note is written here rather than beside it. What reads it: the driver's re-establishment fold takes the identifier from the commit that produces it — `ResumeRecord::commit_reestablished` on the initiating side and `ResumeRecord::accept_peer_initiation` on the answering one, each returning it so the commit and the identifier cannot be separated — and holds it beside the resumed channel's ratchet as session-lifetime state, which is the disposition `:670` requires. The obligation the older note records is therefore discharged for the resumed channel's identity and NOT for its frames: no frame is sealed on a resumed channel yet, because a channel frame binds the sealer's own `PK_pc` and no record at rest holds it. When that path is built it binds this value.

- **2026-09-06 — a party's own `PK_pc`/`S_pc` IS read back at load; the note above and the field-list notes at `:514` and `:1070` no longer describe the build, and the *frames* obligation the note above records is still open for a different reason.** The note above says no record at rest holds the sealer's own `PK_pc`. The A4.8 and A9.2 notes of 2026-09-05 put both halves in the resume record and the provisional record, and the driver now reads them: `seed_from_store` restores the pair from the resume record when it rebuilds a correspondence, and `rearm_handshake` restores it from the provisional record where a first-contact entry is still awaiting its acceptance. So an initiator that restarts inside that window collects its acceptance and composes ordinary frames under the pseudonym its own entry published, which its correspondent verifies against the copy it recorded.

  What is still unbuilt is the note above's actual subject, the **resumed** channel's frames — and the keys are not what is missing. Two things are. Each side derives the resumed ratchet's clear generation from its **own** outbox's `last_clear_gen`, and those differ whenever the two sides have cleared to different generations, so the initiating side seals at a generation the answering side has already moved past and the frame is refused as `GenerationTooOld`. And `Ratchet::reestablished` opens a **sending** chain on the initiating side and a **receiving** chain on the answering side, so the answering side has nothing to seal with in either case. Agreeing one generation for both sides — the settling leg is the only frame that could carry it — is a construction question and is left open here rather than settled in a build note. Until it is answered the driver **refuses** the send rather than composing one: a correspondence whose channel a completed re-establishment opened is marked resumed, and `DmMachine::send` declines it with `NotEstablishedThisSession` before the outbox is asked and before the ratchet steps. Refusing at the command is what keeps the failure legible — a frame sealed under a generation the correspondent has moved past is discarded in silence and reaches the user only at the seven-day give-up, and on the answering side, whose resumed ratchet carries a receiving chain only, it fails inside the seal and is reported as a seal fault instead of as an unbuilt path.

- **2026-09-07 — the resumed channel's clear generation is agreed inside the sealed legs, and A8.2's premise that re-establishment restarts the numbering is struck (ratified 2026-09-07).** The note above leaves two things open and this one closes both, on the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-07. **The generation is agreed, not derived twice.** Each side's floor is its own last persisted clear generation, and the two differ whenever the sides have cleared to different generations, so no per-side derivation can produce one number: the `RE-ACK`'s signed payload carries the answering party's floor, the initiating party computes `agreed = max(own floor, peer floor) + 1`, and the `RE-CONFIRM`'s signed payload names it. Both fields sit inside the payload the leg's `s_pc` signature covers and inside the leg's AEAD, so each value is authenticated by the sender's per-correspondent key and is unreadable to anything that cannot already open the leg. **Nothing changes in the clear.** A3.9 says a leg is shaped exactly like an ordinary frame and identified by trial decryption; the fields ride inside the existing padding, `LEG_LEN` is unchanged, and all three legs remain one length, so the wire carries no new discriminator and A3.9's sizing is untouched. **The resumed ratchet opens at the agreed value on both sides**, which continues the counter rather than restarting it, exactly as A3.9 requires and as the 2026-08-14 note requires of send `seq`; each side still refuses a value at or below its own floor, so a forged or replayed settling leg cannot pull either counter backwards. **The answering party's floor for the agreement is the value it advertised, pinned at the moment it answers**, not wherever its counter has since got to: the old chain stays usable for as long as the exchange takes, so a message sent on it raises that party's clear counter above the number its correspondent was given and told to compute from, and judging the settlement against the later value would refuse the correct answer and leave the two ends on different chains. The initiating party needs no such pin, and has nowhere to put one: it reads its own counter, agrees the generation and commits the exchange in a single step, so there is no window for the number to move under it. Reopening the agreed number is legal against that party's own later frames because A8.2 scopes the rollback high-water per `chan_id` and the resumed channel's identifier is a new one. **One chain per side stands.** The initiating party owns the first chain because its `RE-CONFIRM` is the first frame under the re-rooted root, and the answering party steps off that frame; no ephemeral rides the settling leg, and the asymmetry A3.2 describes is unchanged. **A3.2's word *"empty-bodied"* for the `RE-CONFIRM` is struck**: the settling leg's body holds the agreed generation, inside the same padding, so what A3.2 was requiring — one length for all three legs — is untouched.

  **A8.2's premise is struck, on the same terms the 2026-08-14 note struck A9.2's parenthetical.** A8.2 scopes the `(gen,seq)` rollback high-water per `chan_id` and justifies it with *"a legitimate re-establishment restarts generation numbering under a new `chan_id`"*. The first half of that sentence is false and now doubly so: A3.9 already had the clear counter continue, the 2026-08-14 note said the same of send `seq`, and the construction above makes continuity the thing the two sides go to the trouble of agreeing on. Numbering does not restart. **The scoping itself stands as built and no code changes**, and its grounding is owed rather than supplied here: what the per-`chan_id` scoping buys once numbering continues is not the false rollback A8.2 named, which cannot occur, and this note does not re-derive what it is instead. The rule is retained as built. What is withdrawn is the justification, not the rule — and that is the more dangerous of the two to leave standing, because a reader checking the stated reason finds it false and concludes the scoping is unnecessary.

  **Rejected, and why each is worse.** A one-way carry — the initiating party naming a generation off its own floor alone, as the build did before this — is wrong exactly when the answering party's floor is higher, which is the ordinary case after the answering side has sent more than it has received: the initiator seals at a generation its peer has already moved past and every frame is refused as `GenerationTooOld`. A stride derived from `reconnect_gen`, computed identically on both sides with nothing carried, avoids the exchange but reintroduces the marker A3.9 spent its residual budget removing: a generation that jumps by a fixed amount at every re-establishment is a restart signature legible to a page co-host from the clear counter alone.

  **The send path on a resumed channel, and what one side still cannot do.** The initiating party composes an ordinary frame at the agreed generation the moment the exchange completes, and the earlier note's blanket refusal is struck. The answering party cannot, and the reason is A3.2's asymmetry rather than anything about the generation: it holds a receiving chain until the initiating party's first frame under the re-rooted root publishes the ephemeral its own chain steps off. A send before that frame is **refused at the command**, before the outbox is asked and before the ratchet steps, under a reason that names exactly that state — the correspondent has not yet written — so nothing is spent and the same message can be sent again. It is never reported as a failure to seal, which is what asking the ratchet to mint first would produce.

  **Not built: queuing that message unsealed and sealing it when the chain opens.** The outbox already carries entries that reserve a sequence and hold no frame, and the shape is right — the message would take its position now and be sealed once, at that position, on the first pass after the correspondent's frame lands. What is missing is that such an entry deliberately holds **no plaintext**, and no pass composes a frame outside the send command, so the path needs a store for the composed body and a sealing site that does not exist. Recorded here rather than built, because the refusal above is legible and the missing pieces are outbox construction rather than a gap in this amendment.

- **2026-09-08 — a settlement the answering party refuses is an alpha residual: the initiating party believes the channel resumed until `T_RETIRE` (accepted for alpha on the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-08).** The note above agrees the resumed channel's clear generation inside the sealed legs and has each side refuse a value at or below its own floor. That refusal has nowhere to go. The `RE-CONFIRM` is the settling leg and the last of the three; nothing answers it, and the initiating party reads its own counter, agrees the generation and commits the exchange in a single step before it emits, as commit-then-emit requires. So when the answering party refuses the settling leg the initiating party learns nothing on the wire: it holds a channel it believes resumed, composes ordinary frames at the agreed generation, and the answering party discards each of them under a chain it never opened. **What closes the gap is the retirement horizon and only that.** Absent a confirming observation (A3.6) the initiating party's `T_RETIRE` fires from its own commit, `RS_n` retires, and the unconfirmed re-establishment surfaces loud (A3.8) — so the state is bounded and eventually visible rather than terminal-and-silent, which is the property A3.16 requires and the one this residual does not break. What it costs is the interval: for up to `T_RETIRE`, every message sent on that channel reaches its own give-up instead of the correspondent, and the user sees undelivered mail before it sees the reason. **Nothing is built to close that interval for alpha, and nothing is promised here.** A later fix needs one of two things this amendment does not have — a refusal signal the answering party can send, which is a fourth leg and reopens A3.9's sizing argument, that holding only while all three legs are one length; or a bounded confirmation wait, in which the initiating party holds the exchange open until a frame under the re-rooted root confirms it, which makes one party's commit depend on the other party's return and reopens what A4.1 settled about recovery not depending on the other's clock. Both are future work and neither is chosen here.
- **2026-09-10 — a message composed while no chain exists keeps the sequence it reserved, the resumed chain opens at the lowest such sequence, and a resumed receive chain takes its base from the first frame's header (ratified 2026-09-10).** Every outbound message takes its sequence number when it is composed, and that number is permanent: it is the address of the page slot the message is published to, the key of its outbox entry, and the position its acknowledgement settles. A message composed while the correspondence holds no key schedule, which is the state every established correspondence is in after a restart, reserves its number at once and holds no frame; it is the standing cause that re-establishes its own channel. The exchange that re-establishes the channel is three legs, and two of them ride the same outbox in the same direction as the waiting message, each at its own sequence position. As built, the resumed chain opened one past the settling leg, the key schedule refused to derive a key for any position below its first, and so the message that caused the exchange could never be sent (issue #444). This note settles the contract.

  **The frame takes the sequence reserved at compose.** Nothing is re-sequenced and nothing is left behind: the reservation becomes the frame's own entry through the outbox's one frame-installing edge, at the same number, once a key schedule exists. With several messages waiting, each takes its own reserved number, in order; a message composed between an in-flight exchange's legs sits between them and is sealed there. The spent-at-compose rule stands unamended.

  **The resumed sending chain opens at the lowest sequence still awaiting a key, and steps over every position the outbox already holds.** The party that asked for the re-establishment opens its chain at that number rather than one past its settling leg; the answering party's sending chain, which opens on the first frame it receives under the re-rooted root, starts at that party's own lowest waiting number in the same way. When either chain reaches a position the outbox has already spent on a leg, it steps over it, minting and discarding that position's key, and the outbox, not the chain, is the authority on which positions are spent. This moves the authority for a resumed channel's next sequence from the key schedule to the outbox, on both sides, and it needs a sending-side step the key schedule does not have today: a sending chain seals at its own counter and advances by one, the counter has no other writer, and two debug assertions forbid a sending chain that skips; the contract adds one operation that advances the counter to a named position, minting and discarding the keys in between, and relaxes those assertions to admit it. Without that step the first send after the waiting messages asks the outbox for the position the first leg holds, is refused as a duplicate before the key schedule moves, and every later send on that correspondence is refused the same way for ever. Where nothing is waiting, both chains open exactly where they do today.

  **Each waiting message is admitted before its key is minted, and a message the exchange outran is ended, not sealed.** A reservation is admitted at no cost and holds no bytes, so the outbox can be full, or the reservation past its seven-day window, by the time a key schedule exists; a key minted for a message the outbox then refuses is spent for nothing, and the message would sit at a consumed position until its give-up, which is the failure the outbox's second capacity gate exists to prevent. So the pass that seals waiting messages takes them in ascending order and, for each, first ends it undelivered and surfaced if its window has closed, then asks the outbox whether a worst-case frame fits at that position and stops the whole batch at the first refusal, and only then mints, seals, installs and re-schedules. Reservations also count against the record's capacity at compose, cumulatively at the worst-case frame, so a message that could not be sealed later is refused when it is composed, with the record full as the reason; that bounds waiting messages per correspondence at about a hundred.

  **A resumed receive chain takes its base from the first frame's clear header.** Every other receive chain in this design is already positioned that way: a frame under a new generation names the first sequence of its chain in the clear, the receiver opens the chain there, walks at most a bounded distance to the frame's own position, and commits nothing until the frame's authenticated encryption opens. The one chain that was positioned differently, the answering party's chain for the resumed root, still opens where it did, one past the settling leg's position, which is the right base when nothing is waiting; and on the first frame it receives at that chain's own generation, while it has opened nothing, it takes the frame's stated base as its own, walks under the same bound, and commits after the frame opens, as the new-generation path does. That is the only place the re-base can live: a chain deferred until the first frame would demand the encapsulation a new generation carries, and a resumed root's first frame carries none. The initiating party's receive chain already learns the answering party's base this way and is unchanged. No leg carries anything new; the legs' length and shape are untouched; nothing in the clear changes.

  **What the receiver sees, in numbers.** For the life of the exchange the waiting positions are unwritten below two settled leg positions: the receiver's contiguous cursor waits below them, its acknowledgement carries that prefix plus one run over the legs, and the sender reads the legs as settled through that run, which is what the confirming observation already reads. The first dispatch of the exchange is drawn from the reconnect band, so this interval is hours by design. When a waiting message is published, the receiver's watch collects it: a page holds sixteen positions, so the waiting position shares the legs' page in fifteen cases of sixteen and that page is already watched; in the sixteenth it sits on the page before, which the receiver's backfill re-reads every 30 s, oldest hole first. Either way the message lands at exchange completion plus at most one probe interval. If the exchange never completes, the waiting messages give up seven days from compose and the receiver abandons their positions a day later: the fate any undelivered message already has. No new frame signals a hole, so the byte-novelty rule is not engaged. A receiver that opens a later frame before a waiting one walks the gap between them and keeps at most sixty-four skipped keys, as it does for any batch a sender wrote while the receiver was away; the waiting batch inherits that ordinary loss bound and no stronger promise.

  **The settlement is re-jittered, because the waiting messages fall due together.** A reservation's re-seed schedule starts at compose and nothing advances it while no key schedule exists, so at the first pass after the exchange settles every waiting message is due at once, beside the settling leg: with k waiting, k+1 writes to one record in one pass, k of them legible as a burst to a co-host of that record. Each is a standing cause, so this is not an emission the exchange caused, but it is a synchronised first dispatch of the kind the reconnect emissions were jittered to avoid. So the pass that seals a waiting message also places it on the first rung of the ordinary ladder with the ordinary jitter, and the burst spreads as any first dispatch does. Re-sequencing the messages instead would have paid this identically.

  **Write-once and rollback are unchanged.** Every position is written once. A wholesale rollback of the sender to before a reservation composes at that number again under a new generation and a new channel identifier, and is caught or missed exactly as a rollback is today. A co-host can only roll a page value back to bytes it captured; a reserved position held no bytes until its frame, and a captured leg re-presented at its own position is byte-identical to one already processed and changes nothing. The re-base above reads an unauthenticated header, for bounded work only and never past the point where the frame fails to open; that is the trust the receive path already places in the same field at every generation switch.

  **The trigger and the sealing site.** A send to a correspondence that holds a resume record and no key schedule reserves the outbox's next sequence, holds the body in this process's memory keyed by correspondence and sequence, reports the message composed at that sequence, and arms the pass that surveys the outbox and opens the exchange on that entry's own schedule; until now that send was refused, and the survey, finding nothing waiting, opened nothing, so no re-establishment was ever initiated in a running build. At load, a reservation whose body this process does not hold is ended undelivered and surfaced, loudly, before the survey runs, so it opens no exchange and a restart triggers no emission. When a key schedule is installed, on the initiating side as the exchange settles and on the answering side as the first received frame opens its sending chain, every waiting entry is taken in ascending order and, under the admission rule above, sealed under it, installed at its own sequence, and placed on the first ladder rung with jitter, after which it re-seeds like any other entry; positions the outbox already holds are stepped over.

  **The delivery report does not change.** A message composed while no chain exists is reported composed at its sequence once, and every later report for it names the same sequence. A client that pairs a body to the first report naming a new sequence is therefore right, and the separate defect that such pairing is a heuristic (issue #449) is neither fixed nor required here.

  **What this supersedes.** The build's rule that the resumed chain opens one past the settling leg, and the answering party's rule that its chain's base is the settling leg's position plus one. The 2026-09-07 note's paragraph headed *Not built: queuing that message unsealed* is built by this, on the shape that paragraph describes. The cause listed second in A4.2's standing causes is narrowed to a running process: across a restart the reservation survives and the body does not, so the entry is ended at load rather than dispatched. The counters-continue rule of A3.9 is untouched, since every number here is at or above every number previously written. The spent-at-compose rule of A4.8 stands, and the ratified home of the unsealed body of 2026-09-09 stands, its key being stable precisely because the sequence is.

  **Residuals accepted.** A page slot written after higher slots on the same record is visible to a page co-host as a backfilled slot; accepted in the F1 family on the terms of the 2026-09-08 note, since the same observer already sees the two legs and learns nothing further. Every restart while a message is waiting is an undelivered message and a permanent hole, paid at the receiver's abandonment horizon; that is the cost of the body's memory-only home, recorded there, not of this contract, and the lever is that ruling's second option. The two positions a sending chain steps over leave two never-used keys in the receiver's bounded skipped-key cache until eviction, and such keys are the first evicted at capacity. The authority for a resumed channel's next sequence moves from the key schedule to the outbox on every such send, on both sides; that is a real change in who decides, accepted because the outbox is the record that survives a restart and the key schedule is not. The waiting batch carries the ordinary out-of-order loss bound and no stronger one. Waiting messages per correspondence are bounded at about a hundred by the record's capacity, refused at compose beyond that. A waiting message lands at most one probe interval after the exchange completes.

  **Rejected, and why each is worse.** Re-sequencing the waiting message to the chain's first position keeps every leg where it is and every ratchet rule as built, and manufactures a permanent hole in a delivered conversation on every re-establishment with mail waiting: the receiver's cursor waits below it for eight days, its backfill re-reads that page every 30 s for the duration, every later position confirms only through the gap bitmap, and one client re-establishing repeatedly can leave one run per event in a bitmap capped at 64 runs that refuses rather than truncates. It also re-emits the composed report under a second number, which the client's pairing cannot follow without a token this design does not yet carry. Carrying the base inside the settling leg's sealed payload opens the chain at the right number and costs a leg payload field, while leaving the two directions learning the same fact two different ways, since the answering party's own base already reaches the initiating party through the header.

  **Verification this owes.** A test that composes one, two and several messages while no chain exists, one of them between an in-flight exchange's legs, runs the exchange, finds every in-window message sealed at its reserved sequence, and then sends once more and finds that send sealed one past the settling leg, with a control that opens the chain where the build did and fails. A test that fills the record to one frame short and finds the batch stop with the last message still waiting and no key minted for it; a test that ages one reservation past its window and finds it ended undelivered, unminted; a test that the hundred-and-eighth reservation is refused at compose. A key-schedule test that the sending step advances the counter to a named position, discarding the keys between, and that the receiver walks that gap and opens the next frame. A ratchet test that re-bases an untouched chain from a header, refuses to re-base one that has consumed, and refuses a base beyond the bound with no state change. An outbox test that k messages sealed at one settlement carry k distinct due times. A collection test that shows the prefix, the single run and the backfill page while the positions wait, and their release when the message lands. The two-node oracle with its premise corrected, the sends issued before the wait. A client test that one composed report at one sequence attaches one body once.

## Amendment A1 — channel re-establishment after a restart (~~PENDING ratification~~ **SUPERSEDED by A2, refuted on all three axes**)

> **Withdrawn 2026-07-30, before ratification.** A confirming adversarial review refuted this construction on all three axes: it omits `msg_sig` from a new frame kind against the doctrine at `dm/frame.rs:36-42` (a frame sealed under a shared key proves *one of the two parties* wrote it, never which) and then decides a tiebreak and triggers a teardown on that frame; Clause 1's healing claim is false, because an at-rest compromise can reconnect first, self-renew its own authority, and lock the rightful holder out permanently; the responder→initiator leg is never specified at all, which would put request and response under one key (F9's shape); Clause 1 has no commit point, so ordinary frame loss desynchronizes `RS` terminally and silently; and the frame is a clear length-visible restart marker (fresh `eph_ek`, absent `eph_ct` — the opening-burst shape the code documents as distinguishable regardless of padding bucket) whose startup burst links N unlinkable records to one device with no jitter. **Kept as the record A2 builds on, not deleted.**

**This is a construction amendment, not a build note.** The build-notes section above is explicitly notation-and-wording only; this changes the construction, so it is recorded separately per the status footer's "any change now is a deliberate, reviewed amendment." It is **not in force until the maintainer ratifies.** Validated 2026-07-30 by a review on three axes (crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, erasure/availability) run as adversarial *validation across competing options*, not as an attack on one proposal.

### The gap this closes

`ss0` is deleted at establishment (the F1 fix, § D-PFS) and — per the #243 scope decision — the steady-state ratchet is not persisted either. So a restart tears down every open channel and **v6 defines no resumption path.** #262 surfaced the conflict from the other side: build slice #236's scope text has the contact cache retaining `ss0`, which `§ D-PFS` forbids ("only `AR` is retained"). #236's scope line is the error; the design is not open on that point, and retaining `ss0` is not a live option — it regenerates `RK0` and with it every message key the ratchet believes it deleted.

### What is retained at rest, exactly

Per correspondent, under the profile seal: **`AR`** (already retained by v6), **`RS_n`** (new, below), the **`reconnect_gen` counter `n`**, a non-secret **`previously_established`** marker, and the correspondent's long-term public key plus cached EK. **Deleted:** `ss0`, `chan_id`, and all ratchet state.

### The construction

**A fourth sibling.** At establishment, alongside `AR` and `chan_id` from `HKDF-Extract(DM_ROOT_SALT, ss0)` and `RK0` from the same PRK, derive `RS_0 = HKDF-Expand(PRK, DM_REESTAB_ROOT)` — a fourth Expand sibling under a new label, at `ROOT_LEN`. It is a sibling and not a chain for the same reason the other three are: the retained value must never yield the deleted one.

**The re-establish frame rides the existing channel plane.** It is written to the next page slot of the initiator's sending direction on the `AR`-derived paging plane — the record whose write authority only the two parties hold. **No doorbell write, no key-record fetch, no admission interaction, no proof-of-work.** The peer finds it by the sweep it already performs on cached channel records; v6's "the doorbell is not consulted after first contact" is preserved rather than weakened.

**Sealing.** `reestab_prk = HKDF-Extract(DM_REESTAB_SALT, RS_n)`; seal key `K = HKDF-Expand(reestab_prk, DM_REESTAB_SEAL ‖ lp(u64-BE(n+1)))`. The AAD follows `firstcontact::seal_aad`'s prefix-then-`push_lp` shape and binds the recipient's key-record address, a commitment to `AR` (a hash, never `AR` itself), the direction, and `n+1`. Nonce per the global rule — fresh random per seal, never derived from the counter.

**The new root.** The responder encapsulates to the fresh ephemeral ML-KEM-1024 key carried in the frame, yielding `ss_new`, and both parties compute `RK_0' = advance_root(RS_n, ss_new)` using the existing function unchanged — previous root as extraction salt, fresh secret as IKM. This is what makes a holder of `RS_n` alone unable to read the resumed channel: `advance_root`'s own contract is that "one who holds the old root cannot compute it without the new secret."

### Clause 1 (MANDATORY) — `RS` ratchets on every successful reconnect

`RS_{n+1} = HKDF-Expand(HKDF-Extract(DM_REESTAB_SALT, RS_n ‖ ss_new), DM_REESTAB_NEXT)`, computed by both parties (both hold `RS_n`; the initiator decapsulates `ss_new`, the responder encapsulated it), with `RS_n` zeroized and its at-rest slot overwritten in place.

Without this clause `RS` is a **static, immortal at-rest credential** granting channel-resume authority for the life of the relationship, which nullifies D-PFS's post-compromise healing against a single profile-seal compromise — and a static `RS` cannot be rotated from a deleted `ss0`, so there would be no later opportunity to fix it. The erasure review judged the amendment refuted without this clause; it is not optional.

### Clause 2 (MANDATORY) — a strictly-increasing `reconnect_gen`, bound and checked

`n` is bound into both the seal-key derivation info and the frame AAD (above), and a frame carrying `gen ≤ n_stored` is **rejected**, not merely ignored. Without it, a fixed `RS` reused as extraction salt lets a replayed re-establish frame re-derive an already-consumed root — message-key reuse and a ratchet rollback.

Clause 1 largely subsumes this: a ratcheting `RS` is monotonic by construction, so a replayed frame's key no longer derives at all. The counter is retained as the **explicit, checkable** form, because "the derivation happens to fail" is not an enforcement a reviewer can verify and a future refactor could restore a static salt without tripping anything.

### Clause 3 (MANDATORY) — a re-establish implies immediate-Undelivered for the dead chain

A peer's restart deletes their receive chain, so frames already published under the previous chain are **permanently undecryptable** even though `AR` keeps their addresses valid. On a successful re-establish, every outbox entry sealed under the previous chain is marked **Undelivered immediately**, re-seeding stops, and the UI surfaces them. They are **not** re-sealed: § m7 forbids a second distinct ciphertext for one logical message, and the outbox has one frame-installing edge with no edge back.

Without this rule the sender re-seeds dead frames for the full seven days and reports them as in flight — the same misinformed-UI defect #261 exists to prevent, reached by a different road. The amendment's advantage over #261's situation is that its teardown signal is **authenticated**, where a doorbell knock is a world-writable one.

### Simultaneous mutual re-establish

Both parties may restart before either reconnects, and each then mints its own ephemeral, so the two candidate `ss_new` values differ. Deterministic tiebreak, no extra round trip: on observing a competing re-establish at the same `gen`, **the frame whose `eph_ek` has the lexicographically smaller SHA-384 digest wins**; the loser adopts the winner's `RK_0'` and `RS_{n+1}` and discards its own. Both parties compute the same answer from data both hold. The tiebreak is on the *frames*, not on identity roles, because whichever party restarted must be able to initiate.

### Cold start is unchanged, deliberately

A reinstall from mnemonic loses `RS`, so genuine cold start and device recovery still go through the doorbell exactly as v6 specifies. The doorbell therefore remains a **once-per-relationship** surface, which preserves the basis on which its `dflt(32)` / 32-slot sizing was accepted ("established contacts leave the doorbell").

### The honest security property — what changes and what does not

§ D-PFS's statement stands unchanged: **content forward secrecy holds; address-linkability is not forward-secret.** This amendment adds one clause and no more: a compromise of at-rest state additionally yields **channel-resume authority until the next successful reconnect ratchets `RS`**. It does not yield past message keys (`RS` is an Expand sibling of the PRK, not the PRK) and does not passively yield the resumed channel's content (the resumed root requires the fresh KEM secret). Active impersonation is available to an at-rest compromise regardless, because the long-term identity key is also at rest. State exactly this — no overclaim.

### Why the alternatives were refused

Both were refuted on all three axes. The summary below is the durable record.

- **Reconnect via a fresh doorbell knock, with a previously-established marker bypassing admission** — refuted three times. It collides with the frozen verifier rule at § Crypto MINOR invariants (b): keep that rule and the reconnect is rejected as an idempotent re-accept, drop it and any doorbell reader replays a captured entry for up to two `FC_PERIOD`s to force ratchet resets on demand. **Both branches deny.** It also converts the doorbell — world-writable from the recipient's public key alone, and by design "best-effort under attack" — from a first-contact nuisance into the routine continuation path, so a wipe loop denies *all* DM to a target permanently rather than only new relationships, regressing the closed E9/e7-D7 finding. And it leaks a recurring per-correspondent restart-and-cadence beacon plus a contact count on the one world-readable record, where v6 pays that once per relationship ever. Finally it discards the retained `AR` on every reconnect (a fresh knock mints a fresh `ss0`), so two parties restarting before either reconnects split-brain with nothing to tie-break them.
- **No reconnect at all — a restart means a new conversation.** Viable and unchanged in cost, and it remains the honest fallback if this amendment is not ratified. Rejected on the ambition call (2026-07-30): a resumption requiring the correspondent to act is not acceptable, and under invite-only admission it requires a fresh one-time token issued out of band on every restart.

### Preconditions before this can be claimed, not merely built

- **#282** — the four sibling roots need known-answer tests pinning their bytes. The existing `the_three_roots_from_ss0_are_independent` asserts only pairwise distinctness: it passes for any distinct labels and would pass if `AR` were chained from `RK0`, so it cannot support this amendment's independence claim. The defect that matters is a copy-paste passing `DM_RATCHET_ROOT` into the `RS` expand, making `RS == RK0` and silently voiding the whole `ss0`-deletion story; only a KAT catches it.
- **#281** — there is no writer anywhere under `dm/`, so `ss0` erasure, `RS` retention and `RS` rotation are all unrealized until the store lands. Shared with the frozen design rather than introduced here, but Clause 1 is unenforceable without it.
- The new labels (`DM_REESTAB_ROOT`, `DM_REESTAB_SALT`, `DM_REESTAB_SEAL`, `DM_REESTAB_NEXT`) join `domain.rs`'s `ALL` for the enforced prefix-freeness test and `labels_are_byte_pinned`.

### Verification obligations the build owes

Each must be shown able to **fail**, not merely to pass.

1. KATs pinning all four sibling roots for a fixed `ss0` — proven by refactoring the fan-out into a chain and observing red.
2. `RS_{n+1}` differs from `RS_n` **and** depends on both `RS_n` and `ss_new`, two-sided, in the shape of the existing `advance_depends_on_both_inputs`.
3. A re-establish frame at `gen ≤ n_stored` is rejected.
4. After a reconnect, `RS_n`'s bytes are absent from the store's at-rest form and from the freed allocation (the existing zeroize-witness harness).
5. A re-establish marks every prior-chain outbox entry Undelivered immediately, and re-seeds none of them.
6. Two simultaneous re-establishes converge: both parties compute the same `RS_{n+1}`, the same `RK_0'`, and the same next address.

### On ratification

The ISC-C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25 family in `ISA.md` gains criteria for the three clauses and the six obligations above, and #262 closes citing this section. Until then #262 stays open and #236's contact-cache scope line stands corrected but unamended.

## Amendment A2 — channel re-establishment: the high-level map (supersedes A1; ~~PENDING ratification~~ **stress-tested and REFUTED in part — superseded by A3, below**)

> **Stress-tested 2026-07-30 by an adversarial review on four axes** (the three project axes plus implementability of the store requirements). **Refuted on three axes; SATISFIABLE-with-required-changes on the fourth.** Unlike A1 this is a *partial* refutation: M1, M8, M11's core statement and M2's two-leg structure survived every review that examined them, and M4's split, M6's premise and M5's trial-decryption choice were accepted in principle. **Do not build against this section.** The most serious finding is that M3's retirement condition is unreachable — nothing obliges the initiator to emit anything under the new root, so an idle initiator retains `RS_n` for the life of the install and any actor able to suppress one frame chooses the window length. Four of A2's own decisions also contradict each other pairwise. **The store question it was written to answer did get answered:** the minimum mechanism is one sealed fixed-size blob per correspondence per record kind behind a single atomic-replace helper — no journal, no index, ~30 lines. Numbering note: A2's `M1`–`M11` collide with this document's own metadata-residual numbering and A3 must renumber.

**Deliberately a map, not a wire specification.** A1 failed partly by specifying byte-level detail — a seal key, an AAD shape, a tiebreak — for a protocol whose *decisions* were unmade, and partly by resting on store semantics that do not exist. A2 settles the decisions that shape the store and **explicitly defers** the wire detail to A2-final, to be written once the store's commit semantics are real. The two are chicken-and-egg (2026-07-30): a store built without the end goal straightjackets the protocol, and a protocol written without the store invents storage semantics inside a crypto amendment. The map breaks the cycle in the right place.

The option A1 embodied — a fourth retained sibling of the `ss0` extraction, reconnect on the `AR`-derived plane rather than the doorbell — was validated at the option level against two alternatives and is **not reopened here**. Everything below is a construction decision.

### M1 — every re-establish frame carries `msg_sig`

Non-negotiable, and A1's central omission. Page owner-write authority is **symmetric**: both parties derive the owner seed for both directions, so a frame's presence in a direction's pages proves only that one of the two parties wrote it. `msg_sig` under the sender's pseudonym key is the sole authorship proof in the feature (`dm/frame.rs:36-42`). A re-establish frame decides a tiebreak and triggers a teardown; both are authorship-sensitive, so both legs are signed. This does **not** defend against an at-rest compromise (see M11) — it defends against anyone holding only the shared re-establish secret.

### M2 — the handshake is two legs, both named

`RE-EST` (initiator → peer, carrying a fresh ephemeral) and `RE-ACK` (peer → initiator, carrying the encapsulation to it). A1 named only the first, which left the initiator unable to reach the new root and would have put two plaintexts under one key. **Each leg gets its own domain label and its own derived key**; neither reuses the other's. The initiator's retry of `RE-EST` at a fixed generation must not produce a second plaintext under the first's key — so the derived key takes a retry-independent input and the seal takes a fresh nonce, per the global rule, whose key-uniqueness premise (`§ Nonce rule`) A2-final must show is actually met rather than cited.

### M3 — `RS` commits in two slots, never in place

The store holds `RS_n` **and** `RS_{n+1}` through the window between sending `RE-ACK` and observing traffic authenticated under the new root. `RS_n` is retired only on that observation. A1's immediate in-place erasure made ordinary frame loss terminal: the responder could accept only `n+1` while the initiator could produce only `n`, with no event and no clock.

**The honest price, stated rather than hidden:** one generation of resume authority persists longer than the ideal. That is the correct trade — a recoverable relationship beats a marginally smaller compromise window, and the alternative is a channel that dies silently on a dropped packet.

**This is the amendment's principal store requirement**, and it is why the map precedes the store: "hold two generations of a secret and atomically retire one on a confirming observation" is a storage capability, not a crypto detail.

### M4 — routine restart is silent; an unfollowable state is loud

The split A1 lacked. A reconnect that completes needs no user involvement — that is the whole point of the ambition call. But a party that observes a generation it cannot follow (a `gen` above its own `RS` can produce, an `RE-ACK` it cannot open, an `RS` absent or unreadable while `previously_established` is set) must **surface it**, with a `TrustEvent`, and offer an explicit recovery path. Silence in that state is the defect #243 exists to prevent, and A1 reintroduced it.

Recovery is a fresh first contact — which *does* need the correspondent to act, and that is acceptable here precisely because it is an anomaly path rather than the routine one. The ambition call rejected correspondent action on every restart, not on a genuine loss of state.

### M5 — a re-establish frame is shaped exactly like an ordinary frame

A1's frame carried a fresh `eph_ek` and no `eph_ct`, which is the opening-burst shape the code documents as "about 1571 bytes shorter than every later frame, so it is distinguishable **regardless of padding bucket**" (`dm/frame.rs:13-29`) — and those are clear fields outside the sealed, padded body. A recurring, timestamped, co-host-readable "this party restarted" is not acceptable on a plane whose whole premise is that pages are unlinkable.

**Decision: no clear discriminator exists.** The `eph_ct`-shaped field carries uniform filler (an ML-KEM ciphertext is indistinguishable from random of its length), and a receiver identifies a re-establish by **trial decryption** — it attempts the `RE-EST` key on a frame its chain cannot open. The cost is one AEAD attempt per otherwise-unopenable frame, which is bounded, and the benefit is that a reconnect is invisible to a page co-host. A2-final must state where the filler comes from and confirm it is not distinguishable by any other field.

### M6 — the reconnect burst is jittered

A device resuming N channels writes to N deliberately-unlinkable records; without spreading, co-hosting any two links them to one device and dates the restart. WB-3 I6 forbids stable cross-record phase relationships, and v6 already jitters the weaker `fc_epoch`-boundary analogue. The window is A2-final's; that there is one is settled here. Pre-A1 the baseline was zero — a restart put nothing on this plane — so this channel is created by the amendment and must be paid for by it.

### M7 — the tiebreak orders on long-term identity, not on grindable material

A1 ordered simultaneous reconnects on `SHA-384(eph_ek)`, which the initiator chooses freely and can regenerate cheaply: ~n keygens buys the 1/n-quantile digest, so the party willing to spend always wins and thereby chooses whose ephemeral roots the resumed channel.

**Decision: order on the two long-term identity public keys** — fixed, ungrindable, and known to both parties from first contact. The lower key's `RE-EST` wins; the higher party adopts it and abandons its own. This is only ever consulted when both parties restarted before either reconnected; a single restart has no contest.

### M8 — the re-establish root step gets its own domain label

A1 reached the new root through `advance_root`, which expands under `DM_RATCHET_STEP`. Reusing that label for a different purpose leaves domain separation resting on an argument about values (`RS_n` is not any `RK_n`) rather than on labels, against the module's own "one distinct label per purpose". The function may still be reused; the label must not be.

### M9 — the outbox needs three things this amendment obliges

A1's Clause 3 was not expressible in the record it governed. Settled here as record-format requirements:

1. **Each entry records the chain it was sealed under**, so "entries sealed under the previous chain" is a question an entry can answer, and entries composed *during* a reconnect are not swept spuriously.
2. **A distinct teardown cause for a re-established channel.** `channel_torn_down` currently *retains* `AwaitingCollection` entries under the existing causes, and its match is deliberately wildcard-free so a new cause fails to compile — so the amendment owes that variant explicitly.
3. **The give-up clock does not run while no channel exists.** It is wall-clock from compose and indifferent to channel state, so reconnect latency would otherwise be charged against a message's seven-day insurance window.

The disposition itself stands as A1 had it: on a completed re-establish, entries sealed under the dead chain are marked **Undelivered immediately** and are never re-sealed — a peer's restart destroyed their receive chain, so those frames are undecryptable however long they are re-seeded, and § m7 forbids a second ciphertext for one logical message.

### M10 — the re-establish frame re-seeds on the ordinary ladder

A single write into an eviction-only store with no TTL is the failure the keep-alive schedule exists to prevent; A1 wrote the frame once. `RE-EST` and `RE-ACK` re-seed like any other DM write, and the state machine advances on **received** frames only, never on an assumption that the peer has seen ours. A reconnect that never completes reaches a terminal, surfaced state rather than waiting forever.

### M11 — the honest security property, rewritten

A1 claimed at-rest compromise yields resume authority "until the next successful reconnect ratchets `RS`". **That is false and it is withdrawn.** An attacker holding at-rest state can reconnect first, and because it holds `RS_n` it computes `RS_{n+1}` too — its authority renews itself, and M1's signature does not help, because the pseudonym signing key is at rest as well.

State instead: **an at-rest compromise is total for the relationship and the ratchet does not heal it.** What `RS` rotation genuinely buys is narrower and still worth having — a *stale* copy is useless, so an old backup, a discarded device, or a snapshot from before the last reconnect confers nothing. Content forward secrecy for past messages is unaffected: `RS` is an Expand sibling of the PRK, not the PRK, and cannot regenerate `RK0`.

The new harm A1 introduced and A2 must answer is the **silent lockout** of the rightful holder by an attacker who reconnects first. M3 keeps the rightful holder alive through the window and M4 makes the unfollowable state loud with a recovery path. That is a mitigation, not a cure: a determined at-rest attacker still evicts the owner, and the owner then recovers by re-doing first contact. State exactly this — no overclaim.

### Requirements this map hands the store (Track 2's brief inherits these)

1. Two-generation retention of a secret with atomic retirement on a confirming observation.
2. Atomic co-commit of `RS_{n+1}` with `reconnect_gen` — a torn update between them is the terminal desync M3 exists to prevent.
3. Crash-safe replacement, not in-place overwrite. "Overwritten in place" is the claim #281 records as resting on a property nothing provides.
4. `unreadable` distinguishable from `absent`, since M4 branches on it and lowering a read error to "no record" reads as "start a new conversation".
5. Per-entry sealing-chain provenance in the outbox, plus the new teardown cause and the pausable give-up clock.
6. Retention of the peer's long-term identity key for M7's ordering, and of `previously_established` and `reconnect_gen`.
7. Erasure that is real: `RS_n` gone from the at-rest form *and* from freed allocations on retirement.

### Deferred to A2-final, after the store

Byte values for every new label; the two legs' exact AAD composition and whether it is reconstructed or carried; the nonce derivation and the argument that the global rule's key-uniqueness premise is met; the filler's provenance in M5 and a check that no other field distinguishes; the pad bucket; M6's jitter window; the retry ladder's parameters; and the wire form of the sealing-chain provenance in M9.

### What a stress test of this map should attack

Not byte layouts — decisions. Whether M3's two-slot window is the right length and whether "a confirming observation" is well-defined enough to be implementable; whether M4's loud state can be *induced* by an attacker as a denial or a social-engineering prompt; whether M5's trial decryption is a usable oracle for an attacker probing which key a client holds, and whether the filler is genuinely indistinguishable; whether M7's identity ordering leaks or biases anything; whether M9's give-up pause can be abused to hold a message alive indefinitely; whether M11's honest property is now complete or still undercounts; and whether the seven store requirements are together satisfiable without inventing a database.

## Amendment A3 — channel re-establishment: the revised map (supersedes A2; ~~PENDING ratification~~ ~~stress-tested and REFUTED in part — A4 pending~~ **superseded by A4, below**)

> **Stress-tested 2026-07-30 by an adversarial review on three axes (crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, erasure/availability). REFUTED; the spine holds, so an A4 revision follows — not a redesign.** The surviving core (retained sibling `RS`, reconnect on the `AR` plane, RE-CONFIRM-driven reachable retirement, the shared-secret coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped idempotent sweep, no-silent-content-regression) passed all three axes. Five clusters failed, ranked by fix depth: (1) **retirement availability** — A2's fatal retirement-timing class is closed against immortality but reopened against availability: a responder that commits `RS_{n+1}` then hits `T_RETIRE` before an absent initiator collects `RE-ACK` strands the channel terminally by asynchrony (B-CRYPTO-1); (2) **reconnect-burst metadata** — A3.10's decorrelation rests on jitter absent at first dispatch and an inapplicable oracle, the prioritized trickle adds a correspondent-ranking leak, and A3.4's idempotent re-serve is an active page-to-page confirmation oracle (B-META-1/2/3); (3) **the A3.9 residual is materially larger than the accepted sizing** — single-record discriminators plus a third correlated record make it a cross-plane join, not "weaker than F1"; the maintainer's `029ab72` acceptance is reopened on corrected facts (S1–S5); (4) **key collision** — `K(RS_n, gen, leg)` omits party/direction, sealing two plaintexts under one key in the contest case (B-CRYPTO-2, one KDF input); (5) **store gaps** — `next_send_seq` must stay in the outbox not the resume record (B-ERAS-1), the pseudonym signing key `S_pc` is un-enumerated making A3.1 unimplementable after restart (MAJOR-CRYPTO-4), and per-correspondence files leak at rest without the seal key (M-b). **Do not build against this section.**

> Authored independently of the A1 and A2 drafts, as a control on authorship bias — the known residual of the two refuted amendments. Input: this document plus the consolidated A2 findings. One pre-flight adversarial review was run against the draft before it landed and its thirteen findings folded in — the notable ones: the responder's commit was cross-record (resolved by single-authority derivation, A3.12/A3.14), the confirming-ack claim contradicted the frozen settled-not-collected doctrine (A3.6), the contest lacked a local-detection rule and an asymmetric-observation row (A3.7, A3.15), and the failed state had no exit (A3.8). Same scope discipline as A2: a high-level map that settles the decisions shaping the DM store and defers byte values (labels, AAD composition, pad buckets, exact constants) to a final pass written once the store exists. **A full stress round is still expected before ratification; this section is written to be attacked, not to look finished.** Decision IDs are `A3.1`–`A3.16` — a namespace nothing else in this document uses — replacing A2's `M1`–`M11`, which collided with the document's own metadata-residual numbering.

**Disposition of A2, decision by decision — the survivor record.** M1 survives as A3.1 with its open sub-question closed. M2's two-named-legs structure survives inside A3.2's three legs; its nonce consequence is resolved by A3.3. M3 is replaced by A3.5 and A3.6 — its retirement condition was unreachable and its window adversary-timed. M4's silent/loud split survives as A3.8 with its three condition defects fixed and its doorbell recovery oracle removed. M5's trial-decryption choice survives in A3.9; its "no clear discriminator exists" claim is withdrawn as false and replaced by a named residual. M6's premise survives; its window mechanism is replaced by A3.10's funnel classification. M7 is replaced by A3.7 — identity ordering was publicly computable and therefore a passive de-anonymization predicate. M8 survives as A3.11. M9's record-format obligations survive in A3.12; its pausable clock is deleted, not bounded. M10 survives in A3.12's ladder inheritance, now with a reachable terminal. M11's core statement survives verbatim in A3.16; its stale-copy rider is restored because retirement is now bounded.

**The four cross-decision incoherences, resolved as pairs.** (1) M9(3) × M10: the pause is deleted rather than bounded, and the terminal state is re-homed onto the handshake frames' own give-up clocks — one clock family, already built, unpausable by construction (A3.8, A3.12). (2) M4(b) × M5: the per-frame discriminator M4(b) needed and M5 removed is deleted along with M4(b) itself; the loud condition becomes the leg's give-up, which trial decryption never touches (A3.8, A3.9).
