(3) M3 × M7: the resume record carries two always-present fixed-size handshake slots — own-initiation and peer-acceptance — so the simultaneous case holds every value it transiently needs, and the replacement tiebreak no longer needs the loser's ephemeral to reappear (A3.7, A3.14). (4) Store requirement 3 × requirement 7: crash-safety wins outright; erasure is re-scoped to live-record removal under seal with the unlinked-block residual priced inside the at-rest totality clause, and the anti-rollback counter is bound as AAD for the splicing class it can actually close (A3.14, A3.16).

### A3.1 — every re-establish leg carries `msg_sig`, and the signature covers the contest it decides

Unchanged from A2 (accepted on two review axes; the doctrine is `dm/frame.rs:36-42`). The open sub-question is closed here: the preimage binds a per-leg frame-kind label — the `lp(frame_kind)` discriminator the build notes already use — and the `reconnect_gen` the frame acts at, so a tiebreak or a retirement is only ever decided by a frame bound to that contest. A frame kind without `msg_sig` cannot exist in this feature; build-contract item (iii) already says so.

### A3.2 — the handshake is three legs, and the third is what makes retirement reachable

`RE-EST` (initiator → peer, carrying a fresh ephemeral), `RE-ACK` (peer → initiator, carrying the encapsulation), and `RE-CONFIRM` — the initiator's first frame under the re-rooted chain: protocol-generated, mandatory, empty-bodied and padded like any frame, composed and published immediately on the initiator's commit, riding the ordinary outbox machinery (ladder, ack, give-up) at its own sequence position. A2's worst finding was that nothing obliged the initiator to emit anything under the new root, so an idle initiator held the retirement window open for the life of the install; `RE-CONFIRM` is that obligation, and it is the protocol's, not the user's. Each leg has its own domain label and its own derived key; no leg reuses another's. Cold start is unchanged: a reinstall from mnemonic loses `RS` and goes through the doorbell exactly as v6 specifies, so no routine path touches the doorbell — the E9 closure and the `dflt(32)` sizing basis ("established contacts leave the doorbell") stand.

### A3.3 — seal once, commit before emitting, re-seed byte-identically: the nonce premise is met, not cited

Each leg's frame is sealed exactly once, persisted, then emitted; every retry is a byte-identical re-seed of the stored frame on the ordinary ladder. There is no fresh-nonce retry path, so the key `K(RS_n, gen, leg)` seals exactly one plaintext ever, the global nonce rule's key-uniqueness premise (§ Nonce rule) holds by construction rather than by citation, § m7 sees one ciphertext per logical frame, and the re-seed contract M10 inherits is the ordinary one. The ordering rule generalizes into this amendment's one structural invariant: **a party persists any state it derives from a handshake step before emitting anything that reveals the step happened** — commit-then-emit. A crash before the commit leaves the wire unchanged, which is what deletes generation-regression-by-crash (A3.15 row 5) instead of detecting it: A2's commonest-desync row cannot occur, because a party that lost `ss_new` to a crash never told anyone it had it.

### A3.4 — generations: strictly monotonic, one acceptance per generation, initiation is not acceptance, replays idempotent and metered

`reconnect_gen` advances only by a completed handshake. **A party's own initiation does not consume the generation for acceptance** — the resume record carries two distinct handshake slots, own-initiation and peer-acceptance (A3.14), and a contest (A3.7) is the coin deciding which survives, with the abandoned one marked in the same commit that keeps the other. One `RE-EST` is *accepted* per generation — the first the receiver commits; commit-then-emit means an honest party can never publish two distinct frames at one generation, so a differing frame at an already-accepted generation is dropped without any state change. A byte-identical replay of an accepted generation's `RE-EST` is answered idempotently: the stored `RE-ACK` is re-served, byte-identical — safe because it is the same frame — and **re-serves coalesce into the ordinary ladder** (at most one emission per rung interval, per I3's logical-id coalescing), so a co-host replaying stored bytes cannot pump the client's write budget: a replay storm collapses into the schedule the `RE-ACK` was re-seeding on anyway. **The acceptance window is the generation, never the retention window**: retaining `RS_n` (A3.5) does not extend what a frame sealed under it can cause. A replayed `RE-EST` cannot manufacture a contest (its generation is accepted), cannot trigger the dead-chain sweep (A3.12 — derivation from a commit a replay never reaches), and cannot re-initialize anything. This is the replay rule A2 omitted for its second re-rooting frame kind while v6 already required one for the first (`fc_epoch` idempotent re-accept), and it removes the co-host's phantom-tiebreak-into-permanent-message-loss upgrade.

### A3.5 — retention and retirement: a confirming observation or a hard ceiling, whichever comes first

Each party retains the superseded `RS_n` from its own commit of `RS_{n+1}` until its confirming observation (A3.6) or **`T_RETIRE`** — wall-clock from that commit, suggested at 14 days (twice the give-up; final value at the byte pass), never pausable, persisted with the same future-timestamp refusal `dm/outbox.rs` applies to `composed_at_ms`: a stored time we cannot verify may bound what we do but never overwrite what we were told. The refusal is fail-safe in the same direction as the outbox's — a future `superseded_at_ms` refuses the record's decode rather than disabling the ceiling (the fail-unsafe branch), which means a clock that steps backwards renders the resume record transiently unreadable until real time passes the stored value; that window lands in A3.15 row 6's loud-and-retrying path and heals on its own, nothing rewritten, exactly the trade the outbox's own doctrine already made and for the same reason. At the ceiling without confirmation the party retires `RS_n` regardless **and surfaces the unconfirmed re-establishment** (A3.8). So an actor able to suppress frames buys at most `T_RETIRE` of extended exposure and pays with a surfaced anomaly; the window length is never the peer's choice; and the idle initiator no longer exists as a category, because the initiator's confirming emission is A3.2's protocol obligation. What retention buys while it lasts: the ability to open the peer's frames at the superseded generation — the contest path (A3.7) and the regressed-peer recovery row (A3.15 row 7). The stored handshake frames themselves (own `RE-EST`/`RE-ACK` bytes, kept for idempotent re-serve) outlive `T_RETIRE` harmlessly — they are published wire bytes and carry nothing an observer has not already seen.

### A3.6 — the confirming observation, defined per role, both protocol-generated

For the responder: the first frame that opens under the re-rooted chain — `RE-CONFIRM` or content, whichever arrives first; one is guaranteed to exist because `RE-CONFIRM` is mandatory, persisted, and re-seeded. For the initiator: the ordinary acknowledgement settling `RE-CONFIRM`'s sequence position **within its give-up window**, or any opened frame from the peer under the new root, whichever comes first. The window qualifier is load-bearing, because the frozen ack doctrine is *settled, not collected* — build-contract item (ii) lets a receiver advance its cursor past a message its sender abandoned at give-up, so an unqualified ack is not proof of opening. Within the window it is: the receiver has no licence to advance past an unabandoned position, the collection rules settle nothing for a frame that did not open, and the outbox already refuses confirmation for any entry past its give-up window, swept or not — the existing machinery, invoked rather than duplicated. The residual is a responder that dishonestly settles `RE-CONFIRM` early without opening it: the initiator then retires `RS_n` believing the handshake confirmed — and the party harmed is the liar itself, whose regression row (A3.15 row 7) depended on the peer's retention it just talked away; this is the design's standing "a peer lying about its own collection costs only the liar" residual, unchanged. The receiver's duty to ack an unacknowledged message is the existing D-DELIV mutual-observation contract — advisory and aggregate-capped, so it may be slow, and the ceiling covers the pathological case. Neither observation depends on the user of either party doing anything.

### A3.7 — the tiebreak is a shared-secret coin, and contest detection is a local check, not a sweep race

The selector: each party expands a per-contest value from the superseded root — `HKDF(RS_n, <tiebreak label> ‖ reconnect_gen)`, bytes at the final pass — whose first bit names the direction (`a2b` or `b2a`) whose party's handshake survives. Ungrindable: no frame material enters the selector, so no keygen spend biases it — the outcome was fixed when generation `n` committed. Unobservable: a third party holds neither `RS_n` nor the outcome, so A2's passive predicate binding anonymous pages to named identities does not exist here. Fair in expectation across contests; and the "winner chooses whose ephemeral roots the channel" concern dissolves anyway, because the surviving initiator contributed only a public key — the responder's encapsulation coins co-determine `ss_new`, and `advance_root` mixes `RS_n` besides.

**Detection is a rule, not a race.** Before answering any `RE-EST`, a party consults its own resume record; if it holds a pending initiation at the same generation, the contest fires *at that moment*, locally — a party with a pending initiation can never answer as an oblivious plain responder, whatever order its sweeps run in, so boot-time ordering between initiating and sweeping carries no correctness weight. On a contest firing: the coin's **loser** abandons its own handshake and answers the winner's frame as an ordinary responder — abandonment and acceptance committed together, intra-record (A3.14's two slots); the coin's **winner** does nothing with the loser's frame and keeps waiting for its `RE-ACK`. Asymmetric observation converges because the coin is the same on both sides: if A sees B's frame first and wins, A ignores it and B answers when B's sweep finds A's frame; if A sees B's frame first and loses, A answers it immediately and B's handshake simply completes — B later sweeps A's abandoned frame, finds the generation already accepted, and drops it as A3.4 requires. If the two frames never cross at all (mutual eviction — the hole disclosed in A1's own round), neither party observes a contest, both handshakes re-seed independently on the ladder, and eventual observation is the same store-and-forward argument every frame in this design rests on; the bounded worst case is both give-ups, both loud (A3.15 row 9).

### A3.8 — routine is silent; every anomaly is loud, classed, and suppression-protected

The split survives; the conditions are rebuilt. Loud states, each a `TrustEventKey` of class `PersistentNonBlocking` (the class that protects a signal from suppression), each extending the three exhaustive surfaces `dm/provisional.rs` already extends: **re-establishment failed** — a handshake leg reached its give-up; this is M4(b) and M10's terminal collapsed into one reachable condition, and the per-frame "an `RE-ACK` it cannot open" discriminator is deleted, resolving the incoherence with trial decryption. **Re-establishment unconfirmed** — the `T_RETIRE` ceiling fired. **Peer state regressed** — an authenticated sub-generation `RE-EST` opened inside the retention window: the peer holds rolled-back state; answered by re-serving the stored frame where that recovers (A3.15 row 7), surfaced where it cannot. **Resume state unusable** — `RS` absent or unreadable while `previously_established` is set, split exactly as `TeardownCause` already splits: `unreadable` surfaces its event immediately (the existing `DmProvisionalRecordUnreadable` shape — loud on first occurrence, never a silent loop) *and* retries on a backoff, because offering a destructive fresh first contact on an `EIO` spends an invite token to destroy a working channel; `absent` surfaces the recovery offer. **Re-establishment backoff engaged** — A3.13. **The failed state has an exit, because a slow peer is not a dead peer.** A give-up stops the writes, not the listening: the stored handshake frames persist past give-up, sweeps of cached channel records continue regardless, and **observed peer activity re-arms the handshake** — a late authentic leg arriving on a sweep resumes it directly, and fresh unopenable frames on the channel plane (the expected shape of a returned peer still sending under chains we lost) re-arm the stored `RE-EST`'s ladder at the same generation, coalesced to at most one re-arm per observation window, surfaced as recovering. So a peer offline past the give-up produces failed-then-recovered on its return, and fresh first contact — a human-gated anomaly path, which the ambition call permits (it rejected correspondent action on every restart, not on genuine loss) — is reserved for genuinely lost state, never demanded by mere asynchrony. What M4 had wrong was reaching recovery *through the world-readable doorbell as a cross-plane confirmation oracle on routine desyncs*; none of these conditions consults the doorbell, ever. The UI state while a re-establishment is pending is distinct and visible — a channel re-establishing for a month must not render as healthy, which is #243's guarantee arriving by the new road A2 lost it on.

### A3.9 — the wire shape: an ordinary frame, counters that continue, and an honestly named residual

A re-establish frame is shaped exactly like an ordinary frame and identified by trial decryption — one bounded AEAD attempt per otherwise-unopenable frame with the expected generation's key, plus the contest key while a contest is live. That survives. What A2 claimed beyond it is withdrawn: the clear `eph_ek`/`eph_ct` grouping structure (`dm/frame.rs:22-27`) makes generation boundaries legible to a page co-host regardless of frame shape, so "no clear discriminator exists" was false as written. Three decisions close most of the distance and the remainder is priced. **The sequence spaces and the clear ratchet-generation counter continue across re-establishment**: the re-rooted ratchet initializes its clear generation at one past the last persisted value, never at zero, and `next_send_seq` continues per direction — both are retained non-secret counters (A3.14) — so no counter regresses, no write-once slot is rewritten, and a backwards generation jump does not exist as a marker; this also answers the finding that a restarted party held neither counter. The filler in the `eph_ct`-shaped field is CSPRNG bytes — an ML-KEM ciphertext is indistinguishable from random of its length; provenance stated at the final pass. **The residual that remains is named, not claimed away, and it is a NEW residual requiring its own explicit acceptance at ratification — it is not silently folded into F1.** A co-host that groups frames by ephemeral repetition sees a reconnect as two adjacent short generations followed by re-keyed traffic; the matched pair across both directions is visible only to an observer already co-hosting *both* direction records — whose addresses are mutually unlinkable, so acquiring that vantage is exactly the cross-record linkage the paging design already prices — but to that observer it is a restart signature ordinary short turns only partially mimic. **ACCEPTED (2026-07-30), as its own line item — F1 family, new member.** The rationale, recorded so the acceptance is hard to vary: the only moderate defense would be network scale (the both-records vantage shrinks as the network grows); what it yields is metadata only — pseudonymous record-pair linkage plus restart timing, no identity, no content; and the same restart event is already *more* legible on the lobby, whose presence beacons broadcast the stable identity's online windows on a world-readable record — so this signal is strictly weaker than what the accepted F1 lobby residual already concedes about the same moment, and it composes with F1 rather than opening a new class. The final pass still owes the check that the grouping signature stays inside ordinary shapes — as diligence on the residual's stated size, no longer as a gate on the decision.

  **2026-09-08 — the leg's length is a second discriminator, measured and accepted (2026-09-08).** A re-establishment leg is sealed at a fixed 8220 bytes and published as one value of the same page record ordinary frames use, through the same publish path; an ordinary frame pads to the same 8192 rung and then carries the frame envelope header on top of its sealed body, which a leg does not. The two values therefore differ by a fixed number of bytes, so a co-host of the page record that reads value lengths can tell a leg slot from a frame slot without opening either. Padding legs to the frame bucket ladder changes nothing, because they already sit on its lowest rung; the only closure is giving legs the frame envelope, a wire change, and it is declined. The signal is accepted as a residual in the F1 family: it is visible only to the page co-host that already sees the reconnect as two adjacent short generations, it yields the same restart timing and nothing more, and it is bounded by the DHT subkey ceiling of 32768 bytes on both sides.

### A3.10 — reconnect emissions are class-4 funnel traffic; the funnel owns their timing

All re-establish emissions — first dispatch and every re-seed, all three legs — classify as `Keepalive` (rank 4) in the existing WB-3 funnel: they are machine-generated, not user actions. They inherit the funnel's dispatch machinery, the per-emission jitter the outbox already draws, and I6's independence discipline, probed by WB-ISC-1's KS/correlation test — not by any window this amendment would invent, because WB-3's own doctrine is that a common-cause burst survives zero-mean jitter and only statistical independence at dispatch closes it. **Initiation is prioritized, not broadcast**: a restart re-establishes channels holding pending outbound entries or live user intent first, and trickles the remainder — so the channels whose messages' give-up clocks are running (A3.12 deleted the pause) are exactly the ones at the head of the burst, and a large contact set does not multiply the prompt cost. The `RE-ACK` has no promptness requirement pulling it stimulus-locked — nothing in A3.8 demands one; the ladder's first rung is the latency floor, and a routine reconnect completing at the funnel's pace rather than at user-action latency is the priced trade for not creating the stable cross-record phase relationship I6 forbids (A2's M4-promptness × M6 conflict dissolves with M4(b)). First-dispatch-as-class-4 is a deliberate difference from user sends, whose first dispatch is `Chat`. WB-3 is not amended — new writers classified into existing classes is use of the scheduler — and **the reconnect burst's arithmetic against decision #4's ~0.4–0.9/min headroom is a ratification gate at the final pass, not an assertion here**: if the arithmetic does not close under prioritized initiation, the class choice and the trickle rate are the levers to re-decide, and this decision says so rather than discovering it in a build.

### A3.11 — the re-establish root step keeps its own domain labels

Unchanged from A2, accepted on both review axes that examined it: `advance_root` may be reused, its label may not. Every derivation this amendment adds — the retained sibling root, the per-leg seal keys, the tiebreak selector, the per-leg frame-kind labels — gets its own registered label, joining `ALL`, the byte-pin test, and prefix-freeness, under #283's caveat that the line-scoped guards must be fixed or the declarations kept one-line until they are. Byte values enumerated at the final pass.

### A3.12 — outbox and ratchet obligations: provenance, direction-scoped death, no pausable clock, one re-seed machinery

Four of M9's obligations survive unchanged: per-entry sealing-chain provenance; the dedicated teardown cause (the wildcard-free match makes its absence a compile error); provenance is also what exempts entries composed during a reconnect from the sweep; and the ladder inheritance with state advancing on received frames only. Two are replaced. **The pausable give-up clock is deleted, not bounded.** The give-up runs from compose, unpaused, exactly as built. What bounds a reconnect's cost to a pending message is that the reconnect itself is clocked by its own frames' give-ups (A3.8's failed state), so the insurance a reconnect can consume is bounded by construction, `OnDht` never renders for a message whose channel is in a surfaced failed state, and the fail-unsafe pause accumulator the implementability review flagged never exists. An entry composed while no channel exists waits unsealed — it cannot seal without a chain — with its clock running; a completed re-establishment seals and publishes it with its remaining window; a failed one marks it Undelivered at that moment, loudly, under the reconnect's own event. **The dead-chain sweep is direction-scoped, and it is a derivation, not a transaction.** A restart kills only the restarted party's *receive* chains, so the entries that die are the peer's entries *toward* the restarted party. The condition "sealed under a chain older than the committed generation" is decidable from two persisted facts — the entry's provenance and the resume record's `reconnect_gen` — so the sweep is **idempotent and re-run at every load**: the responder's commit is one intra-record write to the resume record (the single authority), the sweep and its `surfaced` flags follow as derived repair, and a crash between the two is healed at the next boot rather than prevented by a multi-record transaction. The misinformed-`OnDht` window is therefore crash-only and bounded by one restart — never permanent, and the give-up backstops it regardless. The sweep never fires on mere receipt of an unverified frame. The restarted party's own already-published entries remain collectible — the peer's receive state survived — and keep re-seeding, their acks riding the `AR`-rooted ack surface unchanged; the responder retains its superseded receive-side ratchet state in memory until every outstanding position sealed under it settles or gives up (bounded by the give-up; a responder restart during that drain loses them, which is the ordinary restart cost, not a new one). A1 and A2 both stated this sweep direction-blind, which would have killed messages that were still deliverable. Finally, #278's rung-advance-on-attempt tension is inherited, not forked: the re-establish legs ride whatever emission-outcome semantics the outbox lands for #278 — one re-seed machinery, decided once, applied uniformly.

**2026-09-08 — the receiver gives up on a position it never collected, on its own clock, and nothing on the wire changes (on the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-08).** The receiver-side rule this design states — *"the receiver advances its contiguous cursor past a message the sender has abandoned at the 7-day give-up"* — reads as needing to be told that the sender gave up, and nothing carries that: an acknowledgement record and a frame's piggyback both carry the receiver's own `high_water` and beyond-prefix runs, and no field travels the other way. The receiver measures the horizon itself instead. **What is abandoned:** a position below the highest one the receiver has settled that has stayed uncollected for longer than the horizon — `GIVE_UP` plus one re-seed interval, the margin being the longest rung of `RESEED_LADDER`. It is abandoned through the existing `Collection::abandoned`, which advances the contiguous prefix over it and folds the runs above it into the prefix. **On whose clock:** the receiver's own, and as an accumulated age rather than as a deadline — each sweep adds the time since the last one, capped at a few cadences, so a host clock that steps forward cannot abandon a position the sender is still re-seeding, which is the dangerous direction: an abandoned position is settled, and a settled position is filtered out of everything a page fold offers. It runs on the collector's existing probe cadence, so no timer is added anywhere, and a gap ages only while this side is reaching the correspondence at all — a block, an unreadable block list or a correspondence with no key schedule delays a give-up rather than preventing one. The margin covers the interval between the sender's last re-seed and its give-up, during which a copy may still be on its way. **The moved cursor is published**, and a give-up is its own reason to write: the standalone acknowledgement cadence is keyed on messages collected inside the sender's window, which is over by the time this horizon expires, so the ordinary case of one lost message and no later traffic would otherwise move the cursor at a moment when nothing would ever report it. **The wire is unchanged** — no frame, record or field is added, and a correspondent sees a cursor that moved and cannot tell an abandoned position from a collected one, which is the ack doctrine's standing *settled, not collected*. **Rejected — accepting the stall.** The beyond-prefix set is capped at `MAX_ACK_RUNS` runs, and a position that is never settled holds one of them for the life of the conversation; past the cap every further collection is refused, so a conversation that has lost that many positions reports messages it has read as undelivered and keeps its correspondent re-seeding them. **Rejected — a give-up marker on the wire.** Telling the receiver directly is a frame-shape change, on the surface whose one-length and one-shape discipline the whole erasure argument rests on, and it buys nothing the horizon does not: the sender's give-up is computed from a timestamp both sides already have, so a marker would carry a number the receiver can derive.

### A3.13 — re-establishment participation backs off; no human gate, no dead-end state

A2 made queue destruction free where it had cost an out-of-band invite token: a restart-looping correspondent kills the peer's pending queue at every completed handshake, loudly but repeatably and at no cost. Gating participation behind explicit user confirmation past a threshold is rejected: a flaky device restarting several times a day is *routine* for that user, and gating the correspondent reintroduces exactly the correspondent-action the ambition call rejected. The mechanism is instead **participation backoff**: the responder's answer to the K-th completed re-establishment inside a sliding window is delayed on a growing schedule (immediate for the first; suggested window 24 hours and schedule at the byte pass), surfacing its own event once engaged, with no confirmation step and no terminal state — the window slides, the backoff decays, and a peer that stops looping gets immediate answers again. This bounds the queue-destruction *rate* mechanically, keeps every loop individually loud through the Undelivered surfacing, and gives the abusive pattern a visible name without ever asking a human to approve a routine reconnect. Only completed handshakes count — an attacker cannot inflate the counter with replays (accepted generations are inert) or unopenable frames (bounded trial cost, no state change) — and the backed-off party's own legs still ride their ordinary ladders toward their own give-ups, so nothing here creates a new silent state.

### A3.14 — the store: one blob family, one helper, one authority per invariant, and the crash-safety/erasure opposition resolved rather than asserted

**The minimum mechanism stands as the implementability review named it**: one sealed, fixed-size blob per correspondence per record kind — provisional (exists), resume, outbox, cursor — written through a single `replace_atomically(path, bytes)` helper (write `path.tmp` → `sync_all` → `rename` → fsync the directory; ~30 lines, and genuinely the only new machinery — outside `storage/fetched.rs` the repo has no atomic-replace writer, and `profile/persist.rs` is bare `std::fs::write`). No journal, no index, no count: a pending-count-revealing directory is a privacy defect against the existing one-length sealing discipline, so the record kinds are a fixed set at fixed sizes. **The consistency principle that makes "no journal" honest: every atomic invariant lives inside one record, and every cross-record invariant is a derivation from a single authority, re-run idempotently at load.** Intra-record: `RS_{n+1}`, `reconnect_gen`, the retained `RS_n` with its `superseded_at_ms`, the two handshake slots, and the anti-rollback counter commit together in one replace. Cross-record: the resume record's committed generation is the sole authority, and everything the outbox must reflect about it — the dead-chain sweep, its `surfaced` flags — is derivable from provenance-versus-generation and repaired at every boot (A3.12); there is deliberately no invariant whose truth requires two records to have committed together. **The resume record holds**: both root generations and the counter above; **two handshake slots, own-initiation and peer-acceptance, always present and fixed-size** — a contest is the coin choosing which survives (A3.7), both zeroed on completion, so slot occupancy leaks nothing through record size; the stored handshake frames for byte-identical re-serve; the legs' ladder positions — a restart mid-reconnect resumes re-seeding the same frames at the same generation, no re-burst; the per-direction `next_send_seq` and last clear ratchet-generation (A3.9's continuity counters); `previously_established`; and the peer's long-term identity key and cached EK — which A2's requirement 6 silently outsourced to a contact cache that does not exist. This record family *is* that store's first tenant: slice #236's contact cache is the same blob discipline in the same store, not a second store. **Persisted `surfaced` flags land now** (#279): every state whose loudness A3.8 promises carries a persisted flag, so the surfacing is derivable from state rather than from the return value of the call that caused it — the record-format decision two review axes independently named as cheapest exactly now. **The opposition resolves for crash-safety, and erasure is re-scoped to what is true.** Atomic replacement wins every commit. "Erasure" of a superseded root means its removal from the live sealed record by one replace; the unlinked previous blob's blocks survive at the filesystem's pleasure, exactly as `dm/provisional.rs` already concedes that in-place overwrite is a filesystem-API promise an FTL or copy-on-write filesystem does not keep. What that honestly costs: an adversary holding both the raw device and the profile seal key may recover a superseded root from unlinked blocks — and that adversary holds at-rest state, which A3.16's core clause already prices as total; against every lesser adversary the blocks are sealed noise. The freed-allocation half is the repo's standing zeroize hygiene, not an amendment obligation. The anti-rollback counter, bound as AAD, closes splicing and torn-state confusion; it does not close wholesale rollback to a consistent earlier record, which no self-contained store can detect (`dm/provisional.rs:526-529`) — that case lands in A3.15 row 7 as a loud path and in A3.16 under the at-rest totality it belongs to. `unreadable` versus `absent` is not a store requirement — the distinction already exists in `restart()`'s input type; A3.8 is its consumer, which is the downstream use M4 had squandered.

### A3.15 — the divergence table: every row recovering or loud

State pairs over persisted generation state, each with the outcome it lands on. The table is the availability review's instrument, reproduced for this construction as the findings require.

| # | State pair | What happens | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Both torn down at `n`, neither initiated yet | either or both initiate; a contest resolves per A3.7 | recovering |
| 2 | Initiator committed `n+1`; peer unaware (frame in flight or evicted) | `RE-EST` re-seeds on the ladder; peer answers on sweep | recovering; at give-up, loud (failed) |
| 3 | Responder committed and emitted `RE-ACK`; initiator has not seen it | both legs re-seed under mutual observation | recovering; at give-ups, loud on both |
| 4 | Initiator completed and emitted `RE-CONFIRM`; responder awaiting new-root traffic | `RE-CONFIRM` re-seeds; responder retires on opening it | recovering; if suppressed: responder loud at `T_RETIRE`, initiator loud at `RE-CONFIRM`'s give-up |
| 5 | Either party crashed before a commit, at any step | commit-then-emit: nothing was published, peer state unchanged | indistinguishable from rows 1–3; recovering — generation-regression-by-crash cannot occur |
| 6 | Resume state absent or unreadable, marker set (including transient clock-skew unreadability, A3.5) | `unreadable` surfaces immediately and retries on a backoff; `absent` surfaces the recovery offer | loud and recovering, or loud with a recovery path; never a destructive offer on an I/O error |
| 7 | One party rolled back to a consistent `RS_{n-k}` | it speaks sub-generation frames; within retention the peer opens them and re-serves or surfaces *peer state regressed*; past retention its frames are filler and its own give-up fires | loud on both, by different clocks; recovery is fresh first contact |
| 8 | Simultaneous initiation, frames cross | A3.7 coin; loser answers winner, winner ignores loser's frame | recovering |
| 9 | Simultaneous initiation, frames never cross | both ladders re-seed independently | recovering on eventual observation; else both give-ups, both loud |
| 10 | Restart mid-reconnect | persisted ladder positions and stored frames resume; same generation, same bytes | recovering; no re-burst |
| 11 | Peer completes handshakes abnormally often | A3.13 participation backoff | loud, rate-bounded, self-healing |
| 12 | Asymmetric contest observation (one party sees the other's frame first) | the coin is the same on both sides: the loser answers whichever moment it observes; the winner waits; the loser's abandoned frame is dropped at an accepted generation | recovering, by A3.7's local-check rule |
| 13 | Peer offline beyond the give-up | every leg gives up → failed, loud; stored frames persist; the peer's return is observed (a late leg on a sweep, or fresh unopenable traffic) and re-arms the handshake at the same generation | loud, then recovering on peer return; fresh first contact never demanded by mere asynchrony |

No row is silent, and no row is terminal-and-silent. Row 4 is why `RE-CONFIRM` exists; row 5 is why commit-then-emit exists; row 7's within-retention branch is why retention exists; row 13 is why the failed state has an exit.

### A3.16 — the honest security property

Stated last; every clause either survived review or is new and falsifiable. **An at-rest compromise is total for the relationship, and the ratchet does not heal it** — the attacker holds the pseudonym signing key, the resume roots, and the addressing root; it can reconnect first and its authority self-renews (A2's core statement, which survived every attack made on it). **The silent-lockout harm is mitigated, not cured**: the evicted rightful holder's next attempt lands in row 7's loud path rather than in silence, and recovery is a fresh first contact. **A stale copy confers nothing — restored, and now true**: a copy of at-rest state older than the last completed re-establishment loses resume authority at that party's retirement of the superseded root, which A3.5 bounds by a confirming observation or `T_RETIRE`; the window in which a stale copy retains value is at most `T_RETIRE`, and extending it by frame suppression is capped by the same ceiling and surfaced. A2's unbounded version of this claim was false precisely because its retirement could never fire. **Content forward secrecy for past messages is unaffected**: `RS` is an HKDF-Expand sibling of the `ss0` extraction and cannot regenerate `RK0` — resting on HMAC one-wayness as a named assumption plus #282's KATs as the structural oracle, never on the distinctness test the comment currently cites. **Metadata**: reconnect timing decorrelation is owned by the WB-3 funnel under WB-ISC-1's probe; A3.9's grouping-signature residual is its own accepted line item (2026-07-30), in the F1 family; and nothing touches the doorbell, so the per-victim censorship surface the refused alternatives opened stays closed. **Replay**: an accepted generation is inert, an unaccepted one is single-acceptance, and no frame sealed under a retained-but-superseded root causes any state transition — retention is not acceptance. **Availability**: a peer's absence, however long, costs latency and loud states, never the relationship — the only path to fresh first contact is genuine loss of at-rest state.

### Preconditions and verification obligations

Carried from A1, still owed: #282's KATs pinning every sibling root, proven able to fail; #281's store — nothing in this amendment is enforceable until a writer exists; label registration under #283's caveat. New, each to be shown able to fail, not merely to pass: commit-then-emit ordering — a crash injected between seal and commit leaves the wire empty; one-acceptance-per-generation and idempotent re-serve — a replayed `RE-EST` changes no state and the re-served `RE-ACK` is byte-identical and ladder-coalesced; the contest local check — a party holding a pending initiation can never answer as a plain responder, and both parties compute the same surviving handshake from the same `RS_n` under every observation order; `T_RETIRE` integrity — a future `superseded_at_ms` is refused on decode, in the shape of the outbox's `composed_at_ms` refusal, and the ceiling fires with no confirming observation ever supplied; confirmation windowing — an ack settling `RE-CONFIRM`'s position after its give-up is not a confirming observation (the outbox's past-window confirmation refusal, exercised on this path); the sweep as derivation — a crash injected between the resume-record commit and the outbox sweep is repaired by the next load, sweeping the same entries with the same `surfaced` flags; the direction-scoped sweep — the restarted party's published entries stay collectible while the peer's entries toward it die at the committed generation; counter continuity — the clear generation and `next_send_seq` never regress across a re-establishment; failed-state re-arm — a late authentic leg, and separately fresh unopenable traffic, each re-arm a given-up handshake at the same generation, coalesced; and the divergence table's rows as integration obligations, rows 5, 7, and 12 first.

### On ratification

The ISC-C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25 family gains criteria for A3.1–A3.16 and the obligations above; #262 closes citing this section; #236's contact-cache scope line is corrected to A3.14's record family. #279's record-format question and #281's store shape are answered by A3.14 — both issues stay open as the build work they track. Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A4 — channel re-establishment: the revision after A3 (supersedes A3; ~~PENDING ratification~~ ~~stress-tested and REFUTED in part — A5 pending~~ **superseded by A5, below**)

> **Stress-tested 2026-07-30 by an adversarial review on three axes (crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, store/erasure). REFUTED; the spine held on every axis, so an A5 revision follows — not a redesign.** The surviving core (the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped idempotent sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto, A4.2's causal-edge removal, A4.3's jitter + trickle-deletion, A4.6's `dir`, A4.8's `next_send_seq`/`S_pc`+`PK_pc`/anti-rollback-drop) passed all three. The headline is a cross-axis convergence: all three axes independently refuted **A4.1's recovery mechanism** — a deleted correspondent and a long-absent one are wire-indistinguishable so no single trigger is both "recover at any delay" and "loud on deletion" (crypto B-A4-1); the single peer-acceptance slot can be evicted during the unbounded absence window (store F3); and the past-give-up standing re-seed is a new single-record discriminator A4.7 never priced (metadata M3). The convergent fix: recovery is an **active `(gen, attempt)` rendezvous** with peer-activity liveness, not a passive stale-slot sweep + give-up timer. Two further clusters: A4's crypto sections created two persistent-state requirements (`attempt`, a byte-novelty dedup memory) that A4.8 never carried, reopening B-CRYPTO-2 and MAJOR-CRYPTO-3 (crypto B-A4-2/B-A4-3); and the single "outbox blob" conflates immutable sealed frames with the volatile re-seed schedule, giving O(N²) rewrite volume and making its fixed-bucket sizing self-contradictory (store F1/F2). The A4.7 residual is re-sized: more accurately a **selectivity amplifier on the already-accepted F1 cross-plane join**, not a new class (metadata M4), but carrying M3's new discriminator and a downtime cessation floor that network scale does not reach and only a deferred pinning node would mask — **unreachable in MVP**. **Do not build against this section.**

> **A revision, not a redesign.** The A3 stress round (three independent axes: crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, erasure/availability) refuted A3 but confirmed its spine, and named five failure clusters ranked by fix depth. A4 fixes those five clusters plus four MAJORs and two doc-hygiene items, and carries A3's surviving decisions forward unchanged. Every A3.x decision not explicitly revised below stands as A3 wrote it; A4 renumbers nothing it does not touch, so a reader resolves any A3.k by reading A3.k unless an A4.j says otherwise. Decision IDs are `A4.1`–`A4.10`, a namespace nothing else in this document uses.

> **A pre-flight adversarial review on three axes (crypto/availability, metadata, store) was run against a first draft of A4 and its ~18 findings folded in before this section landed.** All three axes refuted the draft in part and all three accepted the spine. The load-bearing fold: the first draft had A3's own class of interlock — A4.1 (retirement recovery), A4.2 (no read-caused writes), and A4.5 (replay inertness) were mutually unsatisfiable, because the recovery `RE-ACK` gave up at 7 days while `T_RETIRE` ran 14 and A4.5 forbade answering the returning re-seed. The fold makes the unconfirmed accepted handshake keep its `RE-ACK` on a standing re-seed and recover by the initiator's *pull*, so no clause fights another (A4.1/A4.2). Other folds: A4.5's discriminator moved from sequence-freshness to byte-novelty (a genuine rollback carries *reused* low sequences, so the seq test silenced exactly what it must catch); a pending-unsealed-entry standing cause and an overdue-schedule re-jitter restored the delivery liveness and the anti-burst the deleted trickle had been carrying (A4.2/A4.3); A4.6 gained an attempt counter; A4.8 gained the peer's `PK_pc`, a persisted-not-derived send seq, a per-send clear-generation home, fixed-bucket blob sizes, a stated read order, and the deletion of an anti-rollback counter that protected the empty set; and A4.7's residual grew — the cross-plane join is available passively from a downtime cessation shadow, so the census is six writes not four and the acceptance A4 reopens is larger than the draft claimed. **A full stress round is still expected before ratification; this section is written to be attacked, not to look finished.**

**The survivor set A4 does not relitigate**, confirmed sound on all three axes: the retained sibling `RS` and reconnect on the `AR` plane; RE-CONFIRM as the mechanism that makes retirement *reachable* (its retirement *timing* is what A4 fixes, not the leg); the shared-secret tiebreak coin (`HKDF(RS_n, label ‖ gen)` — ungrindable, unobservable, convergent); commit-then-emit, which deletes generation-regression-by-crash structurally; the direction-scoped idempotent dead-chain sweep as a derivation from the resume record's committed generation; CSPRNG filler indistinguishability in the `eph_ct`-shaped field; the deleted pausable give-up clock; and the crypto soundness of counter continuity (its *persistence granularity* was the open question, closed in A4.7). These carry from A3 verbatim.

### A4.1 — retirement retires the opening key, not the recognition of an accepted handshake (closes B-CRYPTO-1)

The headline refutation: a responder that receives `RE-EST`, commits `RS_{n+1}`, emits `RE-ACK`, and then hits `T_RETIRE` before an absent initiator ever collects that `RE-ACK` was said to strand the channel terminally — the initiator returns still at generation `n`, its only speakable frame is `RE-EST` under the now-retired `RS_n`, and the responder can no longer open it. The error is conflating two different capabilities that A3.5 already separated but did not lean on: retiring `RS_n` deletes the ability to *open a new plaintext* sealed under it; it does not delete the ability to *recognize and answer a byte-identical replay of an already-accepted `RE-EST`*, because recognition is a byte comparison against the stored accepted-`RE-EST` bytes, key-free. A3.5 already keeps those stored handshake bytes "past `T_RETIRE` harmlessly"; A4 makes the consequence load-bearing.

So the resolution is a retention split plus a standing re-seed, not a re-timed clock — and recovery is a **pull, not a push**:

- **`RS_n` (the opening key):** retired at the confirming observation (A3.6) or `T_RETIRE`, whichever comes first — unchanged from A3.5. Retiring it at the ceiling is safe because the only thing lost is opening *new* sub-generation plaintexts, and the exposure bound `T_RETIRE` buys is the whole point.
- **The accepted handshake stays live on a standing re-seed until confirmation.** A responder that accepts `RE-EST` and composes `RE-ACK` keeps that `RE-ACK` re-seeding on the `b2a` page — but the accepted-but-unconfirmed handshake **does not take the ordinary 7-day give-up**; it winds down to a slow standing cadence and persists until the responder's confirming observation (A3.6). The `RE-ACK` therefore has a live schedule for the whole window, not one that dies at day 7 while `T_RETIRE` still has a week to run. **Recovery is the returning initiator's sweep, never the responder reacting to an inbound frame:** the returning-at-any-delay initiator sweeps the `b2a` page, finds the standing `RE-ACK`, collects it, derives `ss_new`, roots the chain, and emits `RE-CONFIRM`. The responder writes nothing *because of* the initiator's return — the `RE-ACK` was already on the page on the responder's own clock (A4.2). No inbound-triggered re-serve exists, so B-META-3's oracle has no edge, and A4.5 never enters (there is no answer-on-read). This is the design's ordinary pull-based store-and-forward, not a new mechanism.

The accepted handshake occupies A3.14's fixed peer-acceptance slot (one, fixed-size), so there is no unbounded per-unconfirmed-handshake growth — it is zeroed on confirmation. **The one removal path is deliberate contact deletion, and it lands loud, never silent.** A responder that deletes the correspondence GCs the slot; a later returning initiator sweeps and finds nothing, its `RE-EST` re-seeds get no answer, and after its handshake's own give-up cycles it surfaces a distinct loud **peer-unreachable** state that offers fresh first contact as a user-gated action — genuine loss of the far side, which the ambition call permits, not a silent spinner and not the mere-asynchrony case it forbade. The terminal-by-asynchrony case B-CRYPTO-1 constructed cannot occur: an absent-but-existing peer recovers by sweep at any delay, and a deleted peer surfaces loud.

**The two `T_RETIRE` clocks are asymmetric and that is now benign (folds MINOR-CRYPTO-5).** Each party's clock runs from its own commit, so the responder's can fire well before the initiator's. Under A3 that asymmetry threatened availability; under A4 it bounds only *exposure duration* — because retirement no longer gates the other party's recovery, one clock firing early cannot strand the other. State the ceiling against each party's own commit; the skew needs no shared anchor.

### A4.2 — no emission is caused by an inbound read; a restart is not an emission event (closes B-META-3, B-META-4, and the inducibility half of MAJOR-CRYPTO-3)

The sharpest metadata finding: A3.4's "the stored `RE-ACK` is re-served" reads as a response *to* the replay, which makes it an active page-to-page confirmation oracle — a co-host rolls an `a2b` page value back to captured `RE-EST` bytes, the peer re-reads and (on A3's reading) writes a re-serve into the `b2a` page, and an observer holding `b2a` gets one linkage bit on demand. A4 removes the causal edge rather than rate-limiting it:

**The invariant, scoped to attacker-drivability: no write is caused by an inbound value or frame that an adversary can present.** Reading updates local state — accept a generation, collect an ack, advance a cursor, recognize a replay — and emits nothing in the same turn. Every emission is scheduled by the *emitter's own* persisted ladder, sweep cadence, user action, or standing-state timer, never triggered by an arriving value. This amends **I9** (`docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` — "no read-triggered writes; the enqueue surface is reachable only from user actions, session boundaries, and cadence timers"): A4 owns that a re-establish leg's dispatch is a cadence-timer/standing-state emission, and states so rather than smuggling it in. Concretely, the responder's `RE-ACK` re-seeds on its own jittered standing schedule until the confirming observation (A3.6) terminates it (A4.1); it is *not* re-served in response to reading a rolled-back `a2b` value, so a co-host rolling that value back produces no `b2a` write. B-META-3's oracle has no edge, and B-META-4's I3-misuse is moot — the re-serve-driven write pump A3.4's rung-coalescing defended does not exist.

**The one attacker-drivable read→write edge that remains is bounded to the F1 rhythm class, not closed to zero.** A sweep that finds a fresh unopenable frame on a channel we have given up (A3.8's failed-state re-arm) is a read that leads to a write. A4 constrains it two ways: the re-arm gates on the frame's *clear* ratchet header advancing past a persisted per-direction clear high-water — unauthenticated but monotone, so captured bytes replayed by a co-host drive it at most once, not repeatedly (`msg_sig` lives inside the seal and cannot gate an unopenable frame, so this clear-header monotone check is the only lever, and A4 names it as such); and the re-arm dispatches through the jittered funnel, coalesced to one per observation window, so what an observer buys is a jittered, coalesced, one-bit-per-window timing correlation of the F1 rhythm class — not a tight oracle, not a plaintext, not an identity. This is a priced residual, not a closed edge, and A4 says so.

**Corollary: a restart triggers no burst.** Nothing dispatches *because* a restart happened. After a restart, emissions resume from standing causes, each decorrelated from the others and from the restart instant:

1. **Already-sealed outbox entries** re-seed on their *persisted* ladder phase (A3.14 persists `ReseedSchedule`'s rung and due-time; `dm/outbox.rs:509-523`), **with overdue schedules re-jittered forward at load** (A4.3) so entries whose due-time elapsed during downtime do not all fire at boot.
2. **A pending unsealed outbox entry** — an entry composed while no chain exists — is a standing cause that re-establishes its own channel, dispatched at *that entry's own persisted ladder phase*. This is the cause A3.10's deleted prioritized-initiation was actually serving; without it a quiet peer's mail would run to Undelivered with no `RE-EST` ever sent (the liveness regression B3 named). Phased on the entry's own pre-existing ladder, it introduces no synchronized first-dispatch.
3. **Sweep-observed inbound need** — the bounded, jittered, priced re-arm edge above.
4. **User action** (opening or sending in a chat) re-establishes that channel at user timing — the already-priced `Chat`-class leak.

Quiet channels re-establish lazily, when their own cause fires. No emission is conditioned on the restart, so "the device restarted" is not legible as a common cause *at dispatch* across N records — though its *cessation* shadow is a separate residual A4.4 prices.

### A4.3 — reconnect emissions enter the funnel jittered from the first dispatch; the prioritized trickle is deleted (closes B-META-1, B-META-1a, B-META-1b, and M-a)

A3.10 handed reconnect timing to "the per-emission jitter the outbox already draws," but that jitter is re-seed-backoff jitter applied to the *next* emission (`schedule_next_jittered`, `dm/outbox.rs:546-570`); the first dispatch is due immediately and unjittered (`ReseedSchedule::new` sets `rung:0, next_due_ms:now_ms`, `:514-520`). A4 owns the WB-3/I9 change A3.10 wrongly denied, in **two** places, because there are two ways an emission bunches at one instant: **(a) a re-establish leg's first dispatch is scheduled into the funnel at a jittered delay from now, never at the immediate `ReseedSchedule::new` phase; and (b) on load, any persisted schedule whose `next_due_ms` already elapsed during downtime is re-drawn forward by a fresh jittered delay from its rung's band, rather than being left overdue.** Without (b) the burst A4.2 claimed to delete simply moves into cause 1: a device offline for hours resumes with every overdue entry across every record due at one boot instant — verbatim B-META-1's shape. `ReseedSchedule` has no load-time re-jitter today (`schedule_next_jittered` only sets the *next* due time); A4 obliges one. Only with both does "wire rhythm unchanged by restart" hold.

**The prioritized trickle and its ranking leak are deleted.** A3.10 dispatched channels with pending mail or live intent first; the metadata review showed this both leaks the user's correspondent priority ordering off arrival order (B-META-1a) and cannot hold the burst apart anyway, because I8's starvation floor escalates aged writes out of their class (B-META-1b). A4 needs neither, because A4.2 removed the burst: there is no synchronized set of first-dispatches to order or to trickle. Each channel re-establishes when its own standing cause fires, which is the decorrelation the trickle was reaching for, without the ranking side-channel.

**`RE-CONFIRM` is composed immediately, dispatched on the funnel (folds M-a).** A3.2's "composed and published immediately on the initiator's commit" collided with A3.10's "the funnel owns timing." A4 splits the two words A3 ran together: `RE-CONFIRM` is *composed and persisted* immediately (commit-then-emit demands it), and *dispatched* through the jittered funnel like every other leg, with no promptness requirement. There is no stimulus-locked prompt emission, so the promptness×jitter incoherence A3 introduced on its own third leg dissolves.

### A4.4 — A4 owns a cross-record arrival-independence obligation, and the backoff schedule is randomized (closes B-META-2 and M-d)

A3.10 inherited WB-ISC-1, but that probe is presence-scoped: it compares one client's dispatch delay against its own other-class load, and cannot distinguish "all N of a device's records emitted in window W because a restart triggered them" from "delay is independent of chat load." The reconnect threat is cross-record correlation from a common cause, a different quantity. A4 owns its own ratification obligation: **a probe demonstrating that, across a device's several page records, *emission* arrival times carry no dependence conditioned on a restart event** — that observing k of N of a device's records cannot beat chance at recovering "a restart occurred at t₀" *from what was written*. Under A4.2 + A4.3's two-place jitter this is structurally satisfied on the emission side, and the ISC verifies the structure held rather than inventing a jitter window.

**But the emission side is not the whole signal, and A4.4 does not claim it is.** A device offline shows a **synchronized cessation** of the re-seed streams on its records — and then a synchronized resumption — visible to a co-host of k of them without any forged bytes and without any emission to decorrelate: absence is not schedulable. It is sharpest exactly where streams are active (recent traffic, short rungs) and negligible where they are already slow. This is a priced residual, not a closed edge, and it matters to A4.7 because it means **the restart signal exists even with zero A3.9 grouping discriminator** — the cross-plane join key (A4.7/S4) is available to a passive both-records co-host from the cessation shadow alone. The final pass owes a size estimate of the cessation signal against ordinary quiet periods; no jitter removes it, so the honest statement is that the emission side is decorrelated and the cessation side is a residual of the store-and-forward model itself.

**A3.13's backoff delay is randomized, not a growing deterministic schedule (M-d).** A deterministic growing schedule makes `RE-ACK` latency a computable function of the peer's restart history — the stable cross-record timing relationship I6 forbids. The backoff draws a jittered delay from a growing *range*, so participation still throttles a restart-looping correspondent while the per-answer latency carries no fingerprint. Only completed handshakes increment (A3.13, unchanged — replays and unopenable frames cannot drive the counter), and the parameters remain a ratification-gated value, not a build guess (MINOR-ERAS-6).

### A4.5 — the freshness predicate: a superseded-root frame causes a transition only if it is fresh (closes the contradiction half of MAJOR-CRYPTO-3)

A3.16's replay clause ("no frame sealed under a retained-but-superseded root causes any state transition") contradicted A3.8's *peer state regressed* alarm, which fires on exactly an authenticated sub-generation `RE-EST` opened inside the retention window — and a co-host re-serving a captured generation-`n-1` `RE-EST` could raise that rollback alarm at will. The discriminator must separate "a co-host replayed bytes it captured" from "the peer genuinely rolled back and is speaking again." Sequence-freshness is the **wrong** test: `seq` is one monotone space per direction persisted in the outbox (`dm/outbox.rs:583-584`), so a party rolled back to a consistent earlier at-rest state restores that outbox and its genuinely-fresh content carries seqs it has *already used* — at or below the peer's high-water — so a seq-high-water gate would silence exactly the rollback it must catch, and inversely could be satisfied by a replay that happened to sit above the mark. A4 uses **byte-novelty**, not seq-monotonicity: **an authenticated superseded-root frame causes a state transition (the regressed alarm, or any answer) only if its bytes are novel — not a byte-identical replay of a frame already processed at that logical position, per the design's existing idempotent-processing dedup; a byte-identical replay is inert, changing no state and raising no alarm.** A co-host holds no key and can only re-serve captured bytes, so every induced frame is byte-identical to one already processed → inert; a genuine rollback re-seals its content under a fresh nonce (A3.3 makes bytes identical only for the *same* logical frame, never for a re-seal at a reused position) → novel bytes → the alarm fires correctly. The cost is a bounded dedup memory of processed-frame identifiers per position, which idempotent processing already needs; the seq space is left to A4.8's write-once-slot discipline, not overloaded as a rollback anchor.

### A4.6 — the leg seal key binds direction (closes B-CRYPTO-2)

A3.3's leg key `K(RS_n, gen, leg)` has three inputs and omits both direction and attempt, so a single key can seal two distinct plaintexts along two paths: the simultaneous-contest case A3.7 is built for (both parties seal their own `RE-EST` under an identical key), and — because `reconnect_gen` advances only on a *completed* handshake (A3.4) — a second `RE-EST` sealed at the same generation after a contest's loser abandons and the winner then gives up, both still at generation `n`. A4 adds **two** KDF inputs: **`K(RS_n, gen, attempt, leg, dir)`**, where `dir` is the direction the sealing party owns (`a2b` or `b2a`, well-defined per party in the symmetric-owner-write setting and already bound in the frame AAD at `dm/frame.rs:284`) and `attempt` is a per-direction counter advancing on *every* `RE-EST` seal, not only on completion. `dir` separates the two parties in the contest; `attempt` separates a re-initiation from its predecessor at an un-advanced generation. Together they close the collision at its source rather than relying on 96-bit nonce-collision resistance. Two KDF inputs; byte-level label composition at the final pass.

### A4.7 — the A3.9 residual is re-sized honestly and its acceptance is REOPENED (answers cluster 3, S1–S5)

The metadata review falsified the sizing on which the maintainer accepted the A3.9 grouping-signature residual at `029ab72`. **That acceptance is reopened; A4 does not re-accept on the maintainer's behalf.** What A4 can do is shrink what is shrinkable and price the rest correctly so the re-decision rests on true facts.

Shrunk:

- **`RE-CONFIRM` carries a fresh ephemeral in `eph_ek` and CSPRNG filler in `eph_ct` (addresses S1b, m1).** A3 left the `eph_ek` continuity between `RE-EST` and `RE-CONFIRM` unspecified, and one resolution created an `eph_ek`-repeat-with-changed-`eph_ct` pattern occurring nowhere else — a single-record discriminator. A4 specifies the other: `RE-CONFIRM` is the first frame of the *re-rooted* chain and carries that chain's fresh ratchet ephemeral in `eph_ek`, distinct from `RE-EST`'s handshake ephemeral; and because the root already came from the `RE-EST`/`RE-ACK` KEM exchange, `RE-CONFIRM` encapsulates nothing, so its `eph_ct`-shaped field is CSPRNG filler like any continuing frame (indistinguishable from an ML-KEM ciphertext, confirmed sound last round). A page co-host sees two adjacent short generations with distinct ephemerals — the shape of any two short conversational turns — not a bespoke repeat pattern.
- **Counter-continuity granularity is pinned to per-send persistence, and A4 concedes the per-turn commit S3 named (corrects the A3 sizing).** The A3 draft claimed the clear ratchet-generation counter could commit only at generation boundaries with no per-turn cost; that is wrong. The clear `generation` is the *ratchet* generation, which advances on every direction switch (`dm/frame.rs`, "the reply takes the first step") — a per-turn counter. Committing it only at a per-reconnect boundary leaves it stale: a session that ran the ratchet from generation 5 to 40 in-memory, with the ratchet not persisted (`0691a7a`), restarts and re-roots at "one past the last persisted value" = 6, and a page co-host sees 40 → 6, the exact backwards jump plus rewritten write-once slots the claim denies. So the counter **must persist at per-send granularity**, riding the outbox blob's already-existing per-send write (A4.8) — no *new* blob on the compose path, but a real per-send field, not a free lunch. Pinned there, `next_send_seq` and the clear generation both continue across re-establishment and neither regresses; the honest statement is that the granularity is per-send, not per-generation, and S3's "the ~30 lines did not price a per-turn commit" is correct — the per-turn commit is the send's own existing write, reused, not avoided.

Priced honestly, not claimed away — these are the corrected facts the re-decision rests on:

- **Single-record discriminators exist (S1a, S1c).** A3.2's mandatory `RE-CONFIRM` forces a protocol-guaranteed two-frame sequence on one record with no human in the loop — ordinary traffic never guarantees a follow-up frame — so the reconnect is partially legible on a *single* record, not only to a both-records observer. And A3.12 stops the dead-chain re-seed stream at the responder's commit, which a co-host of the `b2a` pages sees as an abrupt re-seed termination at neither give-up nor presence — a reconnect marker on the *other* record alone. (Lever for the final pass, if the sizing needs it: let dead-chain entries wind down to their own give-up instead of stopping at commit, trading a little wasted send for removing the abrupt-stop edge.)
- **The ack record is correlated, and the census is six writes, not four (S2, corrected).** All three legs ride the ordinary outbox machinery — ladder, **ack**, give-up (A3.2) — and none can piggyback a confirming ack (RE-EST/RE-ACK/RE-CONFIRM carry no content), so a reconnect emits **three standalone acks in addition to the three legs — six writes in fixed causal order across the page records and the ack record**, not the four A4's first draft stated. The jittered standalone ack cadence (`dm/ack.rs`) spreads them in time but does not remove them. This is materially more than A3.9's "two adjacent short generations," and it spans a record A3.9 omitted — and because A4.7 asks the maintainer to re-decide *on corrected facts*, the corrected census is load-bearing.
- **The correct classification is a cross-plane join, not "weaker than F1" (S4) — this is the crux of the re-decision.** The lobby residual (F1) publishes "named identity X was online at T" on a world-readable, identity-attributed surface; the page grouping-signature publishes "anonymous page-set S restarted at T." These are not substitutes on the same axis — they sit on opposite sides of the pseudonymity boundary, and their *conjunction is a join key* that yields candidate identity attribution for an anonymous page record. The lobby half is free to every page co-host (world-readable). So the residual is not a weaker member of the F1 family; it is a mechanism for crossing from the anonymous plane to the identified plane, and its selectivity is *highest when few clients restart concurrently* — i.e. at alpha scale, the deployment this ships into (S5). The one named defense, network scale, is weakest exactly there.

**And the join key is available even with zero grouping discriminator (S4 × A4.4's cessation residual).** A both-records co-host reads the restart instant off the synchronized cessation-and-resumption of the two records' re-seed streams (A4.4) without needing any of the grouping signatures above — so shrinking the emission-side discriminators (S1b, S3) reduces the *precision* of the timing, not the *existence* of the join. The cross-plane join from the anonymous page plane to the named lobby plane therefore does not depend on the residual A4 shrinks; it depends on the both-records vantage, which the paging design already prices as a cross-record linkage cost. At alpha scale, "no certain identity" is soft: with one restarting client in the window, the join is effectively certain.

**The decision A4 puts to the maintainer:** accept the residual at its corrected size (a cross-plane join yielding pseudonymous-record-to-candidate-identity linkage plus restart timing — no content, no certain identity except at alpha scale — available passively from the cessation shadow and sharpened by the emission-side discriminators), or spend against it. The available spend levers, cheapest first: the dead-chain wind-down above (removes S1c); making `RE-CONFIRM` optional-when-content-follows so the two-frame sequence is not protocol-guaranteed on quiet channels (erodes S1a, at the cost of A4.1's confirming-observation guarantee — a real tension, not free); and, structurally, the both-records vantage still costs an adversary a share of the keyspace, so the honest mitigation remains "the residual shrinks as the network grows." The cessation shadow is the floor none of these levers reach — only network scale does. This is the maintainer's call on corrected facts, recorded here as an open ratification line-item, not resolved in the amendment.

### A4.8 — store deltas: seq home, the missing signing key, opaque names, commit ordering, AAD scope (closes B-ERAS-1, MAJOR-CRYPTO-4, M-b, MINOR-ERAS-2, MINOR-ERAS-9a)

A3.14's store answer is sound except at these points A4 corrects:

- **`next_send_seq` stays in the outbox as an explicit persisted field, never derived from entries (B-ERAS-1, F8).** A3.14 moved it into the resume blob, creating exactly the cross-record torn state A3.14 claimed cannot exist: composing a content frame must write the outbox entry and advance the counter, in two blobs, while `replace_atomically` is single-blob — a crash between reissues a sequence (two ciphertexts at one write-once slot) or leaves a permanent hole the receiver's contiguous cursor waits on forever. The counter is intra-record with the entry that consumes it, so it belongs in the outbox blob, where A3.9's cross-restart continuity is satisfied because the outbox survives restart. It must be a **stored monotonic field**, not the implicit `max(seq over entries) + 1` the current `Outbox` shape (`{direction, entries}`, `dm/outbox.rs`) would compute — that derivation regresses the moment any terminal entry is pruned, and A4.1's contact-deletion GC contemplates exactly that pruning, so the derived form reintroduces B-ERAS-1 through A4's own door. A4 keeps the seq in the outbox and pins it to a persisted field; homing the counter there does make compose a genuine single-blob write, because the ratchet is not persisted (`0691a7a`) and nothing else in the send path commits.
- **The clear ratchet-generation counter is homed in the outbox at per-send granularity too (F1).** It rides the same per-send outbox write as `next_send_seq`, for the reason A4.7's S3 correction gives: it is a per-turn counter and persisting it only at a reconnect boundary produces the backwards jump. This keeps the compose path single-blob (both continuity counters live in the outbox the send already writes) and is the concrete home A4.7 promised.
- **The party's own signing key `S_pc` *and the peer's verifying key `PK_pc`* are enumerated in the resume record (MAJOR-CRYPTO-4, F2).** A3.1 makes `msg_sig` mandatory on all three legs under the per-correspondent keypair, which is at-rest-only and NOT mnemonic-derivable. `S_pc` (ours) is needed to *sign*; the peer's `PK_pc` is needed to *verify* the legs we receive — and A3.14's list carried only the peer's long-term identity key `PK_lt`, not `PK_pc`, while the frozen design already required persisting `PK_pc` (§ the resume tuple `{ss0, AR, RK, PK_pc}`) and `ProvisionalRecord` (`dm/provisional.rs:396-400`) holds neither. Without *both* keys, `msg_sig` is unproducible or unverifiable after the very restart A4 exists to survive, and every inbound leg fails signature check → indistinguishable from filler. A4 enumerates `S_pc` and the peer's `PK_pc` in the resume record; they commit intra-record with the rest.

  - **2026-09-05 — the resume record enumerates a third key: *this party's own* `PK_pc` (ruled 2026-09-05).** The pair above is what the three legs need and no more, because a leg's signature preimage binds no public key: `S_pc` signs, the peer's `PK_pc` verifies. An ordinary channel frame is different — it binds the sealer's own `PK_pc` into what it seals — and ML-DSA-87 offers no way to recover a public key from a private one. So a correspondence restored from a record holding only those two keys can complete a re-establishment and then cannot compose a message on the channel it just re-established. The record carries all three; they commit intra-record with the rest. Build note: `dm::resume::ResumeRecord`.
- **Per-correspondence files get opaque names *and fixed-bucket sizes* (M-b, F5).** A3.14's file family leaks at rest *without* the seal key along three channels: names are identifiers, mtime is a per-commit timeline, and — the one most easily missed — **size**. `Outbox::encode` length-prefixes every `AwaitingCollection` entry at `PAD_BUCKETS` (8192/16384), so the outbox blob's size is ≈ 8 KB × pending-message count, and a keyless observer reads pending-message count per correspondence straight off `stat`. That is precisely the "pending-count-revealing directory" A3.14 declared a defect, arriving through file size instead of directory listing, and it falsifies A3.14's "fixed set at fixed sizes." A4 therefore names each file `HKDF(profile-seal, correspondent-id ‖ record-kind)` (kills the identifier and the join to a named correspondent) **and pads each record kind to fixed size buckets so blob size reveals nothing about contents** — or takes the single-bundled-per-profile-blob lever now (which removes name, size, *and* the mtime/count timeline at the cost of rewriting the whole store per commit). Padding is the minimum; the bundled blob is the clean answer and A4 recommends deciding it at the store's design, not deferring to v2, given three independent leak channels converge on it. What remains after padding is an *anonymous* mtime timeline and a file **count** that is 2–4 per correspondence (not a flat 4 — provisional is deleted on establishment while resume/outbox/cursor appear, so the present-set encodes lifecycle stage, MINOR-ERAS-9b), one bit per correspondence off every `stat`. That is at-rest cardinality metadata — a different class from the *wire*-length sealing discipline, and A4 does not conflate them; it is what the bundled blob exists to remove.
- **The provisional→resume transition names both a write order and a read order (MINOR-ERAS-2, F7).** First establishment is delete-provisional + create-resume across two blobs. **Write order: create the resume record first** (it is the authority and carries `previously_established`), then best-effort delete the provisional record — so a crash never leaves "neither present," which `restart()` would misread as `Ok(None)` = ordinary established-teardown (`dm/provisional.rs:757-761`). But create-first *creates* a both-present crash window, and `restart()` today reads the provisional record and returns `HandshakeResumes` (`:800-805`) — re-running first establishment on an already-established channel, a fresh `ss0` and a colliding seq space. So A4 also fixes the **read order: the loader consults the resume record first; a present resume record is authority and a lingering provisional record beside it is ignored and cleaned.** This must be structural — `restart()` is fed the resume record's presence, not only the provisional's — not a comment.
- **The anti-rollback counter is dropped, not scoped.** An anti-rollback counter bound in the resume record's own AAD does not "close splicing." It closes nothing a self-contained store does not already close: the blob is one whole-record AEAD seal, so intra-record splicing and torn state are caught by the tag itself (`dm/provisional.rs:510-529` makes exactly this argument), and a counter bound in its own AAD is self-referential — a wholesale rollback supplies the old blob together with its own consistent old counter, and it verifies. It protects the empty set. A4 removes it rather than imply protection it cannot give: **wholesale rollback to a consistent earlier record is undetectable by any self-contained store (`dm/provisional.rs:526-529`), full stop, and lands in the divergence table's row 7′ (loud on the fresh content a rollback must eventually send, A4.5) and A3.16's at-rest totality — nowhere else.**

### A4.9 — doc hygiene: the load-bearing invariant is cited (closes the two hygiene items)

A3.16's no-silent-content-regression clause rests entirely on the code invariant that the ratchet is not persisted across restart (`0691a7a`): any restore forces re-establishment, so a regressed peer can only emit sub-generation *handshake* frames, never content, and A3.8 fires loud. A4.10 cites `0691a7a` as that clause's load-bearing basis — with the standing warning that if steady-state ratchet persistence is ever reintroduced, the no-silent-content-regression guarantee goes silent and this design breaks. The commit-ordering hygiene item is discharged in A4.8.

### A4.10 — the honest security property, revised (supersedes A3.16)

Every clause survived review or is new and falsifiable.

- **An at-rest compromise is total for the relationship, and the ratchet does not heal it** — the attacker holds the pseudonym signing key, the resume roots, and the addressing root; it can reconnect first and its authority self-renews. Unchanged; survived every attack.
- **Availability: a peer's absence, however long, costs latency and loud states, never the relationship.** This is A4.1's correction to A3.16's availability clause: retirement retires the opening key, not the standing `RE-ACK`, so a responder that retires `RS_n` at `T_RETIRE` still holds the accepted handshake's `RE-ACK` re-seeding on the page for a returning-at-any-delay initiator to collect by sweep, and the only path to fresh first contact is genuine loss of at-rest state or deliberate deletion of the correspondence — never mere asynchrony.
- **The silent-lockout harm is mitigated, not cured**: the evicted rightful holder's next attempt lands in a loud path (A3.15 row 7), recovery is fresh first contact.
- **A stale copy confers nothing beyond `T_RETIRE`**: a copy older than the last completed re-establishment loses resume authority at that party's retirement of the superseded root, bounded by a confirming observation or `T_RETIRE` and surfaced if extended by suppression.
- **Content forward secrecy for past messages is unaffected**: `RS` is an HKDF-Expand sibling of the `ss0` extraction and cannot regenerate `RK0`, resting on HMAC one-wayness as a named assumption plus #282's KATs as the structural oracle — not on the distinctness test the comment currently cites (#282). The no-silent-content-regression guarantee rests on the not-persisted-ratchet invariant `0691a7a` (A4.9).
- **Replay**: an accepted generation is inert; a superseded-root frame causes a transition only if its bytes are novel — a byte-identical replay of an already-processed frame changes no state and raises no alarm (A4.5), so a co-host who can only re-serve captured bytes induces nothing. Wholesale rollback to a consistent earlier at-rest record is not detected by the store (no self-contained store can); it surfaces on the fresh content the rolled-back party must eventually send (row 7′) and is otherwise covered by the at-rest totality clause.
- **Metadata**: reconnect *emissions* carry no restart-conditioned cross-record dependence, because no emission is caused by a restart or by an attacker-presentable inbound value (A4.2/A4.3/A4.4); the residual signals are named, not waved at — a jittered one-bit-per-window re-arm correlation (A4.2) and a passive synchronized-cessation shadow of downtime (A4.4). **A3.9's grouping-signature residual is reopened** — its corrected classification is a cross-plane join from the anonymous page plane to the identified lobby plane, available passively from the cessation shadow and worst at alpha scale, and its acceptance is an open ratification line-item for the maintainer (A4.7), no longer settled. Nothing touches the doorbell, so the per-victim censorship surface stays closed.

### The divergence table, A4 deltas

A3.15's table stands except where A4.1 and A4.5 change an outcome. The changed and added rows:

| # | State pair | What happens | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
