| 4′ | Initiator absent past `T_RETIRE` after responder committed `RS_{n+1}` and emitted `RE-ACK` | responder retires `RS_n` but keeps the accepted handshake's `RE-ACK` on a standing slow re-seed until its confirming observation (A4.1); the returning initiator sweeps `b2a`, collects the standing `RE-ACK`, and completes — no responder reaction to any inbound frame; if the correspondence was deleted, the sweep finds nothing and the initiator surfaces a distinct loud peer-unreachable state offering fresh first contact | recovering at any delay by pull; loud-and-user-recoverable only on deliberate deletion — never terminal-and-silent (was B-CRYPTO-1) |
| 7′ | One party rolled back to a consistent `RS_{n-k}` | it speaks sub-generation frames; the *peer state regressed* alarm fires on a byte-novel authenticated frame (A4.5), which a genuine rollback produces (fresh nonce) and a co-host replay cannot; the store detects wholesale rollback nowhere else | loud on a genuine rollback's fresh content, inert on a replay; recovery is fresh first contact |

Row 4′ replaces A3.15 row 4's suppression sub-case with the availability-safe standing-re-seed retirement; row 7′ replaces row 7's alarm condition with byte-novelty. Every other row stands as A3.15 wrote it, and no row is silent or terminal-and-silent.

### Preconditions and verification obligations, A4 additions

Carried from A3, still owed, plus new obligations each to be shown able to fail:

- **Retirement availability (A4.1):** a responder that retires `RS_n` at `T_RETIRE` still keeps `RE-ACK` re-seeding on a standing cadence; a returning-at-any-delay initiator completes by sweeping and collecting it, with no responder write caused by the initiator's return; deliberate correspondence deletion lands the returning initiator in a distinct loud peer-unreachable state, never a silent spinner.
- **No attacker-driven writes (A4.2):** a co-host rolling back a page value to captured `RE-EST` bytes produces no write on any other record; the responder's `RE-ACK` re-seeds only on its own standing schedule; the one remaining sweep-driven re-arm advances at most once per replayed byte-string (clear-header monotone gate) and dispatches jittered-and-coalesced.
- **First-dispatch and overdue-schedule jitter (A4.3):** a re-establish leg's first emission is due at a jittered delay, not at `now`; and a device restarted after downtime re-draws every overdue persisted schedule forward, producing no synchronized drain instant across its records.
- **Cross-record emission independence + priced cessation (A4.4):** the restart-conditioned probe cannot beat chance at recovering a restart instant from what a device *wrote*; the cessation shadow is measured and reported as a residual, not claimed closed; the backoff delay carries no computable dependence on restart history.
- **Byte-novelty predicate (A4.5):** a byte-identical replay of a processed superseded-root frame changes no state and raises no alarm; a byte-novel genuine-rollback frame (fresh nonce at a reused position) alarms.
- **Leg-key direction+attempt binding (A4.6):** the two parties derive distinct `K(RS_n, gen, attempt, leg, dir)` under simultaneous initiation, and a re-initiation at an un-advanced generation derives a distinct key from its predecessor.
- **Store (A4.8):** `next_send_seq` is a persisted monotonic field (not derived from entries) and survives a crash mid-compose without reissue or hole; `S_pc` and the peer's `PK_pc` are present after restart and `msg_sig` both signs and verifies; the clear ratchet generation persists per-send and never regresses; file names are seal-derived, blob sizes are fixed-bucket (or bundled), so a keyless observer reads no identifier and no pending-count; a crash in the provisional→resume transition resolves to the resume record under a stated read order; no anti-rollback counter is claimed to detect what a self-contained store cannot.

### On ratification

The ISC-C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25 family gains criteria for A4.1–A4.10 and the obligations above, superseding the A3.1–A3.16 criteria wherever A4 revised a decision (A4.1/2/3/4/5/6/8/10) and keeping the A3 criteria for every decision A4 carried unchanged. #262 closes citing A4; #236's contact-cache scope line points at A3.14's record family as A4.8 corrects it; #279's persisted-`surfaced`-flag question and #281's store shape are answered by A3.14 + A4.8 and stay open as the build work they track. **The one decision A4 does not make and puts to the maintainer is A4.7: whether the corrected A3.9 residual is accepted as-is or spent against** — now sized as a cross-plane join available *passively* from the downtime cessation shadow (A4.4), sharpened but not created by the emission-side discriminators, worst at alpha scale; the only lever that reaches the cessation floor is network scale, and A4.8's bundled-blob decision is the adjacent at-rest question. Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A5 — channel re-establishment: the revision after A4 (supersedes A4; ~~PENDING ratification~~ ~~stress-tested and REFUTED in part — A6 pending~~ **superseded by A6, below**)

> **Stress-tested 2026-07-30 by an adversarial review on three axes (crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, store/erasure). REFUTED in part; the spine held on every axis, so an A6 revision follows — not a redesign.** No BLOCKERs and no mechanism breaks — the findings are narrower than the A4 round's, all clause-level seams, named forks, or doc-scope. The surviving core (bounded re-attempt, the symmetric confirmation-locked supersede, the store's hard/soft split, local-timer retry, per-record jitter) passed every axis. Three themes failed: **(1) the availability guarantee overreaches** — A5.8's "recovers whenever both overlap online" reaches only overlaps *during a live, confirmable attempt*: the last-leg confirmation must accept any openable gen-(n+1) frame not just RE-CONFIRM (M-A5-A), inter-attempt backoff leaves dead windows (M-A5-B), and the `attempt` trial-scan cap fights availability against DoS (F-A5-1); **(2) the store split under-specified its cross-record disciplines** — the bundled-record write critical-section must be read-modify-write-under-lock or the cardinal silent-send-loss returns (F-A5-2), a frame-record `next_send_seq` subset-rollback is silent (F-A5-3), and the dedup set both re-induces a loud false alarm on rollback (F-A5-4) and re-commits a milder F1 inside the resume record (F-A5-5); **(3) the A5.6 residual reopens larger and now coupled** — attempt COUNT is a keyless, single-record, downtime-monotone discriminator not dilutable by network scale (F-META-1), and capping re-initiations to bound it directly trades against availability, so A5.6 is now a where-to-set-the-cadence call, not accept-or-spend. **Do not build against this section.**

> **A revision, not a redesign.** The A4 stress round (three independent axes: crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, store/erasure) refuted A4 in part while confirming its spine on every axis, and its headline was a cross-axis convergence: all three axes independently refuted **A4.1's recovery mechanism**. A5 fixes the six clusters that round named, carries A4's confirmed decisions forward unchanged, and renumbers nothing it does not touch — resolve any A4.k or A3.k by reading it unless an A5.j says otherwise. Decision IDs are `A5.1`–`A5.8`, a namespace nothing else uses. **A stress round is still expected before ratification; written to be attacked.**

> **The load-bearing constraint that shaped A5, verified against the frozen design, not assumed.** The tempting fix for A4.1's recovery — gate the re-seed on the peer's lobby presence — is **forbidden**: the frozen design (§ *Established contact*, doc line 118) states *"presence is a stopping condition, never a sending gate — gating sending on presence would reproduce the constraint this design removes,"* and findings M16/B5 show presence is unsound as a delivery-correctness signal (no anti-replay, #78). So A5 recovers **without** presence: it does not gate any emission on presence, and it treats the lobby's world-readable presence only as the residual cost A5.6 prices, never as a mechanism. The one idea that resolves the deepest cluster is **bounded re-attempt inside the `RS_n` retention window, on a local per-record-jittered cadence, with ordinary give-up per attempt** — no standing never-give-up stream, no inbound-triggered re-arm, no presence dependence.

> **A pre-flight adversarial review on three axes (crypto/availability, metadata, store) ran against a first draft of A5 and its findings were folded before this section landed.** All three refuted-in-part and all three confirmed the spine and every mechanism. The recurring fault they caught — the same "store and crypto written by different hands" pattern the A4 round named — was A5.4's first draft filing two *hard* crypto-state fields (the A5.3 dedup memory and the clear-generation continuity counter) in the record it designed to be *volatile/self-healing*; a crash there would have reopened MAJOR-CRYPTO-3 and generation-regression. The fold re-homes all hard state (counters, dedup memory, slots, keys, a write-once `superseded_at_ms`, a durable retained-but-stopped flag) to the durable resume record, leaving only the re-seed schedule soft. Other folds: A5.1's supersede rule made symmetric and confirmation-locked with the initiator binding completion to its current `attempt` (a concurrent-re-attempt split-brain, B-A5-1); the retry cadence drawn from per-record-independent jitter, no shared tick (a cross-record phase-lock, M-A5-1); the `attempt` trial-scan capped and persisted-ahead-of-emission (M-A5-4/5); the dedup key widened to include `attempt` (M-A5-6); the bundled frame record given an explicit single-writer discipline (a last-writer-wins compose drop, S3); and A5.6's residual honestly re-sized *upward* — M3 is reduced-not-closed and the floor is the whole downtime signature (cessation and resumption), reach-extended to quiet channels (M-A5-1/2/3). **A full stress round is still expected before ratification; written to be attacked.**

**The survivor set A5 does not relitigate** (confirmed sound across the A4 round's three axes): the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped idempotent sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler indistinguishability, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto soundness, A4.2's causal-edge removal, A4.3's first-dispatch + overdue-load jitter and trickle-deletion, A4.6's `dir` KDF input, and A4.8's `next_send_seq`-in-outbox / `S_pc`+`PK_pc` enumeration / anti-rollback-counter deletion. These carry from A4 verbatim.

### A5.1 — recovery is bounded re-attempt inside the retention window, not a standing re-seed (closes B-A4-1, store F3, metadata M3, metadata M1)

A4.1's "standing `RE-ACK` re-seed until confirmation, recover by passive pull" was refuted three ways at once: deleted-peer and long-absent-peer are wire-indistinguishable so no single trigger is both recover-at-any-delay and loud-on-deletion (B-CRYPTO-1); the single acceptance slot is evictable during an unbounded absence window (store F3); and a re-seed that never gives up is a single-record discriminator (metadata M3). A5 replaces the standing stream with **bounded re-attempt**:

- **Each re-establishment attempt is an ordinary handshake with the ordinary 7-day give-up.** Its legs re-seed on the ordinary ladder and stop at give-up like any message — so there is no never-give-up stream and M3's distinctive shape does not exist. The responder persists the accepted attempt's sealed `RE-ACK` in its `(gen, attempt)` slot for the whole retention window (seal-once, A3.3, requires the bytes be stored to re-serve byte-identically), but *persisting the slot is not re-seeding it* — the wire emission follows the ordinary give-up; the slot's persistence only enables recovery, it does not put bytes on the wire past give-up.
- **The channel re-attempts while reconnect intent persists, bounded by `RS_n` retention (`T_RETIRE`, from the responder's first commit).** A returning initiator whose prior attempt gave up does not need the responder to have kept re-seeding; it **re-initiates** — a fresh attempt (new `attempt`, A5.2; fresh ephemeral), which supersedes the responder's prior *unconfirmed* candidate `RS_{n+1}`. The supersede rule is **symmetric and confirmation-locked on both sides**, because an asymmetric version split-brains: (i) an *unconfirmed* `RS_{n+1}` may be superseded by a newer attempt's; (ii) a *confirmed* one (RE-CONFIRM received) is locked, and a later-arriving lower-or-stale attempt's `RE-EST` never supersedes it; (iii) the **initiator binds completion to its own current `attempt`** and discards any `RE-ACK` (or root derived from one) whose `attempt` is below current — otherwise a late `RE-ACK` from a superseded attempt would let the initiator lock a root the responder has already discarded, stranding both on different siblings. Because the responder holds `RS_n` for the whole window it can answer any attempt inside it; because `superseded_at_ms` is write-once at the first commit (A5.4), re-attempts never extend the exposure window. **Reconnect intent persists to `T_RETIRE`, not to the first 7-day give-up**: a channel with undelivered mail or a standing reconnect flag keeps re-attempting (each attempt an ordinary 7-day handshake) across the whole retention window, so recovery reaches any overlap of both parties online inside `T_RETIRE`, not only inside the first give-up.
- **The re-attempt is driven by a local timer, never by an inbound frame, and its inter-attempt delay is drawn from wide per-record-independent jitter — no shared tick.** A4.1/A3.8's "fresh unopenable frames re-arm the handshake" was an inbound-triggered write — metadata M1's few-shot oracle (a co-host feeds captured frames at ascending clear generations to drive one re-arm each); making retry a local timer removes that edge entirely. But a *shared* local tick across a client's channels would phase-lock their re-attempt ladders and link the records (WB-3 I6's forbidden stable phase relationship), so each channel's retry delay is drawn independently, per-record, from a wide band — the same I6 discipline A4.3 applied to re-seeds, now applied to re-attempts. No inbound value, forged or replayed, drives a write, and no cross-record phase relationship is created.
- **Beyond `T_RETIRE` the sibling root is retired and recovery degrades to fresh first contact (the doorbell), surfaced loud.** This is honest: the crypto window genuinely closed, and extending recovery past `T_RETIRE` would extend the exposure a stale copy enjoys — the stated tradeoff, not a defect. Fresh first contact after a full retention-window absence is the *genuine-loss* path the ambition call permits, not the every-restart correspondent-action it forbade.
- **Deleted-peer and long-absent-peer are wire-indistinguishable, and A5 says so rather than pretending a trigger separates them.** From the initiator's side, "the responder deleted the correspondence" and "the responder was offline the whole window" both present as no `RE-ACK`, and both correctly resolve to *peer unreachable → fresh first contact* at `T_RETIRE` — because the channel genuinely cannot resume in either case. Loudness is sourced from **retention-window expiry with no completion**, not from a per-attempt give-up that also fires for transient absence. This removes the false-peer-unreachable A4.1 raised for a merely-absent peer.

The `(gen, attempt)` slot (store F3) holds only the current attempt; a newer attempt supersedes it (the old attempt is dead — the initiator moved on), so there is no unbounded slot set and no "which RE-ACK survives" ambiguity: the current attempt's, always.

### A5.2 — `attempt` is persisted and trial-scanned, never a clear field (closes B-A4-2)

A4.6 put `attempt` in the leg KDF but A4.8 never persisted it, so after a crash it reset to 0 and a re-initiation reused a key whose predecessor's bytes were still co-host-replayable — B-CRYPTO-2 through A4's own door. A5 persists the sender's own `attempt` as a monotonic per-(correspondence, direction) counter in the resume record (A5.4), advancing on every `RE-EST` seal and **persisted ahead of emission** (commit-then-emit, so a crash never reuses an `attempt`). **Conveyance is by bounded trial decryption, not a clear field:** the receiver holds `RS_n`, knows `gen`/`leg`/`dir` from the frame, and trial-decrypts over a bounded `attempt` window `[last_seen, last_seen + MAX_GAP]`, rejecting anything beyond — the cap is required because A3.13's backoff bounds the re-initiation *rate* but not the *cumulative* count over a long absence, so an uncapped window would let a junk frame force a full-window scan (a DoS amplifier, not a crypto break). A clear `attempt` field was the other option and is rejected: it would be a new reconnect-count discriminator feeding A5.6's residual. Bounded trial cost leaks nothing.

### A5.3 — the byte-novelty dedup memory is persisted and retention-scoped (closes B-A4-3)

A4.5's byte-novelty predicate (a superseded-root frame causes a transition only if its bytes are novel) named "the design's existing idempotent-processing dedup," but no such receive-side memory exists — the frozen replay defense is single-use `MessageKey` consumption (`ratchet.rs:436-439`), which does not cover handshake-leg keys (re-derivable from retained `RS_n`, never consumed). A5.4 homes a **durable, per-position processed-handshake-frame dedup memory in the resume record** — durable, not soft/self-healing, because a lost entry is a torn *security* invariant (a co-host re-serves captured `RE-EST` bytes into an empty dedup → byte-novel → re-fires the *peer-state-regressed* alarm, reopening MAJOR-CRYPTO-3's inducibility). Its key is **`(gen, attempt, leg, dir, seq)`** — `attempt` is load-bearing, because two legitimate different-attempt `RE-EST`s share `gen`/`seq` (the AAD is attempt-blind, `frame.rs:286-288`; only the KDF differs), so a position-only key would collide them and wrongly drop a legitimate frame. Its lifetime is the **retention window, gated on *actual* `RS_n` retirement, not a fixed 14-day duration**: byte-novelty only matters while `RS_n` is retained (once retired, a superseded-root frame cannot open and cannot alarm regardless), and tying eviction to the write-once `superseded_at_ms` (A5.4) rather than a nominal constant keeps a still-live `RS_n` from ever pairing with an emptied dedup. The set is small (a few handshake frames per attempt, backoff-bounded).

### A5.4 — the store splits the immutable frame record from the volatile state record (closes store F1, F2)

A4.8's single "outbox blob" conflated immutable sealed frames (fat, `PAD_BUCKETS` 8192/16384 each) with the volatile `ReseedSchedule{rung, next_due_ms}` that changes on every re-seed, so `replace_atomically` rewrote the whole ≈8 KB×N blob to update 12 bytes → O(8 KB·N²) write volume per drain, and a fixed-bucket size was impossible for an O(N) blob (store F1/F2). A5 splits the store into:

- **An immutable sealed-frame record**, bundled per profile, rewritten only on message lifecycle events (compose / collect / give-up) — never on re-seed. Bundling per profile removes the per-correspondence pending-count leak A4.8's per-file sizes exposed (F2); it is affordable precisely because, with the schedule split out, it no longer churns on re-seed. It carries `next_send_seq` per direction (A4.8's B-ERAS-1 fix — explicit stored field, so compose stays single-blob and the counter never regresses under GC). **Because it is one shared blob under whole-blob `replace_atomically`, all writes to it are serialized through a single-writer discipline** (one atomic-replace helper, per-profile lock) so two channels composing near-simultaneously cannot last-writer-wins-drop each other's freshly-composed frame (the #246 registry-lock hazard, which per-correspondence files did not have); the coarse per-correspondence-record fallback with padding is the alternative if that serialization proves too heavy.
- **A small volatile state record**, rewritten freely on re-seed, carrying **only** per-entry `{rung, next_due_ms}` — genuinely soft state: a frame present with no schedule defaults to rung-0-due-now and is immediately drawn forward by A4.3's overdue-load jitter, so a crash that writes a frame without its schedule self-heals with no torn hard invariant. **Nothing security-load-bearing lives here** — the A4 round's recurring failure was mixing hard crypto state with losable soft state, and A5 does not repeat it.
- **The resume record** (durable, commit-then-emit) carries the roots (`RS_{n+1}`, retained `RS_n` + a **write-once** `superseded_at_ms` stamped at the *first* supersede so `T_RETIRE` cannot slide forward per re-attempt), `reconnect_gen`, the `(gen, attempt)` handshake slots with their sealed leg bytes **and a durable retained-but-stopped flag** (so emission-eligibility past give-up is never *inferable from* a losable schedule — closing the path where a lost schedule would resurrect a past-give-up re-seed), the `attempt` counter (A5.2, persisted ahead of emission), `S_pc` + the peer's `PK_pc` (A4.8's MAJOR-CRYPTO-4 fix), the **clear ratchet-generation continuity counter** per direction (A4.7 — hard state, homed here not in the volatile record, persisted ahead of emission by commit-then-emit so it never regresses), the **A5.3 dedup memory** (durable — a lost entry is a torn *security* invariant, not a self-healing one), `previously_established`, and the peer's long-term identity key + cached EK.

Single-authority (A3.14) is preserved: each atomic invariant lives in one record (compose = frame-record write with its seq; a re-seed = volatile-record write; a handshake commit or a ratchet-generation advance = resume-record write), and the only cross-record relationship — the dead-chain sweep deriving from `reconnect_gen` — remains an idempotent load-time derivation. The rule this makes explicit, and the one A5's first draft got wrong: **hard state (roots, counters, dedup memory, slots, keys, flags) lives in the durable resume record; only the re-seed schedule is soft/volatile.** A crash between a ratchet-generation advance (resume record) and the frame seal (frame record) burns a generation with no frame — benign only because a skipped clear generation is peer-tolerated (a stated precondition the split makes load-bearing). The bundled frame record's aggregate size leaks only total pending across all correspondents, not per-correspondent counts; the split does leave two files with distinct churn rates, an at-rest/sync-metadata surface (relevant to a Syncthing-style backup observer reading mtimes without contents) that is aggregate-only and subsumed by A5.8's at-rest totality, accepted rather than padded.

### A5.5 — cause-2 first dispatch draws a wide reconnect-cadence delay (closes metadata M2)

A4.2's cause 2 (a pending unsealed outbox entry re-establishes its channel) is gated on the restart by construction — an entry composed offline to a quiet peer can only first-dispatch after reboot — so k such entries first-dispatch in a batch at t₀=boot, and A4.3's rung-0 (minute-scale) jitter around that shared t₀ satisfies I6's letter and fails its purpose (B-META-2's shape). A5 draws cause-2 first dispatches from a **wide randomized delay at reconnect-cadence scale (hours), not `RESEED_JITTER_FRAC`**, so a boot with k pending entries spreads their first `RE-EST`s across hours rather than bunching them. This does not gate on presence (the entry still goes to the DHT for an offline peer to collect) and does not break store-and-forward; it only decorrelates the first-dispatch batch from the boot instant. (Ordinary content re-seeds — cause 1 — remain on their persisted ladder with A4.3's overdue-load re-jitter, unchanged.)

### A5.6 — the A4.7 residual, re-sized smaller and reframed for the maintainer's call (folds metadata M3, M4, M5)

The A4 round corrected the residual on three axes, and net it is **smaller and more acceptable** than A4 stated:

- **M3 is reduced, not closed — and A5 prices what remains rather than overclaiming.** A5.1 deletes A4.1's past-give-up standing re-seed (its specific never-give-up stream is genuinely gone), but two weaker discriminators remain and are priced here, not waved away: (a) a **multi-attempt shape** — an established channel emits no handshake legs and a first contact emits one ladder, so *two or more* ordinary handshake ladders on one record across the retention window still read as a reconnect, at lower duty cycle than A4.1's stream but not zero; (b) a **cross-record phase risk** that A5.1's per-record-independent retry jitter (no shared tick) is written specifically to defeat — without it, phase-locked re-attempt ladders would link a client's records (WB-3 I6). (a) is a priced residual; (b) is closed by the jitter discipline.
- **M4 — correct classification, and it is also a reach-extender.** Frozen F1 (doc line 292) is *already* a cross-plane join (channel write-rhythm ∩ the lobby's world-readable online-windows → de-anonymizes both endpoints). The reconnect residual does not open a new plane — so M4's "F1 family, not a new class" holds — but it does more than sharpen: F1's continuous-rhythm form needs sustained channel traffic to observe, whereas the reconnect's single high-selectivity restart instant works on a **mostly-silent channel F1 proper cannot touch**, extending the F1 join's *reach* to quiet channels. Still F1-family, incremental on a class already accepted (A3.9), but materially so at alpha scale where the vantage is cheapest (S5).
- **M5 — the floor is the whole downtime signature, cessation *and* resumption, and it is unreachable in MVP.** Cause-1 sealed entries re-seed regardless of chain state, so a device offline shows a synchronized **cessation** of its re-seed streams across records, and on reboot a **resumption** whose leading edge dates the boot and whose burst density is monotone in downtime length (A5.5's wide-delay spread dissolves the tight-t₀ *batch* but not this resumption envelope). Network scale does **not** touch either signal — it only dilutes the probability an adversary holds the both-records vantage; only an always-on relay/pinning node (D1, deferred out of MVP) masks the downtime signature itself. So the cross-plane-join **floor is the full downtime signature and is unreachable in MVP**, priced honestly.

**The decision A5 puts to the maintainer (A4.7 reopened) — and it is honestly a larger residual than A4 first stated.** The classification is favorable (F1-family, incremental on a class already accepted, M4), but the pre-flight corrected the size upward on two axes: M3 is *reduced-not-closed* (the multi-attempt shape survives, M-A5-1), and the floor is the *whole downtime signature including resumption, reach-extended to quiet channels* (M-A5-2/3), not just cessation. So the call is: accept a reconnect residual that is an F1-family cross-plane join — pseudonymous-record-to-candidate-identity linkage plus restart timing, no content, no certain identity except at alpha scale — whose irreducible floor (the downtime signature) no v1 lever reaches, only a deferred pinning node; or spend against it. The facts still lean toward acceptance (it is F1-family and the mechanism discriminators are jittered or gone), but on a residual the pre-flight sized larger than A4 claimed. This is the maintainer's call; A5 records it as an open ratification line-item, not resolved.

### A5.7 — doc and precondition debts discharged (folds F4, F5, m1, m2, MINOR-A4-5)

- **F4 — resume-first is real rewiring plus a teardown-taxonomy change, not a doc delta.** `restart()` keys the decision on the provisional record and returns `HandshakeResumes` on any openable provisional (`provisional.rs:800-807`); `TeardownCause::NoProvisionalRecord` deliberately conflates "established (record deleted)" with "lost" (`:754-758`). Resume-first authority forces the distinction — `Ok(None)` on provisional must branch on resume presence (present → established, resume authority; absent → genuinely lost) — and `restart()`'s signature must carry the resume record's presence, not only the provisional's. Named as build work, and as a taxonomy change to #243.
- **F5a is resolved by A5.1's mechanism, not reworded.** A4.10's "a stale copy confers nothing beyond `T_RETIRE`" was falsified by A4.1's key-free byte-comparison recognition kept live past the ceiling. A5.1 has no past-`T_RETIRE` recognition — beyond the ceiling `RS_n` is retired and nothing recognizes or answers — so the clause is now simply true, no carve-out needed.
- **F5b — subset-rollback across the blob family.** The store is now three-plus records, each independently sealed, so a subset-rollback (resume reverted, frame record current) is a state that never legitimately existed. A5.8 states it explicitly: **no subset-rollback across the record family is silent** — a reverted resume record surfaces as a peer that cannot open current traffic (loud, re-establish), and the single-authority load-time derivation (A5.4) re-derives the outbox facts from the authoritative `reconnect_gen`, so a mixed-version family is repaired or surfaced, never silently trusted.
- **m1 — the read→write edges enumerated with their closers.** (i) inbound `RE-EST` → the responder composes `RE-ACK`: on the responder's own schedule after processing, commit-then-emit, not same-turn — and A5.1's retry is a local cadence, so no inbound frame drives a write; (ii) collect `RE-ACK` → the initiator composes `RE-CONFIRM`: the initiator's own action after a successful open, closed by A4.5 byte-novelty (a replayed `RE-ACK` is inert); (iii) contest-answer on an inbound `RE-EST`: closed by A3.4 accepted-generation inertness plus the coin. No remaining edge is driven by an attacker-presentable value.
- **m2 — cessation and resumption are separate residual edges.** A4.4 conflated them; A5.6 prices cessation (the unspreadable floor) and resumption (spread by A4.3's overdue-load re-jitter) as distinct, so the join's timing precision is not overstated.
- **MINOR-A4-5 — the asymmetric `T_RETIRE` clocks are moot.** Recovery is bounded by `T_RETIRE` from the responder's first commit (a single anchor), and no party's recovery depends on the other's clock; each clock bounds only its own exposure. The asymmetry the A3 round flagged has no availability consequence under A5.1.

### A5.8 — the honest security property, revised (supersedes A4.10)

- **An at-rest compromise is total for the relationship, and the ratchet does not heal it** — the attacker holds the pseudonym signing key, the resume roots, and the addressing root; it can reconnect first and its authority self-renews. Unchanged; survived every axis.
- **Availability: a peer's absence up to `T_RETIRE` costs latency and loud states, never the relationship; absence beyond `T_RETIRE` degrades to fresh first contact, surfaced loud — never silent.** This is A5.1's honest bound. Within the retention window, bounded re-attempt recovers whenever both parties overlap online; beyond it, the sibling root is retired and recovery is a fresh first contact — the genuine-loss path, not a silent spinner. Deleted-peer and long-absent-peer are wire-indistinguishable and both resolve to this same loud outcome.
- **The silent-lockout harm is mitigated, not cured** — the evicted rightful holder's next attempt lands loud (A3.15 row 7 / A5's divergence rows), recovery is fresh first contact.
- **A stale copy confers nothing beyond `T_RETIRE`** — true without carve-out (A5.7/F5a): there is no past-ceiling recognition.
- **Content forward secrecy for past messages is unaffected** — `RS` is an HKDF-Expand sibling of the `ss0` extraction and cannot regenerate `RK0`, resting on HMAC one-wayness + #282's KATs; the no-silent-content-regression guarantee rests on the not-persisted-ratchet invariant `0691a7a` (A4.9).
- **Replay**: an accepted generation is inert; a superseded-root frame causes a transition only if its bytes are novel (A4.5), backed by the persisted retention-scoped dedup memory (A5.3); no self-contained store detects wholesale rollback, and **no subset-rollback across the record family is silent** (A5.7/F5b).
- **Metadata**: no emission is caused by a restart or by an attacker-presentable inbound value (A4.2/A5.1/A5.5); the reconnect residual is a selectivity amplifier on the already-accepted F1 cross-plane join with an MVP-unreachable cessation floor (A5.6), reopened for the maintainer's acceptance; nothing touches the doorbell on a routine path, so the per-victim censorship surface stays closed.

### The divergence table, A5 deltas

A3.15's table stands except where A5.1 changes an outcome:

| # | State pair | What happens | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4″ | Initiator absent then returns *within* `T_RETIRE` after the responder committed `RS_{n+1}` | the prior attempt gave up ordinarily; the initiator re-initiates (new `attempt`, A5.2), superseding the responder's unconfirmed candidate; the responder (still holding `RS_n`) answers; both complete | recovering whenever both overlap online inside the window |
| 4‴ | Initiator absent *beyond* `T_RETIRE`, or the responder deleted the correspondence | no completion by the ceiling; `RS_n` retired (or slot GC'd); the channel surfaces peer-unreachable and offers fresh first contact | loud at `T_RETIRE`; deleted and long-absent are indistinguishable and both land here (was B-CRYPTO-1) |
| 7″ | One party rolled back to a consistent earlier record (whole or subset of the family) | it speaks sub-generation frames; the *peer-state-regressed* alarm fires on a byte-novel authenticated frame (A4.5/A5.3); a subset-rollback surfaces as a peer that cannot open current traffic | loud on a genuine rollback's fresh content; no subset-rollback is silent (A5.7/F5b); recovery is fresh first contact |

Row 4″/4‴ replace A4's row 4′ with the bounded-re-attempt model; row 7″ extends row 7′ to the record family. No row is silent or terminal-and-silent.

### Preconditions and verification obligations, A5 additions

Carried from A4, plus, each to be shown able to fail:

- **Bounded re-attempt (A5.1):** an initiator returning inside `T_RETIRE` recovers by re-initiation without the responder having re-seeded past give-up; an unconfirmed `RS_{n+1}` is superseded by a newer attempt while a confirmed one is not; beyond `T_RETIRE` recovery is fresh first contact and is loud; deleted and long-absent both land in the same loud outcome; retry is a local cadence and no inbound frame drives a re-attempt write.
- **`attempt` persistence + trial-scan (A5.2):** `attempt` survives a crash monotone; the receiver opens the correct leg by a bounded attempt scan with no clear `attempt` field.
- **Dedup memory (A5.3):** a byte-identical replay of a processed handshake frame raises no alarm; an entry is safe to evict once its generation's `RS_n` retires; a restart preserves the memory across the retention window.
- **Store split (A5.4):** a re-seed rewrites only the volatile record, not the fat frame record; the bundled frame record's size reveals no per-correspondent pending count; compose stays single-blob; a frame written without its schedule self-heals via overdue-load jitter.
- **Cause-2 dispatch spread (A5.5):** a boot with k pending unsealed entries produces first `RE-EST`s spread across a reconnect-cadence window, not a t₀ batch.
- **Read-order + taxonomy (A5.7/F4):** a both-present crash resolves to the resume record; `Ok(None)` on provisional branches on resume presence.
- **Subset-rollback (A5.7/F5b):** no mixed-version record family is silently trusted.

### On ratification

The ISC-C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25 family gains criteria for A5.1–A5.8, superseding the A4 criteria wherever A5 revised a decision and keeping A4's for every decision carried unchanged. #262 closes citing A5; #236's contact-cache scope points at A5.4's record family; #279 and #281 are answered by A5.4 and stay open as build work. **The one decision A5 puts to the maintainer is A5.6: whether the reopened A4.7 residual — now sized as a selectivity amplifier on the already-accepted F1 cross-plane join, with an MVP-unreachable cessation floor — is accepted or spent against.** Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A6 — channel re-establishment: the revision after A5 (supersedes A5; ~~PENDING ratification~~ **stress-tested and REFUTED in part — superseded by A7, below**)

> **A revision, not a redesign.** The A5 stress round (three independent axes: crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, store/erasure) refuted A5 in part with **no BLOCKERs and no mechanism breaks** — the findings were all clause-level seams, named forks, or doc-scope, confirming the design is converging. A6 folds the three themes that round named, carries A5's confirmed spine forward, and renumbers nothing it does not touch. Decision IDs `A6.1`–`A6.4`, a namespace nothing else uses. **Written to be attacked; a stress round is still expected.**

> **The one-knob unification that shaped A6.** The A5 round's Theme 1 (availability) and Theme 3 (the attempt-count metadata leak) turn out to be the *same* knob: the **per-window re-initiation cap `C`** (equivalently, the inter-attempt gap `T_RETIRE/C`). `C` simultaneously (i) sets the receiver's trial-scan window `MAX_GAP = C` — resolving the availability-vs-DoS opposite-pull the round flagged (a peer absent the whole window returns to an attempt no further ahead than `C`, so a bounded `MAX_GAP` is also *sufficient*); (ii) bounds the leaked attempt-count integer a co-host can read (F-META-1); and (iii) via the inter-attempt gap versus the DHT eviction-linger, sets coverage continuity — the axis that makes "recovers on any overlap" true or not. So the availability↔metadata-leak curve is a single ratification-gated value, and A6 sets a documented default while marking it PENDING the maintainer (A6.3).

**The survivor set A6 does not relitigate** (confirmed across the A5 round's three axes): the bounded-re-attempt spine, the symmetric confirmation-locked supersede, the store's hard/soft re-homing (hard state in the durable resume record, only the re-seed schedule soft), `attempt` in the dedup key, eviction gated on actual `RS_n` retirement, the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped idempotent sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto, per-record retry jitter, A4.2's causal-edge removal, A4.3's jitter+trickle-deletion, A4.6's `dir`, and A4.8's `next_send_seq`/`S_pc`+`PK_pc`/anti-rollback-drop. These carry from A5 verbatim.

### A6.1 — the availability guarantee, corrected to what the mechanism delivers (closes M-A5-A, M-A5-B, F-A5-1)

A5.8's "recovers whenever both parties overlap online" over-claimed; A6 makes it true by construction and states it exactly:

- **Any openable generation-(n+1) frame confirms and locks the candidate — not only RE-CONFIRM (M-A5-A).** A5.1 defined the responder's lock on "RE-CONFIRM received," so an initiator that completed and then fell silent past RE-CONFIRM's give-up (while the responder was absent >7d, <`T_RETIRE`) left the responder stranded on an unconfirmed candidate that ordinary content would never resolve. A6: the first frame that opens under the re-rooted chain — content *or* RE-CONFIRM, whichever arrives — confirms the candidate and ends supersede-eligibility. This closes the responder-side non-completion split-brain (distinct from A5.1's concurrent-attempt one) with no new leg.
- **Re-attempts chain continuously across the window (M-A5-B).** Each attempt is an ordinary 7-day handshake, but the *next* attempt launches before the previous attempt's `RE-EST` evicts from the DHT (inter-attempt gap < the eviction-linger), so the `AR`-plane is never bare of a live, collectable `RE-EST` while reconnect intent persists to `T_RETIRE`. This is what makes "recovers on any overlap of both parties online inside `T_RETIRE`" a mechanical fact rather than a hope; A3.13's rate backoff still governs the *answering* side's participation, but the initiator's own coverage is continuous.
- **The receiver's trial-scan window is `MAX_GAP = C` and slides with observed traffic (F-A5-1).** Because the per-window attempt count is capped at `C` (A6.3), a peer absent the entire window returns to a sender whose `attempt` has advanced by at most `C`, so a bounded window `[last_seen, last_seen + C]` is both DoS-safe (junk beyond it is rejected) *and* availability-sufficient (the legitimate current attempt is always inside it). Where the receiver *does* observe attempts, `last_seen` advances, sliding the window forward. The availability-vs-DoS opposite-pull the round flagged dissolves because `C` bounds both ends.

### A6.2 — the store split's cross-record disciplines, specified (closes F-A5-2, F-A5-3, F-A5-4, F-A5-5)

The A5 split moved hard state correctly; A6 names the disciplines the split then requires:

- **The bundled frame record is written read-modify-write under the per-profile lock — not write-serialized (F-A5-2).** A5.4's "single-writer discipline" as worded serialized the *stores* but not the read→modify→write, so two channels composing/settling against the one bundled blob could last-writer-wins and silently drop a send — the cardinal invariant the outbox exists to protect (`outbox.rs:15-16`), and bundling *raises* writer concurrency on that blob. A6: the read, the modify, and the atomic-replace all execute inside one per-profile critical section (or an equivalent per-entry CAS/merge on replace). The throughput cost of serializing all compose/collect/give-up behind one lock is the explicit price of bundling, and it is what keeps the per-correspondence-record fallback (coarser count leak, no shared-blob contention) a live option — the bundle-vs-per-file fork is named here, defaulting to bundle-with-lock.
- **`next_send_seq` regression is detectable: a monotone high-water is mirrored into the resume record (F-A5-3).** A5 homed the authoritative seq only in the frame record, so a frame-record subset-rollback regressed it silently — the next compose reused a seq under the *current* (un-rolled-back) generation, landing above high-water and never tripping A4.5's generation-scoped alarm. A6 mirrors a monotone `next_send_seq` high-water into the resume record (a small counter, deliberately re-coupling what A5.4 split — the named cost), so the load-time derivation detects a frame-record seq regression and surfaces it loud. This is the one place A6 trades a little of the split's independence for closing a silent subset-rollback; the alternative (key the rollback alarm on `(gen, seq)` rather than `gen`) is recorded as the equivalent fix if the mirror proves awkward.
- **A5.3's dedup claim is scoped: it closes crash-*loss* inducibility, not rollback (F-A5-4).** A resume-record rollback regresses the dedup set, and a co-host re-serving handshake bytes now absent from the shrunk set raises a *false* peer-state-regressed alarm — loud (A5.7's letter holds), so not silent, but A5.3 over-claimed "closes MAJOR-CRYPTO-3 inducibility." A6 scopes it: crash-loss inducibility is closed; rollback inducibility surfaces as a loud false alarm, subsumed under the accepted "no self-contained store detects wholesale rollback."
- **The dedup set's co-location with roots/keys is named and accepted (F-A5-5).** Homing the churning dedup set in the resume record with the rarely-changing roots and keys means each handshake-frame insert rewrites them — a milder instance of the very immutable∩volatile anti-pattern A5.4 fixed for the outbox. It does not defeat the split (handshake-frame inserts are rare against re-seeds, the blob is small and bounded) and durability is required (a lost entry is a torn security invariant), so A6 accepts it explicitly rather than presenting the split as seam-free.

### A6.3 — the re-initiation cap `C`: the single ratification-gated knob, with a documented default (folds F-META-1, M-A5-B, F-A5-1; the A5.6 / Theme-3 residual)

Per the one-knob unification above, `C` (the per-`T_RETIRE`-window re-initiation cap, equivalently the inter-attempt gap) is a single ratification-gated value on the **availability ↔ metadata-leak curve**, and A6 refuses to pick it silently:

- **What a co-host reads (F-META-1, honestly).** A5.2 mints a fresh ephemeral per attempt, and ephemerals are clear on the wire and byte-identical within a generation (`frame.rs:22-29`), so distinct ephemeral-groups on one record = attempt count = a keyless, single-record integer monotone in downtime. It is **not** dilutable by network scale (no both-records vantage needed), so it is a genuine addition to the F1-family residual, not a member network growth suppresses. `C` is its ceiling.
- **The curve.** Small inter-attempt gap (large `C`) → continuous coverage, "recovers on any overlap" holds (A6.1), but up to `C` attempts are legible to a co-host. Wide gap (small `C`) → fewer attempts leak, but coverage develops holes (an overlap that falls in a gap misses, degrading toward the honest "recovers on any overlap *during a live attempt*"). The two ends are the availability-favoring and leak-favoring anchors.
- **The documented default, PENDING ratification.** A6 sets `C` at the **availability-favoring anchor — inter-attempt gap = the DHT eviction-linger, giving continuous coverage** and `C ≈ T_RETIRE / eviction-linger` — because it preserves the recovery UX the ambition call demanded and the residual is F1-family metadata (pseudonymous, no content, no identity except at alpha scale), consistent with the maintainer's prior alpha-residual acceptances. **This is a ratification GATE, not a settled value**: the arithmetic (`C` against the eviction-linger and the observed re-seed budget) is to be closed at the byte pass, and the maintainer may move `C` toward the leak-favoring anchor. The knob and its levers are named here rather than discovered in a build. (F-META-2/3 wording folded: A5.6(b)'s "phase-lock closed" is corrected to "phase-lock closed; residual rate-matching from a shared wide band folds into F1"; the frame/volatile churn-ratio is stated as an aggregate pending-age/backoff-distribution estimate, at-rest/sync-metadata only.)

### A6.4 — minor debts discharged, and the honest security property's availability clause reworded

- **RS_n zeroization precedes dedup eviction (m-A5-D).** Both are anchored to the write-once `superseded_at_ms`, and `RS_n` is zeroized no later than — and ordered before — the dedup set's eviction, so the eviction seam cannot pair a still-key-live `RS_n` with an emptied dedup set (which would re-admit a byte-novel replayed alarm).
- **The skipped-generation precondition is now cited, not asserted (m-A5-E).** A crash between the resume-record generation advance and the frame seal burns a clear generation; the receive path tolerates a forward generation gap by construction — a generation change drives skip-batches to the new generation (`ratchet.rs` § generation skip batches, and the `MissingCiphertext`/`UnknownEphemeral` guards that make an *unrootable* gap loud rather than silent). So a burned generation is skipped-over, not stalled-on; A6 records this as a verification obligation to exercise, no longer a bare assertion. (The dedup set's size is also corrected: across roughly one attempt per day over `T_RETIRE` it is tens of entries, not a handful — bounded, still small.)
- **The availability clause of the honest security property (A5.8) is reworded to the mechanism A6.1 delivers:** *within `T_RETIRE`, recovery reaches any overlap of both parties online, because re-attempts chain continuously (A6.1) up to the cap `C` (A6.3); beyond `T_RETIRE` the sibling root is retired and recovery degrades to fresh first contact, surfaced loud; a deleted correspondent and a long-absent one remain wire-indistinguishable and both resolve to that same loud outcome.* Every other clause of A5.8 stands.

### On ratification

The ISC family gains criteria for A6.1–A6.4, superseding the A5 criteria where A6 revised a decision and keeping A5's otherwise. #262 closes citing A6; #279/#281/#236 are answered by the A5.4 store shape as A6.2 disciplines it, and stay open as build work. **The one decision A6 puts to the maintainer is A6.3: the value of `C` on the availability↔metadata-leak curve** — A6 defaults it to the availability-favoring anchor (continuous coverage, `C ≈ T_RETIRE / eviction-linger` attempts legible), an F1-family residual, and marks it PENDING. Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A7 — channel re-establishment: the revision after A6 (supersedes A6; ~~PENDING ratification~~ **stress-tested and REFUTED in part — superseded by A8, below**)

> **A revision, not a redesign.** The A6 stress round (three independent axes: crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, store/erasure) refuted A6 in part with **no BLOCKERs and no mechanism breaks** — the fourth consecutive round where the spine held and the findings were clause-level refinements of the *previous* fix, not new problem areas. Cross-axis convergence was tight (two axes independently prescribed the identical fix in two places). A7 folds the five clusters; decision IDs `A7.1`–`A7.5`. **Written to be attacked; a stress round is still expected. The store fixes cite in-tree primitives to reuse, not invent.**

> **The recurring adversary this round was crash-before-emit**, and A7 treats it as one named case (A7.5): it decoupled the `attempt` counter from the leak-cap `C` (so `MAX_GAP=C` no longer held), and it falsified A6.4's skipped-generation discharge (a burned generation is permanently dead, not skipped). The other convergent theme: **A6.1's continuous chaining is not a free availability win — it is the maximally-legible absence beacon**, i.e. the max-leak end of the very `C` curve A6 exists to expose, so A7 corrects the framing and moves the default (A7.1, A7.3).

**Survivor set A7 does not relitigate** (held across the A6 round's three axes): the bounded-re-attempt spine, the symmetric confirmation-locked supersede, the store hard/soft split, `attempt` in the dedup key, eviction gated on actual `RS_n` retirement, the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto, per-record retry jitter, A6.1's last-leg fix (any openable gen-(n+1) frame confirms+locks), A6.4's `RS_n`-zeroize-before-dedup-eviction ordering, the one-knob `C` *unification*, and all carried A4/A5 items. These carry verbatim.

### A7.1 — the availability guarantee corrected on the answering side, and continuous chaining reframed as the leak end (closes crypto F-A6-1; folds metadata M-A6-META-1/3, store F-A6-4)

A6.1 made the *initiator's* coverage continuous but left the *answering* side gapped — A3.13's participation backoff and the responder's own-schedule RE-ACK latency mean a short overlap can pass with no completed round-trip. A7 states the guarantee to what the mechanism delivers on both sides: **recovery reaches any overlap of both parties online *long enough for a handshake round-trip, with the answering side not rate-limited out of it*.** The answer-side quiet window (A3.13) is bounded below the expected-overlap target as a ratification value, so the guarantee is mechanical rather than aspirational.

And A7 corrects A6.1's framing, which sold continuous chaining as a pure availability win: **continuous coverage is the maximally-legible absence beacon.** Keeping the `AR` plane never-bare of a live `RE-EST` for the whole absence gives a co-host of the `b2a`/`AR` record a steady periodic re-seed cadence whose onset edge timestamps "peer went offline," whose count×eviction-linger is a linear real-time downtime clock, and whose offset is the reconnect — and its onset *inverts* F1 (it joins the absent endpoint to lobby *offline* transitions, where F1 joins activity to *online* windows). So continuous coverage is not free: it is the max-leak end of the `C` curve (A7.3), and A7 says so where A6.1 did not.

### A7.2 — drop the `next_send_seq` mirror; key the rollback alarm on `(gen, seq)` (closes store F-A6-1, metadata m-A6-META-5, store F-A6-5)

Two review axes independently prescribed the same fix. A6.2's resume-record seq high-water mirror is self-contradictory — advanced per-send it is a hot two-blob non-atomic cross-record write (a crash leaves `frame.seq > mirror`, making a legitimate lag and an undetectable regression the same state, and reintroducing send-rhythm into a second record's write timing, a WB-3 I6 coupling); advanced lazily it lags below the real seq through any send burst, leaving the F-A5-3 rollback still silent. A7 **drops the mirror and keys the rollback alarm on `(gen, seq)`** — which detects a frame-only seq regression under an unchanged generation with no mirror, no two-blob send write, and no cross-record write-timing coupling. The residual — a *consistent* rollback of both seq-bearing records (frame + resume) together, with the volatile record current — is conceded to fold under the accepted "no self-contained store detects wholesale rollback" survivor: it is the whole seq-bearing family reverting, not a subset, so A5.7's blanket "no subset-rollback is silent" is narrowed to exclude it.

### A7.3 — `C` fully specified: persisted, protocol-global, and decoupled from the crash-inflatable `attempt` (closes store F-A6-3, metadata M-A6-META-2, crypto F-A6-2)

All three review axes hit the one knob; A7 gives it a complete contract:

- **Persistence (store F-A6-3).** `C` is enforced with a per-window count that must survive a crash, but `attempt` (A5.2) is monotone for the channel's whole lifetime, not per-window. A7 persists a **window-start anchor** (`attempt_at_window_start`) in the durable resume record; "attempts this window" = `attempt − attempt_at_window_start`. Both are in the atomically-replaced resume blob, so a crash cannot reset the toward-`C` count and let a crash-loop mint `>C` attempts.
- **Globality (metadata M-A6-META-2).** `C` is a **protocol-global constant, identical across all clients and deployments** — never a per-deployment setting. A per-client `C` is a network-scale-undilutable fingerprint (a co-host seeing two records both saturate at the same integer links them to one client config). "The knob the maintainer may move" means move-for-all-clients-in-a-release, never per-deployment; a global `C` leaks zero identifying bits beyond the count itself.
- **Decoupling from `attempt` (crypto F-A6-2).** Crash-before-emit advances `attempt` (persisted ahead of emit, A5.2) with no wire emission, so the attempt-*number* can exceed the leaked-*count* (~`C`) and the receiver's `[last_seen, last_seen+C]` window would reject a legitimate RE-EST (lockout) — the `MAX_GAP = C` identity is the casualty. A7 decouples them: the scan window is `MAX_GAP ≥ C + crash-inflation-bound` (equivalently, slid purely on the receiver's observed `last_seen`), while the *emitted*-attempt count remains the metadata ceiling. Advancing `attempt` only on emission is rejected — it reopens the B-A4-2 key-reuse the persist-ahead rule closed.
- **The default, on the number not the label (metadata M-A6-META-3).** A7 states the leaked-integer range in absolute terms — at the availability-favoring anchor (gap = eviction-linger), `C ≈ T_RETIRE / eviction-linger`, which for `T_RETIRE` on the 14-day order and a sub-day linger is **dozens to low hundreds** of legible re-attempts plus the continuous onset/duration beacon (A7.1). That is a high-resolution downtime measurement, not F1's binary rhythm-linkage — so A7 **moves the documented default toward the leak-favoring anchor** (a wider gap: fewer legible attempts, coverage that degrades to "recovers on any overlap during a live attempt" rather than truly continuous) and presents the maintainer the two anchors *with the absolute numbers*, PENDING his ratification. This is the A5.6/Theme-3 call, now quantified: the crux is the number, and A7 refuses to hide it behind "F1-family."

### A7.4 — cross-process locking and cite-and-reuse the in-tree store primitives (closes store F-A6-2)

A6.2's "per-profile critical section" is under-specified across processes: the GUI and TUI are separate binaries sharing one profile root (`storage/fetched.rs:174-208`), so an in-process `Mutex` does not serialize them and F-A5-2's cardinal silent-send-loss reopens the moment both front-ends compose. A7: the per-profile read-modify-write critical section is an **OS advisory `flock`** (the existing `IdxLock` pattern), and the frame-record write and any coupled resume-record write **share one lock acquisition** (no re-entrant deadlock, no independent write event). The atomic-replace primitive `A5.4` named as "~30 lines, unbuilt" **already exists in-tree and is validated** — `storage/fetched.rs:946-956` (tmp → write → rename) and the flock at `:174-208`; `profile/persist.rs:161-171` is a plain non-atomic clobber that must not be the DM store's writer. A7's store contract is therefore *reuse these*, not invent.

### A7.5 — crash-before-emit as one named case: reuse-not-skip generations, content post-commit (closes crypto F-A6-3, m-A6-1; corrects A6.4's premature m-A5-E discharge)

A6.4 cited the ratchet's skip-batch machinery to make a crash-burned clear generation "skipped-over, not stalled-on." That discharge was **wrong**: `advance_root` chains each root from the previous plus that generation's rooting ciphertext (`ratchet.rs:415-421`, `MissingCiphertext` `:294-299`), and the steady-state ratchet is non-resumable (`:90-109`). A genuinely burned generation `g` emits no frame → no `eph_ct` for `g` → the peer cannot derive `root_g` or `root_{g+1}` → `UnknownEphemeral`, permanently dead in that direction. Skip-batches skip *sequence numbers within reachable generations*, never the rooting of an untransmitted generation. So A7 establishes the correct invariant: **a crash reuses generation `g` — it re-emits `g` with `g`'s rooting ciphertext, never skips `g`** — which is exactly what commit-then-emit already implies (persist the derived root before emitting, and re-emit the same generation on recovery). The verification obligation exercises both branches (reuse recovers; a forced skip is shown to die loud via #243's teardown, not silently). Folded in as the same crash-before-emit case: **content of generation (n+1) reaches the wire only after its emitter commits that root** (m-A6-1) — the unstated invariant that makes A6.1's "any openable gen-(n+1) frame confirms+locks" forgery- and replay-safe (a co-host cannot present openable content under a root the initiator has not committed).

### On ratification

The ISC family gains criteria for A7.1–A7.5, superseding the A6 criteria where revised. #262 closes citing A7; the store shape (#279/#281/#236) is answered by A5.4 as A6.2/A7.2/A7.4 discipline it. **The one decision A7 puts to the maintainer is the value of `C` on the availability↔metadata-leak curve (A7.3), now quantified in absolute terms** — A7 moves the documented default toward the leak-favoring anchor (fewer legible re-attempts, coverage degrading to overlap-during-a-live-attempt) rather than A6's availability-favoring maximum, and presents both anchors with their leaked-integer numbers, PENDING ratification. Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A8 — channel re-establishment: the revision after A7 (supersedes A7; ~~PENDING ratification~~ **stress-tested and REFUTED in part — superseded by A9, below**)

> **A revision, not a redesign — and largely a collapse, not an addition.** The A7 stress round (three independent axes) refuted A7 in part with no BLOCKERs (the fifth consecutive round in which the spine held), but this round found two *real holes* alongside the pricing: A7 treated in-tree atomic-replace as a solved primitive when it is neither cross-file-atomic nor power-loss-durable, and A7.3's crash-inflation decoupling contradicted A7.5's reuse-not-skip. A8 folds four clusters; three of them *remove* mechanism (unify two crash rules into one, delete a decoupling, reframe a knob). Decision IDs `A8.1`–`A8.4`. **Written to be attacked.**

> **The headline is a reframe the maintainer needs before the `C` call (A8.3): the re-establishment onset beacon is an IRREDUCIBLE cost of reconnection-over-a-co-hostable-record, and `C` tunes its *resolution*, not whether it exists.** Any working reconnection emits a first `RE-EST` at the moment the peer comes back, which timestamps the offline→online transition to a co-host of both direction records — the F1 join run backwards over offline windows. No value of `C ≥ 1` removes it; `C = 0` removes it only by deleting the feature. So the decision A8 hands the maintainer is feature-level (accept the onset beacon, or drop reconnection-without-fresh-contact), and `C` within that is an availability↔downtime-*resolution* dial, not an anonymity dial.

**Survivor set A8 does not relitigate** (held across the A7 round's three axes): the bounded-re-attempt spine, the symmetric confirmation-locked supersede, the store hard/soft split, `attempt` in the dedup key, eviction on actual `RS_n` retirement, the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto, per-record retry jitter, A6.1's last-leg fix, A6.4's zeroize ordering, A7.2's `(gen,seq)` alarm keying, A7.3's globality of `C`, A7.1's guarantee reword + max-leak-beacon framing, A7.5's crypto *diagnosis* (a burned steady-state generation is permanently dead — only its grounding is re-scoped here), and all carried A4/A5 items. These carry verbatim.

### A8.1 — one crash-before-emit rule: reuse-not-skip covers the generation AND the `attempt` counter, which restores `MAX_GAP = C` (closes crypto F-A7-1, F-A7-2, F-A7-3; store F3)

A7 shipped two contradictory crash fixes: A7.3 decoupled the scan window from a crash-inflatable `attempt` (`MAX_GAP ≥ C + crash-inflation-bound`), while A7.5 said a crash *reuses* the committed step and re-emits it. They are the same problem, and reuse-not-skip is the stronger fix. A8 unifies them: **commit-then-emit persists both the re-establishment generation's root and the `attempt` counter before any emission, and recovery re-emits the committed values, advancing neither.** A crash before emit therefore causes zero inflation, so **A7.3's decoupling is deleted and `MAX_GAP = C` is restored** — the clean one-knob identity holds after all. Advancing `attempt` only on emission is still rejected (it reopens B-A4-2 key reuse); the fix is to persist-ahead *and* re-emit the same value, not to advance on restart.

Two scoping corrections A7.5's grounding got wrong:

- **Reuse-not-skip is scoped to the re-establishment generation, grounded in atomic persist-before-emit on the resume record — not the steady-state ratchet.** The steady-state ratchet has no serialization surface (`ratchet.rs:90-109`): commit-then-emit delivers nothing there, so a steady-state crash is a full loud teardown + re-establish (it cannot skip *because it fully resets*, a different argument from reuse). Only the re-establishment step, whose root `RS_n` is re-derivable from the durable resume record, can reuse-and-re-emit.
- **"Byte-identical re-seed" is dropped for the re-establishment legs; a fresh `attempt`-keyed re-encapsulation is correct.** ML-KEM encapsulation is randomized, so a re-emit under the committed `attempt` re-encaps freshly; that is dedup-safe (the dedup key includes `attempt`, A5.3) and does not reopen the nonce premise (each `attempt` is a distinct key namespace). And a peer-observable *skip* has no peer-side detector — a burned re-establishment generation surfaces only through the initiator's own recovery (re-emit), not as a teardown the peer sees; #243 teardown is local-on-our-restart (`provisional.rs:795-808`), the peer sees silence. A8 states this rather than claiming the peer catches a skip.

### A8.2 — the store's real atomicity and durability contract (closes store F1, F2, F4, F5; crypto m-A7-1)

A7.4 mistook mutual exclusion for atomicity and cited a non-durable primitive. A8 gives the store the contract it actually needs:

- **The coupled re-establishment fields live in ONE blob, written by ONE atomic replace — flock is not atomicity.** A7.4's "frame write and resume write share one flock acquisition" serializes processes but leaves two `rename` syscalls; a crash between them tears the pair. A8 **collapses the fields that move together at a re-establishment event — the `next_send_seq` high-water, the `attempt` window anchor, and the toward-`C` count — into the single resume record**, so the coupled transition is one `replace_atomically` with no cross-file torn state. The frame record keeps only what compose needs (the sealed frames + its own authoritative `next_send_seq`); the resume record carries the high-water *copy* used only for the `(gen,seq)` rollback comparison, updated within the same re-establishment write, never per-send. The OS `flock` (`storage/fetched.rs:174-208`) remains for cross-process mutual exclusion (GUI + TUI share one root); atomicity is the single-blob replace, not the lock.
- **`replace_atomically` is real, durable, and ~30 lines of NEW code — not an existing primitive to reuse as-is.** The in-tree `fetched.rs:946-956` is `write`→`rename`: process-crash-atomic but NOT power-loss durable (no `sync_all`, no directory fsync). The DM resume record is the durable authority for the rollback alarm and the window state, so it must use the full **tmp → `sync_all` → rename → fsync-dir** sequence. A8 corrects A7.4: cite `fetched.rs` for the *shape* and the flock, but the durability half is unbuilt and owed. (The inherited `IdxLock` symlink TOCTOU, `:189-193`, is noted as a hardening item.)
- **The re-initiation window boundary is derived from durable `last_seen`, not an unpersisted wall-clock.** A7.3 persisted the window anchor but named no durable rollover trigger; a time-based rollover with an in-memory clock tears at the boundary. A8 makes window membership and anchor-reset an **idempotent derivation from durable `last_seen`** (a survivor), recomputed at load — the single-authority discipline, no torn rollover.
- **The `(gen,seq)` rollback high-water is ratchet-resident, within-session, eviction-bounded, and scoped per `chan_id`.** It lives in the ratchet dedup memory (bounded, non-resumable), so the alarm is blind below the eviction bound and resets on the receiver's own restart — acceptable because a receiver restart tears the channel down loud (#243), degrading silent-accept to loud-teardown. Crucially it is **reset per `chan_id`**: a legitimate re-establishment restarts generation numbering under a new `chan_id`, so an un-scoped high-water would read every reconnect as a massive rollback (m-A7-1). A8 states all of this where A7.2 left it implicit.

### A8.3 — the `C` knob is a downtime-resolution dial, and the onset beacon is the irreducible feature-level cost (closes metadata CM-A7-1, CM-A7-2, CM-A7-3; crypto m-A7-2)

A7 handed the maintainer the `C` call framed as availability↔metadata-leak with absolute numbers. The A7 round showed that framing is wrong on the axis that matters:

- **The onset beacon is C-invariant and irreducible.** Any working re-establishment emits a first `RE-EST` at the reconnect instant; a co-host of both direction records sees the transition from two-way chat to fresh-ephemeral `RE-EST` groups with no answering content (`frame.rs:22-29`, ephemerals clear and countable with no key) = a timestamp of the offline→online transition, at any `C ≥ 1`. Widening the inter-attempt gap (the leak-favoring anchor) coarsens the *downtime clock* (`count × gap`, fewer bits) but does not touch the onset timestamp or the existence of the beacon. This is the F1 join run backwards over offline windows — **a fixed cost of reconnection over a co-hostable record**, removable only by deleting reconnection (`C = 0`).
- **Global `C` calibrates the partial-count leak from relative to absolute.** With `C` a known protocol constant, a co-host reading partial count `k` computes `k/C` = fraction of `T_RETIRE` elapsed = absolute days absent. Pinning `C` global is still the right call (it closes the per-client ceiling fingerprint, A7.3), but it trades that for count-calibration, not zero cost; and a release that moves `C` bins the peer's version cohort (a large-cohort residual, strictly weaker than per-client — m-A7-2, priced).
- **The leak-favoring anchor is non-monotonic.** Its coverage holes raise the chance a long-absent peer misses every overlap → give-up → fresh first contact (a doorbell event) → the next re-engagement mints *another* onset. So "fewer legible attempts per window" can mean *more* onset + first-contact events across windows, for exactly the long-absence case the feature serves.

**Therefore the decision A8 puts to the maintainer is feature-level, and `C` is a sub-tuning within it:** (1) accept that reconnection-without-fresh-contact leaks an irreducible onset beacon (and the backwards-F1 de-anonymization of the absent endpoint to a both-records co-host), or drop the feature and require fresh first contact after any teardown; and if accepted, (2) choose `C` as a downtime-*resolution* / availability / write-budget dial — a smaller `C` (wider gap) leaks a coarser downtime clock and fewer per-window attempts but risks more cross-window onsets, a larger `C` (continuous coverage) gives the crispest downtime clock and the best recovery UX. A8 states the absolute numbers on the resolution axis and defaults `C` to a middle setting that keeps recovery workable without continuous max-resolution coverage, PENDING the maintainer — but marks (1) as the decision that actually matters.

### A8.4 — the answer-side quiet-window bound is scoped to response emission, not open-attempt work (closes crypto F-A7-4)

A7.1 bounded the answering side's quiet window below the expected overlap so recovery is mechanical, which collides with A3.13's abuse throttle unless scoped. A8: the bound applies only to **post-openability response emission** — composing and emitting `RE-ACK` after an incoming `RE-EST` has opened. Only the real correspondent can produce an openable `RE-EST` (m-A6-1), so a low emission-cap only ever lets the genuine peer force `RE-ACK`s, which is the intended availability guarantee. A3.13's **unbounded** backoff is retained on **pre-authentication open-attempt work** (the decap + AEAD-open per incoming frame), so a co-host spamming unopenable `RE-EST`s cannot force open-attempt CPU at the capped-below rate. The two rates are governed separately.

### On ratification

The ISC family gains criteria for A8.1–A8.4, superseding the A7 criteria where revised. #262 closes citing A8; the store shape (#279/#281/#236) is answered by A5.4 as A8.2 gives it its real atomicity/durability contract. **The decision A8 puts to the maintainer is now two-level (A8.3):** first the feature-level accept-the-irreducible-onset-beacon-or-drop-reconnection call, then — if accepted — the `C` downtime-resolution/availability tuning, defaulted to a middle setting, PENDING. The build-obligation list (the real durable `replace_atomically`, the flock, reuse-not-skip on the re-establishment step, the `(gen,seq)` scoping) is specified in A8.1/A8.2. Until ratification, #262 stays open and this section binds nothing.

## Amendment A9 — channel re-establishment: the convergence revision after A8 (supersedes A8; **RATIFIED — the design-of-record for channel re-establishment**)

> **RATIFIED 2026-07-30.** Seven adversarial stress rounds (A3→A9) converged the design; A9's own final round returned it ratifiable on all three axes, its one-line coherence fixes were folded and then verified by a focused fold-check (which caught two stale wordings, now propagated). the maintainer's two human items are decided: **(1) reconnection is ACCEPTED** — it is the metadata-preferable option (its onset is visible only to an incumbent co-host of one opaque `ss`-derived record, strictly narrower than the fresh-contact/doorbell fallback's world-derivable-address observer set); **(2) `C` is ACCEPTED as a tunable dial** at a middle default — a downtime-resolution/availability knob, not an anonymity knob (the reconnect onset is irreducible at any `C≥1`), to be tuned in a release if field data warrants. A9 is the design-of-record; the build-obligation list in *On ratification* below is the implementer's contract.

> **A9's own final stress round (the seventh, on three independent axes) ran and returned it RATIFIABLE on all three axes — no architecture reopen, no BLOCKER — flagging only a handful of one-line coherence contradictions between the invariants A9 itself pins. Those review-prescribed one-liners are folded into the text below (A9.1: option (b) scoped to a new attempt only + the over-specification fixed; A9.2: the sealed RE-EST bytes added to the single blob and the send-side floor `(gen,seq)`-qualified; A9.3: the observer-set basis corrected and the `ss`-compromise inversion named). They were folded post-round, prescribed by the reviews that found them, and are build-verification obligations rather than re-stressed here — the amendment sequence stopped at its A9 cap.** The two items that remain are the maintainer's, not the design's: the feature-level accept-reconnection call (recommended) and the `C` downtime-resolution default.

> **The convergence revision.** The A8 stress round (three independent axes, each classifying findings as mechanism/clause-forcing vs doc-scope) converged two of three axes outright (metadata; store, with two one-line clauses) and left the crypto axis with exactly one clause-forcing invariant — and all three axes independently stated that A9 folding these is *ratifiable*. Six consecutive spine-intact rounds have fixed the architecture; A9 only pins two invariants, corrects one decision-framing sign error, and tightens doc-scope. Decision IDs `A9.1`–`A9.4`. **A9 is the amendment cap. Its stress round is the final check.**

> **The one decision-framing correction the maintainer must see (A9.3): the earlier framing had the sign inverted.** A8.3 offered "accept the irreducible onset beacon, or drop reconnection" as if dropping it removed the leak. It does not — dropping reconnection forces the returning party through the doorbell, whose onset is visible to a strictly larger, world-derivable-address observer set (the doorbell is addressed from the recipient's harvestable pubkey; the clear-*edge* leak is F7's separate invite-only path, not the doorbell's). **So reconnection is the metadata-preferable option, not a cost grudgingly accepted; the real call is the `C` downtime-resolution/UX tuning, and A9 recommends accepting reconnection.**

**Survivor set A9 does not relitigate** (the architecture is fixed across six rounds): the bounded-re-attempt spine, the symmetric confirmation-locked supersede, the store hard/soft split and its single-blob atomicity contract, `attempt` in the dedup key, eviction on actual `RS_n` retirement, the tiebreak coin, commit-then-emit, the direction-scoped sweep, RE-CONFIRM as the retirement mechanism, CSPRNG filler, the deleted pausable clock, counter-continuity crypto, per-record retry jitter, the one-knob `C` unification (reuse-not-skip restoring `MAX_GAP = C`), A8.2's real durable `replace_atomically` (tmp→`sync_all`→rename→fsync-dir) with flock as cross-process exclusion, A8.3's onset-is-irreducible reframe, A8.4's two-rate split, and all carried items. A9 touches only what the A8 round named.

### A9.1 — the re-emit invariant, pinned (closes crypto F-A8-2)

A8.1 dropped byte-identical re-seed for a fresh `attempt`-keyed re-encapsulation and justified it by "`attempt` is a distinct key namespace" — the wrong invariant, and a literal build reaches AEAD nonce-reuse forgery (a fresh `eph_ct` changes the seal AAD, `frame.rs:279,290`, under an unchanged (key,nonce) if the key is `(RS_n, attempt, leg)`) or root divergence to permanent `UnknownEphemeral` (`ratchet.rs:299-303`). A9 states the invariant precisely: **a re-emit of an already-persisted attempt is ALWAYS (a) the byte-identical persisted seal; a fresh secret is permitted only under a NEW `attempt`, where (b) it is re-derived under a fresh key (the nonce may safely repeat under a fresh key) and held provisional-until-confirmation — folded into a committed root only at RE-ACK.** The one thing forbidden is a fresh `eph_ct` under an unchanged key. Two coherence points the final stress round pinned: (i) option (b) must NOT be used to re-emit a *persisted* attempt — the peer dedups a re-emit by `attempt` (A9.4) and so would never see or confirm (b)'s re-derived secret, confirming the first secret while the recovering party holds the second → root divergence to permanent `UnknownEphemeral` (`ratchet.rs:299-303`); scoping (b) to a new attempt gives it a fresh dedup key the peer will actually confirm. (ii) A re-emit of a persisted attempt is byte-identical *by construction*, because commit-then-emit persisted the sealed frame bytes before emit and recovery re-emits those same stored bytes (A9.2), not a fresh re-encapsulation — there is no room for "fresh randomization" on a re-transmit, which is why (a) is not merely the default but the only correct re-emit of a persisted attempt. Safety comes from the encapsulated secret flowing into the key (`advance_root`, `ratchet.rs:415-421`), not from the attempt index.

### A9.2 — the single-blob field list is complete, and the two high-waters are named apart (closes store F-A8-STORE-2, F-A8-STORE-1)

A8.2 collapsed the coupled re-establishment fields into one resume blob but omitted the generation-root inputs that reuse-not-skip depends on. A9 completes the list: **the single atomic resume-record `replace_atomically` carries `S_pc`/`PK_pc` (the inputs `RS_n` re-derives from), the committed re-establishment root, the `attempt` counter, the sealed RE-EST frame bytes, the `next_send_seq` send-side floor copy, the window anchor, and the toward-`C` count — all in one write**, so commit-then-emit is provably a single atomic act and a crash cannot pair a committed root with a stale attempt. **The sealed RE-EST bytes are load-bearing in the blob** (the final stress round's most-corroborated finding, raised on both the crypto and store axes): ML-KEM encapsulation is randomized and `ss`→`eph_ct` is not invertible, so A9.1(a)'s "byte-identical persisted seal" cannot be reproduced from the key inputs alone — the seal itself must be stored, in this same blob, or byte-identical re-emit is not achievable atomically. And A9 names the two high-waters that A8.2 collided: the **send-side floor** — durable, in the resume blob, **`(gen,seq)`-qualified with a lexicographic comparison**, the generation distinguishing a re-established channel that has sent nothing yet from a replayed stale blob, which an unqualified counter cannot tell apart (amended 2026-08-14, see the build note below) — versus the **receive-side peer high-water** (ratchet-resident, within-session, eviction-bounded, reset per `chan_id`, cross-restart discharged by loud teardown). They are distinct facts with opposite durability; conflating them would make the send-side floor non-durable and break anti-rollback across re-establishment.
